C1  (Critical)  [DONE]  [LAUNCH-BLOCKER]
===============================================================================
[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: Both paid-API Edge Functions do a single identity check then dispatch to the upstream provider with no per-user metering. fetchOpenAI/index.ts:167-170 verifies the JWT via supabase.auth.getUser and immediately dispatches to callOpenAIVision/callGeminiVision/callOpenAI (label/item force detail:'high' at :120). fetchFoodDB/index.ts does the same before fatSecretRequest. There is no rate limit, quota, or 429 anywhere. Because the functions are directly callable with any valid Apple-Sign-In JWT, one free account can script the vision endpoint in a loop for unbounded spend.

Fix: Per-user/day atomic quota RPC gated before every paid call, returning 429; caps env-configurable with safe defaults. Fail-closed on RPC/DB error (documented availability tradeoff). AMENDMENTS from adjudication: (a) quota semantics must account for the indirect vision->foodDB consumer; (b) the foodDB test file must be repaired/rewritten, not merely appended to.

Files to change:
  - lib/supabase/migrations/ai_usage_quota.sql
      New migration: ai_usage table (PK user_id+day+kind, RLS on, no policies) + SECURITY DEFINER consume_ai_quota(p_user_id, p_kind, p_limit) doing atomic INSERT...ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE...RETURNING count<=limit; grant execute to authenticated & service_role. Deploy with the release (deploy-order checklist like the C2 migration).
  - lib/supabase/functions/fetchOpenAI/index.ts
      After user check (~:170), before vision (:180-190) and text (:193-203) dispatch, call supabase.rpc('consume_ai_quota',{p_user_id:user.id,p_kind,p_limit}) via the existing service-role client (:166); kind='vision'/'text'; return 429 {error:'quota_exceeded'} when not allowed. Fail-closed: treat rpc error as blocked (no paid call). NOTE: quota is charged pre-flight, so refusal (422 at :103/:151) and provider-error (502 at :98/:146) responses now also consume a unit — this is the intended fail-safe for a cost blocker; correct the blast-radius 'preserved' wording accordingly (response bodies unchanged, but a unit is spent). Caps from env with defaults.
  - lib/supabase/functions/fetchFoodDB/index.ts
      After user check, before search/details branches, call supabase.rpc('consume_ai_quota',{p_user_id:user.id,p_kind:'foodsearch',p_limit}); return 429 when not allowed. Fail-closed on rpc error. Cap sizing MUST account for the vision-enrichment path: one meal/item photo scan triggers up to MAX_BRANDED_ENRICH(5) search + 5 details calls to this endpoint via enrichBrandedItem, so a normal scan can consume up to ~10 'foodsearch' units. Either size the 'foodsearch' default cap to absorb routine scanning without starving manual search, or split enrichment onto a distinct p_kind (e.g. 'enrich') so manual-search quota is not depleted by photo scans. Document the chosen approach.
  - lib/openAI/openAI.ts
      In the !res.ok block (:31-45), map res.status===429 to a friendly 'daily scan limit reached' Error instead of the raw 'Edge function error: 429 - <text>' (also avoids leaking upstream body). Leave the 'refused' mapping intact.
  - lib/foodDB/foodDB.ts
      In callEdgeFunction !res.ok block (:37-40), map 429 to a friendly 'daily search limit reached' Error. getFoodSearchResults already rethrows (surfaced in foodDBModal manual search AND swallowed by enrichBrandedItem's try/catch during scans — both correct). getFoodDetails still swallows to null (:74-76). NOTE (finding 4): a cap hit while tapping a search result in foodDBModal yields a silent null 'no details' rather than a surfaced quota message. Accepted as a first-cut tradeoff for minimal blast radius, but explicitly documented as a known degraded add-food path; do not silently pretend it is invisible.
  - lib/openAI/__tests__/openAI.test.ts
      Currently empty — add tests mocking fetch to return 429 and assert askOpenAIVision/askOpenAIText reject with the friendly quota message, and that a 200 still returns the body unchanged (success path preserved).
  - lib/foodDB/__tests__/foodDB.test.ts
      REWRITE, not append. Verified 57/58 tests already fail: the file mocks a defunct direct-FatSecret token-fetch + foods.search client, but foodDB.ts now calls the edge function via the supabase client (which also needs SUPABASE_URL/getSession mocked). The existing 'should handle rate limit errors' test (:181-192) asserts a 429 resolves to [] — this both contradicts the brief's 429->reject behavior AND is already wrong against current code (getFoodSearchResults rethrows all edge errors today). Repair the harness to mock supabase.auth.getSession + a single edge-function fetch, then: 429 makes getFoodSearchResults reject with the friendly message; a cache hit short-circuits with no second call and no double-count; getFoodDetails 429 still resolves to null (documents the swallow). Supersede the stale [] assertion.

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    Server: only the two Deno Edge Functions call consume_ai_quota; each already has a Supabase client (fetchOpenAI=SERVICE_ROLE :166; fetchFoodDB=ANON+JWT). Client DIRECT: askOpenAIVision/askOpenAIText (lib/openAI/openAI.ts) feed cameraScreen/analyzingModal/addNutritionModal; getFoodSearchResults/getFoodDetails (lib/foodDB/foodDB.ts) feed foodDBModal. Client INDIRECT (was omitted, now added): enrichBrandedItem in context/NutritionContext/functions/aiFunctions.tsx:35 (getFoodSearchResults) and :38 (getFoodItem->getFoodDetails) runs per branded item inside runPhotoAnalysis/enrichBrandedItems, so every meal/item photo scan also hits the fetchFoodDB endpoint (1 vision unit + up to ~10 foodsearch units). enrichBrandedItem wraps these in try/catch (:43), so a 429 there is swallowed and the vision estimate is kept — scans still succeed. The 429->message change only alters error text on already-throwing paths, so existing catch/alert handling is unchanged. No PowerSync/Connector/user-table RLS surface touched — ai_usage is standalone, not in the sync schema.
  Edge cases: Concurrency: single-statement upsert with RETURNING is atomic, so the Nth request over the cap deterministically gets false (no TOCTOU). RPC/DB failure: fail-closed (recommended for a cost blocker) — accepted availability tradeoff: today the only pre-call dependency is Supabase Auth (getUser), and this adds a Postgres dependency so an ai_usage/DB outage now returns 429/500 for every scan and search; kept because fail-open would reopen unbounded spend. Pre-flight charge: refusal (422) / provider error (502) / empty result still consume a unit; accepted fail-safe, documented. Vision->foodDB coupling: one scan can spend up to ~10 foodsearch units — cap sizing or a distinct p_kind must absorb this (see fetchFoodDB entry). Label/item both counted under kind='vision' though detail:'high' is pricier — acceptable first cut.

Refusal ('refused'->422) and provider-error (502) RESPONSE BODIES are untouched — but note each now also spends one quota unit (corrected from the original brief's claim that these paths are wholly untouched). Client caching in foodDB and useSubmitOnce/30s-timeout guards untouched. RLS-no-policy on ai_usage means clients cannot read/tamper the counter; only the definer function mutates it. Passing the server-verified user.id (not a body field) prevents charging another user's quota.
  Tests:      Deno Edge Functions are outside jest-expo — validate the branch manually with `supabase functions serve` (or a Deno test) hitting the endpoint N+1 times to confirm the (N+1)th returns 429 and makes no upstream call, plus a SQL/RPC smoke check that consume_ai_quota returns true up to the limit then false and increments atomically. Automated Jest: lib/openAI/__tests__/openAI.test.ts (empty today) — 429 maps to friendly message, 200 passes body through; lib/foodDB/__tests__/foodDB.test.ts — REWRITE the broken harness (57/58 currently failing) to mock supabase getSession + edge fetch, assert 429 rejects search with the friendly message, cache hit avoids a second call/double-count, and getFoodDetails 429 resolves to null.

C2  (Critical)  [MERGED]  [LAUNCH-BLOCKER]
===============================================================================
[Consolidated multi-agent brief (not yet adjudicated)]

Root cause: Client and server schemas have drifted out of deploy order. lib/powersync/AppSchema.ts:62,93 declare `calories` as `column.real` for `nutrition_entries` and `saved_nutrition_entries`, and context/NutritionContext/database/powersyncStore.ts:54,98 (nutritionEntryToRow/savedNutritionEntryToRow) persist `sanitizeMacro(entry.calories)`, a 1-decimal value (e.g. 391.5), for every entry and saved-meal write (insert paths confirmed at powersyncStore.ts:181,222,232). Production Postgres still has `calories` typed INTEGER on both tables — the migration that widens them, lib/supabase/migrations/nutrition_calories_real.sql, is written and correct but has not been applied. lib/supabase/migrations/schema.sql:60,88 already shows the post-migration REAL state, which is aspirational, not the deployed state, masking the drift. When a decimal calorie value is upserted into the still-INTEGER column, Postgres raises a class-22 data-exception SQLSTATE (22P02, 'invalid input syntax for type integer'). Connector.ts's `isNonRetryableUploadError` (lines 15-24) treats any `^2[23]` SQLSTATE as permanent, so `uploadData` (lines 108-117) catches the error, tags it `powersync-dead-letter` in Sentry, and `continue`s past the op; `transaction.complete()` (line 121) then removes it from the local CRUD queue for good. This exact path is already unit-tested with code 22P02 at lib/powersync/__tests__/connector.test.ts:249-279 (Sentry tag confirmed at line 242). Net effect: the entry appears saved locally (it survives in SQLite and renders on-device) but is silently dropped server-side and permanently lost on reinstall or a second device. This is an active data-loss window for every build that ships the REAL client schema ahead of the migration — the dead-letter classification itself is correct behavior (matches H2's design), so this is a deploy-ordering defect, not a Connector bug.

Fix: Two parts, in this order. (1) LAUNCH-BLOCKING / operational: apply lib/supabase/migrations/nutrition_calories_real.sql to production Supabase BEFORE or atomically with the release build that ships the REAL client schema (via `psql "$SUPABASE_DB_URL" -f lib/supabase/migrations/nutrition_calories_real.sql` or the Supabase SQL editor). The file needs no edit — its two `ALTER TABLE ... ALTER COLUMN calories TYPE real USING calories::real` statements are a lossless, idempotent widening that preserves NOT NULL/DEFAULT and casts every existing integer value cleanly. Scope is calories-only on these two tables; protein/carbs/fats are already REAL and settings goals stay INTEGER (Math.round'd, never decimal) — do not touch them. Verify with `SELECT data_type FROM information_schema.columns WHERE table_name IN ('nutrition_entries','saved_nutrition_entries') AND column_name='calories'`, expecting `real` for both rows. Do NOT fix this by reverting the client to `sanitizeInt`/rounding calories — that would regress the already-merged decimal-calorie feature (Issue 3) and break the existing contract tests (powersyncStore.test.ts:27-29,58-60). No other client code path changes. (2) PREVENTIVE / recurrence guard: add a lightweight migration ledger (e.g. a `schema_migrations` bookkeeping table in Supabase plus a short checklist doc) so a client schema/write change can never again ship ahead of its hand-applied migration. This ledger work is shared scope with issue M3 (which already covers a migration-ledger fix spanning all 8 loose `migrations/*.sql` files) — do not create a second, competing ledger/checklist file for C2 alone; fold the 'apply nutrition_calories_real.sql, verify column types before release' checklist item into whatever single ledger artifact M3 produces, to avoid duplicate/conflicting docs.

Files to change:
  - lib/supabase/migrations/nutrition_calories_real.sql
      OPERATIONAL, launch-blocking: run this file against production Supabase before/with the release build. Already correct — no file edit.
  - (shared with M3) migration ledger / deploy checklist artifact
      Add one checklist line: 'nutrition_calories_real.sql applied + information_schema verified real before build promotion.' Do not create a separate C2-only checklist/README/MIGRATIONS file — reconcile into the single ledger M3 owns to avoid two competing docs.
  - lib/powersync/__tests__/connector.test.ts:249-279
      No edit. Keep green as the regression guard proving the 22P02 dead-letter path is real and correctly classified — this is why the migration is mandatory, not optional.
  - context/NutritionContext/database/__tests__/powersyncStore.test.ts:27-29,58-60
      No edit needed. Existing assertions already pin decimal-calorie persistence (391.5 survives) — this is the guard against a wrong 'round calories to int' fix. (One domain draft proposed adding a near-duplicate test; superseded by these existing assertions — no new test required.)
  - lib/powersync/AppSchema.ts:62,93 / lib/powersync/Connector.ts:15-24,108-121 / context/NutritionContext/database/powersyncStore.ts:54,98 / lib/supabase/migrations/schema.sql:60,88
      Reference only, no edits — these are the already-correct client/reference-schema pieces that establish why the migration is mandatory. Do not touch Connector's dead-letter classification (in scope of H2, not C2).

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    Every nutrition-entry and saved-meal add/edit flows through NutritionContext -> powersyncStore upsertNutritionEntry/upsertSavedNutritionEntry (rows built at powersyncStore.ts:161-235) -> Connector.uploadData -> createRecord (new, upsert branch at Connector.ts:181-188) or updateRecord (edits, Connector.ts:243-249) against nutrition_entries/saved_nutrition_entries.calories. The migration only widens those two server columns; no client caller, mapper, or query changes. PowerSync sync rules are SELECT * so no dashboard/bucket change is required. Reads (rowToNutritionEntry, rowToSavedNutritionEntry) already tolerate fractional numbers.
  Edge cases: (1) Recovery gap: ops already dead-lettered before the migration runs are NOT auto-recovered — transaction.complete() already dropped them from the CRUD queue; they exist only in that device's local SQLite and re-upload only if the entry is edited/re-saved (a fresh PATCH/PUT), or lost entirely on reinstall/new device. Pre-launch this population should be ~zero (or limited to TestFlight testers); check Sentry 'powersync-dead-letter' events pre-release and manually re-touch any affected entries if found. (2) ALTER COLUMN TYPE takes a brief ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock — negligible pre-launch, but prefer a low-traffic window. (3) Migration is idempotent (real->real re-run is a no-op) and safe to re-run. (4) Existing integer rows cast losslessly to real, no data loss on the widening itself. (5) Ordering hazard: if the REAL-schema client build reaches real users before the migration runs, the loss window reopens — this is precisely what launch-gating part (1) prevents.
  Preserved:  Zero client code changes, so no runtime behavior can regress. The Connector's class-22/23 dead-letter classification (Connector.ts:15-24) stays intact as the correct safety net for genuinely malformed data — this fix does not touch it, and making class-22 retryable would only wedge the upload queue worse (H2's concern, out of scope here). The decimal-calorie feature (Issue 3) is preserved end-to-end once the migration lands; historical integer rows remain valid under REAL.
  Tests:      Pre-existing regression guards to keep green, no new tests needed: lib/powersync/__tests__/connector.test.ts:249-279 (22P02 dead-letter classification + Sentry tag) and context/NutritionContext/database/__tests__/powersyncStore.test.ts:27-29,58-60 (decimal calories, e.g. 391.5, are preserved not rounded). Post-deploy verification (the real proof of this fix): run `SELECT data_type FROM information_schema.columns WHERE table_name IN ('nutrition_entries','saved_nutrition_entries') AND column_name='calories'` expecting `real` for both; then in staging/production log a fractional-serving meal (non-integer calories), confirm it upserts successfully with no `powersync-dead-letter` Sentry event, and confirm it survives a reinstall or second-device sync. Also fine to run `npx jest lib/powersync/__tests__/connector.test.ts context/NutritionContext/database/__tests__/powersyncStore.test.ts` as a sanity pass before release.
  DevHub:     approval=na; plan={"applicable":false,"naReason":"Pure server-side DDL/deploy-ordering fix with no client UI or code path change; nothing to preview in Dev Hub."}

===============================================================================
C3  (Critical)  [DONE]  [LAUNCH-BLOCKER]
===============================================================================
[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: The settings persist effect (context/SettingsContext/index.tsx:148-191) uses persistSavingRef as a save mutex but releases it CONDITIONALLY (line 181: `if (!cancelled) persistSavingRef.current = false`). Under a mutate-during-save interleaving the mutex wedges for the rest of the session: (1) a mutation sets persistDirty, the effect fires, sets persistSavingRef=true (153) and awaits upsertSettings (156); (2) a second mutation mid-save routes through markSettingsPersistDirty which, seeing persistSavingRef===true, sets persistDirtyDuringSaveRef=true (39) and its setSettingsState changes `settings` (a dep at line 191); (3) React tears down the running effect (cleanup sets cancelled=true) and re-runs it, but the re-run bails at `if (persistSavingRef.current) return` (150) because the mutex is still held, so it registers no cleanup and starts no save; (4) the original upsertSettings resolves, hits `if (cancelled) return` (157), jumps to finally where the conditional release is SKIPPED because cancelled===true. persistSavingRef is now stuck true and persistDirtyDuringSaveRef stuck true. From then on every markSettingsPersistDirty routes to persistDirtyDuringSaveRef and every effect run bails at 150 — no settings write ever fires again; macro targets/goal/units/onboarding edits live only in React state and vanish on app kill, silently. The in-try re-arm at 159-163 cannot save this case (the re-run already bailed at the mutex).

Fix: Make the mutex release UNCONDITIONAL and re-arm pending work through a channel that re-triggers the effect, WITHOUT pre-empting an already-scheduled exponential backoff.

1. Declare a local `let scheduledBackoff = false` alongside `let cancelled = false`. Set `scheduledBackoff = true` at the point the backoff timer is scheduled (line 179), i.e. `persistRetryTimerRef.current = setTimeout(...); scheduledBackoff = true`.

2. Replace the finally (lines 180-182) with an unconditional release plus a GATED re-arm:
```
} finally {
    persistSavingRef.current = false
    // Re-fire the loop for a mutation that landed mid-save. Skip the bump when a
    // backoff retry is already pending (non-cancelled failure path): bumping the
    // nonce would re-run the effect, whose cleanup clears the just-scheduled
    // persistRetryTimerRef and forces an immediate retry, defeating the 1s/2s/4s
    // backoff. Only re-arm where NO backoff is pending: success-with-dirty, or a
    // cancelled run whose re-run bailed at the mutex.
    if (persistDirtyDuringSaveRef.current && !scheduledBackoff) {
        persistDirtyDuringSaveRef.current = false
        setPersistDirty(true)
        setPersistRetryNonce((n) => n + 1)
    }
}
```

3. Remove the now-redundant in-try re-arm block (lines 159-163), leaving setPersistDirty(false) (line 165) as the sole success action; the finally re-arm handles the dirty-during-save case uniformly.

Why this closes the wedge: at step 4 the finally now runs `persistSavingRef.current = false` regardless of cancelled, releasing the mutex; persistDirtyDuringSaveRef is true and (on the cancelled/success paths) no backoff is pending, so it clears the ref, sets persistDirty(true) and bumps the nonce, forcing a fresh effect run against the latest `settings` closure. Loop resumes.

Why backoff is now genuinely preserved: on a non-cancelled FAILING save the catch schedules the backoff timer and sets scheduledBackoff=true; the finally releases the mutex but skips the nonce bump, so the pending backoff timer is the sole re-trigger and fires after persistBackoffMs. persistDirtyDuringSaveRef is left set and is consumed by the success-with-dirty branch on the eventual successful retry. On a CANCELLED failure (catch returns at 167 before line 179) scheduledBackoff stays false, so the finally re-arm fires — correct, since no backoff was scheduled and the re-run had bailed at the mutex.

Why unconditional release is safe (no double-save): an effect instance can only set persistSavingRef=true (153) after passing the guard (150), which requires the ref false; the ref only becomes false in a prior instance's finally, i.e. after its await settled. Two upsertSettings calls can never overlap; the old `if (!cancelled)` guard protected against a hand-off the line-150 guard already makes impossible.

Files to change:
  - c:\Users\aadik\Downloads\App\context\SettingsContext\index.tsx
      Persist effect (lines 148-191): (a) delete the in-try re-arm block at 159-163, leaving setPersistDirty(false) at 165 as the only success action; (b) add a local `let scheduledBackoff = false` next to `let cancelled = false`, and set it true where the backoff timer is scheduled at line 179; (c) replace the finally at 180-182 with an unconditional `persistSavingRef.current = false` plus a re-arm gated on `persistDirtyDuringSaveRef.current && !scheduledBackoff` that clears the ref, setPersistDirty(true) and bumps persistRetryNonce. No signature/API change.
  - c:\Users\aadik\Downloads\App\context\SettingsContext\__tests__\index.persist.test.tsx
      NEW file. renderHook(useSettings) inside SettingsProvider with upsertSettings mocked to a deferred/controllable promise and a userID present. Cover: (1) mutate-during-successful-save resumes the loop and persists the latest value plus a subsequent third change (wedge regression); (2) mutate-during-FAILING-save: assert the next retry fires only AFTER persistBackoffMs (fake timers), i.e. the backoff is NOT skipped by the finally re-arm.

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    All affected code is inside context/SettingsContext/index.tsx. Dirty-state producers (setSettings line 45, handleUpdateBw 89-110, switchToMaintenance, acknowledgeGoalOvershoot) funnel through markSettingsPersistDirty (37) and the single persist effect. No context interface member, return value, or exported signature changes. completeOnboarding (197) writes directly via upsertSettings and is untouched; reloadFromDisk (62) and the useAsyncLoad loader (127) are untouched.
  Edge cases: 1) Mutate during a CANCELLED failing save (settings ref changed, catch returned at 167 before line 179): scheduledBackoff false, finally releases mutex and re-arms -> fresh retry against latest settings. 2) Mutate during a NON-CANCELLED failing save (Object.is-equal setSettings value keeps cancelled=false, backoff timer scheduled at 179): scheduledBackoff true, finally releases mutex but SKIPS the nonce bump, so the backoff timer is the sole re-trigger and the 1s/2s/4s delay is honored; persistDirtyDuringSaveRef stays set and is consumed by the success-with-dirty branch on the eventual successful retry. 3) Onboarding silent-retry path preserved: persistDirty stays true, catch still schedules the backoff, finally re-arm no longer competes with it. 4) Clean save, no mid-save mutation: persistDirtyDuringSaveRef false, no bump, loop terminates exactly as before. 5) Unmount during in-flight save with a raced mutation: finally may call setPersistDirty/setPersistRetryNonce after unmount — benign no-op under React 18.
  Preserved:  Exponential backoff (persistBackoffMs) — now genuinely preserved on BOTH cancelled and non-cancelled failure paths via the scheduledBackoff gate; alert-after-3-misses, reportPersistFailure severity/onboarding flags, silent reloadFromDisk rollback, the completeOnboarding direct-write path, and the no-concurrent-upsert invariant. Logic lives in exactly one place — no duplicated copies to keep in sync.
  Tests:      ADD context/SettingsContext/__tests__/index.persist.test.tsx with two cases: (A) wedge regression — start a save (deferred pending), fire a SECOND change while pending, resolve the deferred, flush microtasks, assert upsertSettings is called again with the SECOND value AND a third change also persists (FAILS before the fix, PASSES after); (B) backoff-preservation — make the in-flight save reject while a mid-save mutation set persistDirtyDuringSaveRef without changing the settings ref, then assert with fake timers that the next upsertSettings does NOT fire until persistBackoffMs elapses (guards against the reviewer-found immediate-retry regression). Also RUN existing context/SettingsContext/database/__tests__/powersyncStore.test.ts to confirm the upsertSettings contract is unchanged.

Adjudication decisions:
  - Finding 1: the proposed finally's unconditional setPersistRetryNonce bump also fires on the non-cancelled FAILURE path; the catch schedules the backoff timer (line 179), then the finally re-runs the effect whose cleanup (186-189) clears that timer, forcing an immediate retry and skipping the 1s/2s/4s backoff — contradicting blastRadius.preserved and edge cases #1/#2.
      -> MATERIAL — ACCEPT and AMEND. Verified against actual code: on a non-cancelled failing save the catch schedules persistRetryTimerRef (179) and the proposed finally would bump the nonce (persistDirty still true, mutex released), re-running the effect whose cleanup clears the pending backoff timer -> immediate retry. This re-introduces the hot-loop the line-178 backoff defends against and falsifies the brief's 'backoff preserved' claim. Amendment: add a local scheduledBackoff flag set at line 179 and gate the finally re-arm on `!scheduledBackoff`, so the bump fires only on success-with-dirty and cancelled paths (no backoff pending). The wedge fix is unaffected.  (Rule 2 (Safety / behavior preserved))
  - Finding 2: the non-cancelled-failure-with-dirty case is reachable via handleUpdateBw, because setSettingsState(prev => result ? result.newSettings : prev) returns prev unchanged when computeBwUpdate yields null (95-98) while markSettingsPersistDirty is still called at line 99 — producing dirty-during-save with cancelled===false.
      -> IMMATERIAL — DISMISS mechanism as factually incorrect. Verified: handleUpdateBw guards `if (updatedWeight <= 0) return` at line 90, and computeBwUpdate (bodyWeightFunctions.tsx:52) returns null ONLY when updatedWeight <= 0. Therefore by line 95 result is always truthy, setSettingsState always yields a NEW object (via withRegeneratedTargets/spread), settings ref changes, the effect is torn down, cancelled becomes true, and the catch returns at 167 BEFORE scheduling the backoff at 179 — so this path cannot reach the backoff-bypass. The stated reachability mechanism is wrong; the underlying backoff concern is already captured and fixed via Finding 1.  (Rule 1 (Correctness — claim verified against actual code and rejected))
  - Finding 3: the finally re-arm is unconditional across all exit paths including catch/backoff; an immediate nonce bump competes with the persistBackoffMs timer. Correctly narrows the trigger: it requires a mid-save mutation that does NOT change the settings reference (otherwise cancelled=true short-circuits at 167), which for handleUpdateBw never happens (line 90 guard => computeBwUpdate never returns null), so the case only arises for an Object.is-equal setSettings value.
      -> MATERIAL — ACCEPT (same defect as Finding 1, with correct reachability analysis). The narrow-but-real trigger (an Object.is-equal setSettings value during a failing save) is exactly the residual risk; the scheduledBackoff-gated amendment closes it and makes the brief's 'backoff preserved' claim true. Narrow reachability does not exempt it because the affected path is the failing-DB path the backoff is specifically designed to protect.  (Rule 2 (Safety / behavior preserved))

H1  (High)  [DONE]  [LAUNCH-BLOCKER]

[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: Premium entitlement is checked only in the React Native client (hasPremium from BillingContext, surfaced as local if (!hasPremium) guards in cameraScreen.tsx:35, addNutritionModal.tsx:62, foodDBModal.tsx:30/93). Both Edge Functions verify the caller's Supabase JWT (getUser) but never verify subscription status, then call the paid upstream provider (OpenAI/Gemini in fetchOpenAI/index.ts:180-203; FatSecret in fetchFoodDB/index.ts:62-96). Because Edge Functions are directly callable over HTTP by any valid Supabase JWT — every onboarded free account has one — a free user (or a scripted curl) gets AI scan, AI macros, and food search for free. This is also the enabler that makes C1 (no server quota) free to exploit.

Fix: Server-side premium entitlement gate in both Edge Functions via a shared RevenueCat REST check; fail-closed with timeout; client guards retained as UX-only. Approach unchanged from the authored brief; blast-radius description amended for the fetchFoodDB fan-out and the getFoodDetails swallow-to-null behavior.

Files to change:
  - lib/supabase/functions/_shared/entitlement.ts
      NEW. Export hasPremiumEntitlement(userId): Promise<boolean> and ENTITLEMENT_ID = 'LiftTrition Pro'. Reads REVENUECAT_SECRET_API_KEY; GETs /v1/subscribers/{userId} with Authorization: Bearer <secret> and an AbortController (~3-5s) timeout; returns true only if the 'LiftTrition Pro' entitlement is present and unexpired; on any error/timeout/missing-key returns false (fail closed) and console.error's for telemetry (telemetry matters — see fan-out note in blast radius, rate-limit-driven false negatives must be observable).
  - lib/supabase/functions/fetchOpenAI/index.ts
      After the getUser/401 block (line 170), add: if (!(await hasPremiumEntitlement(user.id))) return new Response(JSON.stringify({ error: 'premium_required' }), { status: 403 }). Import the shared helper at top. Covers both vision (180-190) and text (193-203) branches. This is the single-lookup path (1 RC call per op) and the one whose fail-closed 403 aborts the whole scan for a premium user — lowest rate-limit exposure, highest user impact if it 403s spuriously.
  - lib/supabase/functions/fetchFoodDB/index.ts
      After the getUser/401 block (line 57), add the same 403 premium gate before the search (62) and details (74) branches. Import the shared helper. NOTE: this is the high-fan-out path — a single photo scan drives up to ~10 fetchFoodDB calls (5 branded items x search+details), so up to ~10 RC REST lookups per scan hit this gate; monitor RC REST rate-limit responses here (they fail closed to 403 and are silently absorbed downstream — see blast radius).
  - Supabase Edge Function secrets (deploy/config, not a repo file)
      Add REVENUECAT_SECRET_API_KEY (RevenueCat v1 REST secret key) via supabase secrets set. Document alongside OPENAI_API_KEY/FATSECRET_* which are already required. If absent, functions fail closed (all callers get 403), so set it before the release build.

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    Client callers are lib/openAI/openAI.ts (askOpenAIVision/askOpenAIText -> callEdgeFunction, sends Authorization: Bearer session.access_token) and lib/foodDB/foodDB.ts (getFoodSearchResults/getFoodDetails). CORRECTION to the authored brief: the two foodDB consumers do NOT both rethrow. getFoodSearchResults (foodDB.ts:56-58) rethrows non-2xx, so a 403 on the search path surfaces as an error the existing catch handles. getFoodDetails (foodDB.ts:74-76) wraps callEdgeFunction in try/catch that returns null on ANY error, so a 403 on the details path is silently swallowed to null (details lookup returns null -> getFoodItem returns null -> enrichBrandedItem keeps the vision estimate). No new client branch is required (client pre-gates on hasPremium; premium users pass the server check), but the failure mode differs by path. The only privileged path through the changed code is the two Edge Functions themselves; nothing else imports them.
  Edge cases: Fan-out amplification (amended in): a single premium photo scan is NOT 'one bounded call' — runPhotoAnalysis fires 1 fetchOpenAI vision call, then enrichBrandedItems runs Promise.all over up to MAX_BRANDED_ENRICH=5 branded items, each calling getFoodSearchResults + getFoodItem->getFoodDetails = 2 fetchFoodDB calls, so up to ~11 gated edge invocations and thus up to ~11 RC REST entitlement lookups per scan. Consequences: (1) RC REST rate-limit exposure under load; (2) when a fetchFoodDB gate fails closed (RC outage/rate-limit) for a legitimate premium user, the 403 is absorbed by getFoodDetails->null and by enrichBrandedItem's catch, so branded enrichment silently degrades to the vision estimate rather than surfacing an error — the user still gets a result (macros are the vision estimate, less accurate), so this is bounded degradation, not a hard failure. (3) A fail-closed 403 on the single fetchOpenAI vision call DOES fail the whole scan for a premium user, but that path is a single RC lookup with low rate-limit risk. Premium user with in-flight RevenueCat purchase: RC REST is source of truth and reflects the completed purchase immediately, even before the client's customerInfo listener updates. App Review sandbox purchases: RC subscriber entitlements include sandbox entitlements as active, so reviewers using sandbox pass — verify during review smoke test. RC not configured (public key == 'NULL'): no user has premium client-side and the server consistently 403s — no divergence. RC REST outage/timeout: fail-closed; premium users temporarily lose AI scan (vision path errors) and get vision-estimate-only macros on branded enrichment (search path errors surface; details path degrades silently) — mitigated by AbortController + console.error telemetry. If RC REST rate-limiting or availability proves material in production, the webhook-synced Supabase subscriptions table (read in-edge; both functions already construct a Supabase client at fetchOpenAI:166 and fetchFoodDB:51) is the follow-up that removes the per-call round-trip and rate-limit exposure — deferred, not adopted now, because it adds a webhook endpoint + table + RLS and introduces its own webhook sync-lag correctness risk (a just-purchased premium user 403'd until the webhook lands) that the RC-REST-as-source-of-truth approach avoids. Anonymous RC id: not reachable — logIn(user.id) aliases before any paid call. Direct-HTTP attacker with a valid free-account JWT: now 403 before any provider call — the core fix.
  Preserved:  Premium users resolve to active entitlement and get 200 on all paths except during an RC outage/rate-limit, where AI scan errors and branded enrichment degrades to vision estimates (bounded, still returns a result). Free users are already blocked in-app; they are now also blocked server-side. No schema, RLS, or data migration; no client edit; the existing 401 auth check, request parsing, provider-selection, and response shaping are untouched. Adds one RC REST call before each paid op — but note that per photo scan this is up to ~11 such calls, not one.
  Tests:      No server-side test harness exists today. Add a Deno unit test lib/supabase/functions/_shared/entitlement.test.ts that stubs global fetch and asserts: active-unexpired => true; expired/missing-entitlement => false; non-200 from RC => false; network-error/abort => false (fail-closed). Integration smoke (add to release checklist): curl fetchOpenAI + fetchFoodDB with (a) a free-account JWT => expect 403 premium_required and confirm no upstream/OpenAI/FatSecret usage logged, (b) a premium/sandbox JWT => expect 200. Re-run the existing client suites lib/openAI/__tests__ and lib/foodDB/__tests__ to confirm the added 403 path doesn't regress error handling — and explicitly assert the divergent client behavior: getFoodSearchResults rethrows a 403 while getFoodDetails swallows it to null (so a premium-user RC outage yields vision-estimate macros, not a thrown error).

H2  (High)  [MERGED]  [LAUNCH-BLOCKER]
===============================================================================
[Consolidated multi-agent brief (not yet adjudicated)]

Root cause: `lib/powersync/Connector.ts` has two coupled defects, both rooted in the fact that per-table conflict handling is hand-copied instead of driven by a shared config, so the two bugs are really one structural gap plus its consequences.

(a) THE WEDGE (infra-reliability concern). `isNonRetryableUploadError` (lines 15-24, verified) dead-letters ONLY SQLSTATE classes 22/23 via `/^2[23]/`. Every other permanent rejection — PostgREST schema-cache misses `PGRST204`/`PGRST205` (the exact scenario `lib/supabase/migrations/ingredient_brand.sql:3-5` warns will "wedge the upload queue" if a client ships a column ahead of its migration) and SQLSTATE class 42 access-rule errors (`42501` RLS denial, `42P01` undefined_table, `42703` undefined_column) — falls through to `throw error` at line 116. PowerSync then retries the identical crud transaction forever: `getNextCrudTransaction()` (line 81) keeps returning the same head transaction, `transaction.complete()` (line 121) is never reached, and every subsequent write (any table) queues up behind it. `flushUploadsOrThrow` (`FlushUploads.ts:44-73`, verified) then times out at sign-out, and the only user escape is a force sign-out that clears the entire un-flushed local queue — total data loss for every pending write, not just the poison row.

(b) THE SILENT DROP (logic-correctness concern). `user_exercises` has a server `UNIQUE(user_id, name)` (`lib/supabase/migrations/schema.sql:36`, verified) but is absent from `SELF_HEALING_CONFLICT_TABLES` (line 10) and has no check-then-update branch in `createRecord` (lines 124-189, verified against actual code). It falls through to the generic `.upsert(record)` (lines 183-187), which only resolves conflicts on primary key `id`. A duplicate-named custom exercise created on a second device (different PowerSync-minted uuid) raises `23505` on `(user_id, name)`; class 23 is already dead-lettered today, so the exercise is silently, permanently dropped — violating CLAUDE.md Gotcha #1 ("New schema tables must handle their own conflict resolution in the Connector").

(c) THE STRUCTURAL ROOT (code-structure concern). The check-then-update-else-insert logic is hand-duplicated for `settings` (129-153) and `weight_progress` (154-180) with `any`-typed records and a separately hand-maintained `SELF_HEALING_CONFLICT_TABLES` literal (line 10) that has no enforced relationship to those branches. That's exactly how `user_exercises` — a third table with the identical natural-key-conflict shape — got forgotten in three places at once. Patching (a) and (b) as isolated edits would just create a third (soon a recurring) hand-copied branch and re-open the same gap for the next natural-key table.

One dissenting draft argued to leave the classifier untouched and only add wedge-detection telemetry (new `SyncWatchdog`/`uploadWedge.ts` observability). Rejected under the correctness gate (see Conflicts resolved) — it does not stop the forced-sign-out total-queue-loss mechanism, it only reports the wedge sooner.

Fix: ONE reworked change in `lib/powersync/Connector.ts` (verified against current code), not three separate edits — the map-driven natural-key branch satisfies infra, logic, and structure simultaneously:

1. **Replace the hand-duplicated conflict branches with one config-driven natural-key map.** Add `const CONFLICT_KEYS: Record<string, string[]> = { settings: ['user_id'], weight_progress: ['user_id', 'date'], user_exercises: ['user_id', 'name'] };` and derive `const SELF_HEALING_CONFLICT_TABLES = new Set(Object.keys(CONFLICT_KEYS));` (replaces the line-10 literal). Rewrite `createRecord` (124-189) to: if `CONFLICT_KEYS[table]` exists and every listed key is present/non-null on `record`, do the existing check-then-update-else-insert pattern generically (select `id` filtered by `.eq()` on each key column via `maybeSingle`/`single` as today; if found, strip `id` and `.update()` filtered by the same key columns; else `.insert(record)`); otherwise fall through to the unchanged generic `.upsert(record)`. This must reproduce the settings/weight_progress calls byte-for-byte (same select→update-stripping-id/insert shape) and automatically gives `user_exercises` the identical treatment — no new branch to hand-copy.

2. **Broaden `isNonRetryableUploadError` (15-24) so the queue can never wedge on class-42/schema-drift errors.** Replace the single-class test with: SQLSTATE regex `/^(22|23|42)/` (covers existing 22/23 plus 42501 RLS, 42P01 undefined_table, 42703 undefined_column) OR a permanent-PostgREST-code set `{'PGRST204', 'PGRST205'}` (schema-cache-miss codes reachable through the `.select/.insert/.update/.upsert` calls in this file — the exact `ingredient_brand.sql` scenario). Keep the non-string-`code` guard (network/fetch errors stay retryable) and the `23505 + SELF_HEALING_CONFLICT_TABLES` carve-out unchanged in effect — it now automatically covers `user_exercises` because step 1 derives the set from `CONFLICT_KEYS`. Do NOT dead-letter `PGRST301` (JWT expired), or SQLSTATE classes 08/40/53/57/58 — these must keep re-throwing so they retry.

Both changes are required together and in the same commit: adding `user_exercises` to the self-healing set without the new conflict branch would make a `(user_id,name)` collision retry forever against the generic PK-only upsert (a new wedge); adding the branch without the set membership would still dead-letter the loser of a genuine simultaneous-create race instead of letting it converge to UPDATE on retry.

Explicitly out of scope for this fix (redirect, don't bundle): wedge-detection telemetry (SyncWatchdog / new `uploadWedge.ts`) is a good complementary idea from one draft, but belongs to H4 (observability) since the existing Sentry dead-letter capture (lines 110-113, unchanged) already makes the now-broadened set of failures visible — telemetry doesn't substitute for fixing the classification, and adding new instrumentation files here would violate least-code for a fix that the map-driven change already resolves correctly.

Files to change:
  - lib/powersync/Connector.ts:10
      Replace the SELF_HEALING_CONFLICT_TABLES literal with a CONFLICT_KEYS map ({ settings: ['user_id'], weight_progress: ['user_id','date'], user_exercises: ['user_id','name'] }) and derive SELF_HEALING_CONFLICT_TABLES = new Set(Object.keys(CONFLICT_KEYS)).
  - lib/powersync/Connector.ts:15-24
      Broaden isNonRetryableUploadError: SQLSTATE regex /^(22|23|42)/ OR PostgREST permanent-code set {'PGRST204','PGRST205'}; keep the non-string-code retry guard and the 23505+self-healing carve-out (now covers user_exercises automatically via the derived set).
  - lib/powersync/Connector.ts:124-189
      Collapse the settings (129-153) and weight_progress (154-180) branches plus the new user_exercises need into one generic CONFLICT_KEYS-driven check-then-update-else-insert path (same select/update-stripping-id/insert shape, filtered by all listed key columns); keep the generic .upsert(record) fallback (181-188) for every other table unchanged.
  - lib/powersync/__tests__/connector.test.ts
      Add: (1) 42501 RLS and PGRST204/PGRST205 schema-drift errors now dead-letter (Sentry.captureException + transaction.complete, no throw); (2) transient codes stay retryable — re-confirm the existing PGRST301 test (~line 346) and add a non-22/23/42 transient case (e.g. 40001) still rethrows without completing; (3) user_exercises 23505 now rethrows/self-heals instead of dead-lettering, mirroring the existing settings (~370) and weight_progress (~394) 23505 self-heal tests; (4) createRecord for a user_exercises PUT takes the UPDATE-by-(user_id,name) branch (id stripped) when a row exists, and INSERT when it does not; (5) re-run/confirm the existing settings and weight_progress createRecord tests still pass unchanged post-refactor (behavior-preservation guard for the map-driven consolidation).

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    Connector is instantiated only in lib/powersync/orchestrator.ts and handed to powerSync.connect(); uploadData runs only inside PowerSync's background upload loop. createRecord/updateRecord/deleteRecord/isNonRetryableUploadError are private with no callers outside uploadData (grep-verified). Every write from every context (Settings, Workout incl. user_exercises via context/WorkoutContext/database/powersyncStore.ts, Nutrition, weight_progress) flows through uploadData, so the classifier change affects all 10 AppSchema tables' error handling — but only which errors retry vs. dead-letter, never the success path. The map-driven createRecord touches only settings/weight_progress/user_exercises; the other 7 tables keep the untouched generic upsert. FlushUploads.ts is an indirect, favorable beneficiary: a drained queue means flushUploadsOrThrow stops timing out into the forced-sign-out data-loss path.
  Edge cases: Transient-vs-permanent boundary is the main risk and must be test-pinned: PGRST301 (JWT expired), SQLSTATE classes 08 (connection)/40 (serialization/deadlock)/53/57/58 (resource/operator), and codeless network errors must all keep re-throwing/retrying — the widened set deliberately adds only class 42 and PGRST204/205, nothing else. Cross-device duplicate-name user_exercises create: loser's 23505 is now retryable, retry's SELECT finds the winner's row, converges to UPDATE-by-(user_id,name) — same last-write-wins/id-divergence contract already accepted for settings/weight_progress (server row's id wins; the loser device's local uuid is discarded, not lost). RESIDUAL TRADE-OFF (documented, not eliminated): dead-lettering a genuine schema-drift PGRST204/RLS 42501 op still permanently drops that one row server-side even though the underlying condition (missing migration / misconfigured policy) is itself operator-recoverable — this converts an unbounded, fleet-wide silent wedge into a bounded, Sentry-visible, single-op loss, which is the audit's intended trade-off (a forced-sign-out today loses the ENTIRE local queue, not just one row). The true preventive guard for the schema-drift case remains deploy-order discipline (migrations before the client version that writes the column, per ingredient_brand.sql and issue C2) — this fix is the safety net, not a replacement for that discipline. Records missing a listed conflict key (e.g. a malformed settings PUT without user_id) fall through to the generic PK upsert exactly as today.
  Preserved:  Settings and weight_progress conflict resolution is byte-for-byte equivalent post-refactor (same select→update-stripping-id/insert shape, same .eq() natural-key matching) — only the code path is now shared instead of copied. The generic upsert fallback for the other 7 tables, updateRecord (191-250) and deleteRecord (252-259), and the Sentry dead-letter capture shape (tags.area='powersync-dead-letter', extra {table,opType,opId}, lines 110-113) are all unchanged. The sequential per-op processing with skip-and-continue on dead-letter (108-117) and transaction.complete() semantics (121) are untouched. prepareRecordForSupabase's accessory_muscles conversion (65-74) still runs before createRecord, so the new branch inherits it exactly as the other user_exercises code paths do today.
  Tests:      npx jest lib/powersync/__tests__/connector.test.ts (extend per Files above — all new cases fail before, pass after; existing cases at ~217, ~346, ~370, ~394 must stay green as regression guards). Also run lib/powersync/__tests__/flushUploads.test.ts (confirm no regression in the sign-out gate) and persistErrors.test.ts if present. Full gate before considering H2 closed: npm run test:ci.
  DevHub:     approval=na; plan={"applicable":false,"naReason":"Backend/sync-layer fix in lib/powersync/Connector.ts — no UI surface, nothing DevHub-previewable.","components":"","scenarios":[],"expected":"","testPage":""}

H3  (High)  [MERGED]  [LAUNCH-BLOCKER]
===============================================================================
[Consolidated multi-agent brief (not yet adjudicated)]

Root cause: `components/GuardComponents/PowerSyncGuard.tsx:20` awaits bare `powerSync.waitForFirstSync()` inside its `useAsyncLoad` loader with no timeout. Per the SDK's own contract (`node_modules/@powersync/common/lib/client/AbstractPowerSyncDatabase.d.ts:194-206`), this promise "will resolve once the first full sync has completed" — it never rejects on a network failure or unreachable sync endpoint, and even passing it an `AbortSignal` makes it RESOLVE early (not reject) once aborted. `useAsyncLoad` (`lib/hooks/useAsyncLoad.ts:22-41`) only flips `status` to `'error'` inside `.catch`, and `AppLoadingScreen`'s only escape hatch — the retry link (`AppLoadingScreen.tsx:93-99`) — is gated on `loadFailed` (`status === 'error'`, wired at `PowerSyncGuard.tsx:27`). So on a fresh install / first sign-in with PowerSync unreachable, the promise hangs forever, status never leaves `'loading'`, and the user is stuck on "Syncing data..." with no timeout, no retry, and no sign-out escape (there is currently no escape affordance on the loading screen at all — confirmed by reading `AppLoadingScreen.tsx` Props at lines 6-10, which only has `message`/`loadFailed`/`onRetry`). Compounding factor: `context/AuthContext/index.tsx:39-52` makes exactly one `ensurePowerSyncConnected('auth_session')` attempt on mount and swallows a failure with `console.warn` — never retried — so nothing else in the app re-attempts the connection once it fails. Returning users are unaffected because their persisted first-sync flag makes `waitForFirstSync()` resolve immediately.

Fix: One reconciled change, three parts, in this order:

1. **New helper `lib/powersync/waitForFirstSync.ts`** — export `FirstSyncTimeoutError` and `waitForFirstSyncOrThrow(timeoutMs = FIRST_SYNC_TIMEOUT_MS /* 30_000 */)`. Implementation must use the SDK's *documented* abort contract rather than an external race, because a bare `Promise.race` against a rejecting timer (as several drafts first proposed) would abandon the SDK's internal wait/listener on every retry instead of tearing it down — a real resource-accumulation risk if a user taps retry repeatedly while offline:
   ```
   const controller = new AbortController()
   const timer = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), timeoutMs)
   try {
     await powerSync.waitForFirstSync(controller.signal)   // resolves early on abort per SDK contract, does not throw
     if (!powerSync.currentStatus?.hasSynced) throw new FirstSyncTimeoutError(...)
   } finally {
     clearTimeout(timer)
   }
   ```
   This is the mechanism that actually rejects the loader (surfacing `'error'`) while still cleanly aborting the SDK's own wait — strictly more correct than a same-shaped external race.

2. **`components/GuardComponents/PowerSyncGuard.tsx`** — in the loader (lines 17-21): keep `if (!session) return` and the existing `__DEV__ throwIfLoadFailureArmed('powersync')` call, then add `await ensurePowerSyncConnected('auth_session')` (imported from `lib/powersync/orchestrator`; it is mutex-serialized and idempotent — no-ops if already connected — so calling it here alongside AuthContext's own call cannot double-connect) before replacing the bare `await powerSync.waitForFirstSync()` with `await waitForFirstSyncOrThrow(FIRST_SYNC_TIMEOUT_MS)`. This closes the "nothing else can un-wedge the screen" gap: retry now actually re-attempts the connection, not just re-waits on a connection nobody is retrying. Then add a `handleEscape` callback — `Alert.alert('Sign out?', ..., [Cancel, { text: 'Sign out', onPress: () => forceSignOut() }])` — and pass `onSignOut={handleEscape}` to `AppLoadingScreen`. Import `forceSignOut` from `@/context/AuthContext/functions/accountFunctions` (the exact function/import path `app/settingsScreens/profile.tsx:4` already uses). Use `forceSignOut`, NOT the context's `signOut()` — `signOut()` calls `flushUploadsOrThrow` (`FlushUploads.ts`) against the very PowerSync connection that is unreachable, so it would hang too; `forceSignOut` skips the flush, which is safe here because a first-sync-blocked device has no local data yet, so there is nothing unsynced to lose.

3. **`components/GuardComponents/AppLoadingScreen.tsx`** — add optional `onSignOut?: () => void` to `Props` (lines 6-10); when `loadFailed && onSignOut`, render a second `TouchableOpacity` ("Sign out") beneath the existing retry link (lines 93-99), reusing `styles.message` tinted `colors.textSecondary` so it reads as secondary against the retry link's `colors.workout` (primary). Add `accessibilityRole="button"` + `accessibilityLabel` to both the retry and sign-out links, and give both a real ≥44pt touch target (today's retry link is a 12.5pt line with only `hitSlop={10}` ≈ 32.5pt total — short of Apple's minimum; bump both via larger `hitSlop` or `minHeight`/padding). The prop is optional and additive — `StackLayout`'s other `AppLoadingScreen` call site (`app/_layout.tsx:136`, context-load gate) is unaffected because it doesn't pass `onSignOut`.

Sequencing: build the helper first (1), then wire the guard (2), then the UI (3) — the guard change is what makes the new prop reachable, so UI without the guard change would be dead code, and the guard change without the helper would not compile.

Files to change:
  - lib/powersync/waitForFirstSync.ts
      NEW. Export FirstSyncTimeoutError and waitForFirstSyncOrThrow(timeoutMs = 30_000): AbortController + setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), timeoutMs); await powerSync.waitForFirstSync(controller.signal); if (!powerSync.currentStatus?.hasSynced) throw FirstSyncTimeoutError; clearTimeout in finally. One-line comment above the function per repo convention. This is the reconciled mechanism — supersedes the alternative 'race against an independent rejecting timer' approach some drafts proposed, because that approach leaves the SDK's internal wait/listener un-aborted on every retry.
  - components/GuardComponents/PowerSyncGuard.tsx

Add a handleEscape function (Alert.alert confirm -> forceSignOut, imported from @/context/AuthContext/functions/accountFunctions) and pass onSignOut={handleEscape} to the AppLoadingScreen render at lines 24-30.
  - components/GuardComponents/AppLoadingScreen.tsx

Backward-compatible: StackLayout's call site at app/_layout.tsx:136 omits onSignOut and is unaffected.
  - components/devTest/LoadingScreenTest.tsx

Today this page doesn't exercise loadFailed or onRetry at all, so this closes that preview gap too.
  - lib/powersync/__tests__/waitForFirstSync.test.ts

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    PowerSyncGuard is mounted once in the provider stack (app/_layout.tsx) directly under AuthProvider, wrapping the entire authenticated app; it is the sole first-sync gate, so the fix only affects that gate's loader. AppLoadingScreen has two real production call sites: PowerSyncGuard (gets the new onSignOut) and StackLayout at app/_layout.tsx:136 for context-load state (passes only message/loadFailed/onRetry, unchanged since onSignOut is optional). ensurePowerSyncConnected is already called identically from context/AuthContext/index.tsx and lib/powersync/watchdogStatus.ts callers; it is mutex-serialized and checks currentStatus.connected first, so a second call from the guard cannot double-connect. forceSignOut is already used by app/settingsScreens/profile.tsx with the identical Alert-confirm pattern, so no new sign-out semantics are introduced; it is covered by context/AuthContext/functions/__tests__/accountFunctions.test.ts (unchanged). The three other waitForFirstSync call sites (SettingsContext/index.tsx:136, NutritionContext/index.tsx:46, WorkoutContext/index.tsx:447) run only as children of this guard, i.e. only after it has already resolved or the user has escaped -- they are unreachable during the hang and need no change.
  Edge cases: Fresh install / PowerSync unreachable (the bug): connect attempt retried once more via ensurePowerSyncConnected, then waitForFirstSyncOrThrow times out at 30s -> abort -> hasSynced false -> throws -> status='error' -> retry + sign-out both render. Returning user: persisted first-sync flag resolves waitForFirstSync (and hasSynced is already true) well under 30s -- behavior identical to today, timer cleared, no false error. Slow-but-succeeding sync just under 30s: resolves normally before the abort fires. Sync completes at ~29-30s right at the boundary: abort may fire concurrently, but the hasSynced check after the (possibly abort-triggered) resolve correctly distinguishes 'synced right before abort' from 'aborted with no sync', so no false positive. Retry after timeout: useAsyncLoad's nonce bump re-runs the whole loader, including a fresh ensurePowerSyncConnected + a new AbortController/timer -- no leaked listener from the prior run because the prior wait was properly aborted (not merely abandoned). Component unmount / deps change mid-flight: useAsyncLoad's existing cancelled flag already ignores a stale resolution/rejection; the helper's finally still clears its own timer regardless. Accidental tap on the sign-out escape: guarded by an explicit Alert confirm. No data-loss edge: a device blocked on first sync has no local writes to lose, so forceSignOut (no flush) is safe; using the flush-gated signOut() instead would itself hang against the unreachable connection, which is why it is deliberately not used here.
  Preserved:  The success path is behaviorally unchanged: when first sync completes before the timeout, waitForFirstSyncOrThrow resolves exactly as the bare await did (same resolve value, no throw) and status goes 'ready' -> children render. useAsyncLoad, FlushUploads, and the three context-level waitForFirstSync call sites are not modified. AppLoadingScreen's animation/message logic and existing retry link markup are unchanged; the sign-out link is purely additive and gated on loadFailed && onSignOut, so StackLayout's context-load usage (no onSignOut passed) renders identically to today. All new/changed styling uses only ThemeContext tokens (colors.textSecondary, colors.workout, existing fonts) with no hardcoded colors -- must be verified in both dark and light via the Dev Hub page.
  Tests:      Add lib/powersync/__tests__/waitForFirstSync.test.ts (resolve-before-timeout / reject-on-timeout / late-resolve-does-not-un-reject, fake timers). Add components/GuardComponents/__tests__/PowerSyncGuard.test.tsx (hung sync -> error -> retry+sign-out render; onSignOut -> Alert confirm -> forceSignOut). Add components/GuardComponents/__tests__/AppLoadingScreen.test.tsx (sign-out link gating + accessibility props on both links). Re-run existing lib/hooks/__tests__/useAsyncLoad.test.tsx (confirm the reject-to-error contract still holds) and context/AuthContext/functions/__tests__/accountFunctions.test.ts (confirm forceSignOut still resolves offline, unchanged). Manually verify via the augmented Dev Hub page (Settings -> Developer -> Dev Hub -> App Loading Screen) in light, dark, and at largest Dynamic Type; check VoiceOver focus order over retry -> sign-out and that both hit the >=44pt touch target.

H4  (High)  [DONE]  [LAUNCH-BLOCKER]

[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: Sentry is initialized (app/_layout.tsx:62) but wired into only 3 capture sites (persistErrors.ts:49, Connector.ts:110 dead-letter, notifications/scheduler.ts:40). No Sentry.setUser (events unattributable), no environment tag (prod/preview/dev unsegmentable). The real silent-failure funnels are untelemetried: PowerSync connect failure is a bare console.warn (AuthContext/index.tsx:51); orchestrator connect/kick/disconnect-clear errors only mutate an in-memory lastError string (orchestrator.ts:66,102-106,138); watchdog kicks/errors only update watchdogStatus (SyncWatchdog.tsx:73-83); the force-sign-out data-loss action and its own possible failure are invisible (accountFunctions.tsx:49-53, 63-77); non-flush sign-out failures are a generic uncaptured Alert (profile.tsx:109-111); AI edge-function failures are invisible to ops even though the user sees an alert (openAI.ts's callEdgeFunction, missing both the network-failure path at the fetch call and the res.ok-false path); foodDB search/detail edge failures are uninstrumented (foodDB.ts). Route render crashes hit expo-router's re-exported ErrorBoundary (app/_layout.tsx:52) with no capture. Net effect: the Critical/High data-loss bugs (C3 settings wedge, H2 sync wedge, flush-timeout forced sign-outs) are invisible in production.

Fix: Additive telemetry wiring, one capture site per genuine failure funnel (never two for the same underlying error), plus setUser and environment. Key resolution vs. the banked brief: (1) capture connect failures ONLY at AuthContext's catch (not also in the shared orchestrator.ensurePowerSyncConnected, which is also called by SyncWatchdog's intentionally-swallowed resume path); (2) capture kick failures ONLY inside orchestrator.kickPowerSync's own catch (its one caller, SyncWatchdog, only gets a breadcrumb); (3) add the previously-missing disconnectAndClearPowerSync capture (sign-out local-DB-wipe funnel); (4) use Sentry.captureMessage (not captureException) for the non-throwing forceSignOut data-loss event, plus a new try/catch around its body to capture its own possible failure; (5) profile.tsx's UploadFlushError branch gets a breadcrumb (not a capture, since Cancel means no data was lost) while its else branch (genuinely unexpected sign-out failures) gets a real capture; (6) openAI.ts's capture is broadened to also cover the network/fetch-failure path, not just the res.ok-false branch; (7) foodDB's capture moves into the shared callEdgeFunction helper so both search and details are covered; (8) the ErrorBoundary wrapper's capture is placed in a useEffect keyed on the error reference (one-shot), not in the render body; (9) setUser is consolidated into a single effect keyed on `user`.

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    Sentry.init runs once at module load (app/_layout.tsx); the consolidated setUser effect is a pure side-effect on the existing `user` state — no caller-facing shape change. Each capture site is now the SOLE owner of telemetry for its funnel (connect failures owned by AuthContext; kick and disconnect-clear failures owned by orchestrator; the ErrorBoundary wrapper is the single root route boundary rendered by expo-router). This still overlaps H2 (Connector dead-letter capture already exists; broadening retry/dead-letter classes is H2's) and H5 (release/source-map upload and env-per-profile) — coordinator should consolidate those with H5 as before.
  Edge cases: DSN absent: Sentry.init and every capture/setUser/breadcrumb/message call are safe no-ops. No session/first-run: setUser is simply not called (or called with null) via the consolidated effect. Offline: SDK queues events locally and flushes later; genuinely offline connect/kick/edge-function failures are now captured (previously openAI.ts's offline path was missed — now fixed). PII: only the user UUID is sent — never email/name. Alert-noise is now exactly bounded: one event per genuine failure funnel (connect, kick, disconnect-clear, sign-out-failure, force-sign-out[+its own failure], edge-openai, edge-fooddb); intentionally-swallowed benign paths (watchdog_resume's empty catch, aiFunctions.tsx's by-design vision-estimate fallbacks at L43/L111, and a user-cancelled offline sign-out) remain uncaptured by design, not by oversight.
  Preserved:  No change to FlushUploads Gate-C gating, Connector retry vs. dead-letter classification, orchestrator mutex/throttle, watchdog throttle windows, the 'refused' AI user-message path, or any user-facing alert copy/retry flow. The ErrorBoundary wrapper renders expo-router's default UI with the same {error,retry} props and captures at most once per distinct error via a one-shot effect. The data-loss scenarios (flush-timeout forced sign-out, fleet-wide upload wedge, local-DB-wipe failure) now each emit exactly one attributable Sentry event without altering any data path or gating logic — it removes the blindness (and the risk of double-counting or misclassifying benign paths as failures) without introducing a new wedge or noise problem of its own.
  Tests:      Reuse the Sentry-mock pattern from lib/powersync/__tests__/persistErrors.test.ts and connector.test.ts throughout. New/extended tests: (1) AuthContext setUser.test.ts — setUser({id})/setUser(null) transitions plus a single-capture assertion for connect failures; (2) accountFunctions.test.ts — captureMessage on normal forceSignOut, captureException on its own internal failure; (3) foodDB.test.ts — add the missing @sentry/react-native jest.mock (this file currently requires the real module unmocked and would otherwise break); (4) openAI.test.ts — fill in the empty file with network-failure and res.ok-false coverage; (5) new orchestrator.test.ts and SyncWatchdog.test.tsx pinning the single-capture-per-funnel invariant and the watchdog_resume/enrichment swallow-by-design behavior. Adjudication decisions:
  - Double-capture of a single PowerSync connect failure: the brief adds captureException in ensurePowerSyncConnected's catch (orchestrator.ts:64-68), which then rethrows at L67, AND in AuthContext's .catch (index.tsx:50-52) which receives that same rethrown error — one failure emits two Sentry events. (Reported by ~7 reviewers.)
      -> Material — confirmed at orchestrator.ts:67 (`throw e` after the catch) and index.tsx:50-52 (`.catch` on the same call). Resolved by making AuthContext's catch the SOLE capture site for connect failures (tag area:'powersync-connect', reason:'auth_session'); orchestrator's ensurePowerSyncConnected catch stays state-only (no capture added there at all).  (Correctness (rule 1) — a fix that double-reports the same failure misrepresents the very signal it exists to create, and Safety/blast-radius (rule 2) — alert-noise inflation.)
  - Double-capture of a single watchdog kick failure: orchestrator's kick catch (L136-140, no rethrow, returns 'error') and SyncWatchdog's 'error' outcome branch (L73-83) both captureException for the same failure. Additionally, SyncWatchdog would pass a bare string (orchestratorState.lastError is typed `string`) to captureException, losing the stack trace. (Reported by ~5 reviewers.)
      -> Material — confirmed: kickPowerSync has exactly one caller (SyncWatchdog) and does not rethrow, so both proposed sites fire per failure. Resolved by capturing once, inside orchestrator's kick catch, where the raw `e: unknown` Error object is still in scope (before being reduced to the `lastError: string`). SyncWatchdog's 'error' branch is downgraded to addBreadcrumb only (consistent with its existing 'kicked'/'throttled' breadcrumb plan) — this also fixes the string-vs-Error typing defect for free, since the capture never touches the string field.  (Correctness (rule 1) + Safety (rule 2) for the duplicate event; Convention fit (rule 3) for capturing where the real Error object is already in scope rather than reconstructing one from a string.)
  - Capturing in the orchestrator's ensurePowerSyncConnected catch would also fire on SyncWatchdog's foreground-resume call (watchdog_resume, SyncWatchdog.tsx:130-135), which deliberately swallows the error via an empty catch (comment: 'check() may still kick') — every offline app-resume would become a captured event, contradicting the brief's own 'volume bounded to genuine failures' claim.
      -> Material — confirmed: ensurePowerSyncConnected is called from both AuthContext (real sign-in path) and SyncWatchdog's resume path (intentionally-swallowed benign race). A single capture site inside the shared orchestrator function cannot distinguish the two callers' intent. Resolved by the same amendment as the first finding: capture only at the AuthContext call site (reason 'auth_session'), leaving the orchestrator function and the watchdog_resume swallow completely untouched.  (Correctness (rule 1) — capturing an intentionally-swallowed benign path is a behavior regression, not additive telemetry.)
  - The orchestrator's disconnectAndClearPowerSync catch (L91-107) — the sign-out local-DB-wipe path — is left uninstrumented by the brief's file list (which only names ensure L64-68 and kick L136-140), even though a failed wipe surfaces as a generic, uncaptured Alert via profile.tsx's else branch.
      -> Material — confirmed: disconnectAndClearPowerSync's catch only sets `lastError` with no capture, and it is a genuine data-integrity funnel squarely within H4's stated scope (rootCause explicitly calls out flush-timeout/sign-out data loss as the headline gap). Added as a new site: capture inside disconnectAndClearPowerSync's own catch (raw Error in scope), tagged area:'powersync-disconnect-clear'.  (Correctness (rule 1) — the brief's own rootCause names this class of failure as the thing to fix; omitting the wipe-failure funnel leaves a real gap in the fix.)
  - The ErrorBoundary wrapper, as described ('captureException(error) then renders default'), places the capture in the render body; since the error-screen component stays mounted and can re-render (theme toggle, parent state), this would re-fire captureException on every re-render, not once per crash.
      -> Material — the brief's wording is ambiguous enough to permit a render-body call, which is a real defect if implemented literally. Resolved by specifying the wrapper precisely: a functional component wrapping expo-router's ErrorBoundary that calls captureException inside `useEffect(() => {...}, [error])` — a one-shot effect keyed on the `error` prop reference, not on every render — before rendering the unmodified `{error, retry}` UI.  (Correctness (rule 1) for the duplicate-event bug; Safety (rule 2) since the current behavior (no capture) is at least not noisy, so the fix must not introduce a new noise problem.)
  - Adding a top-level `@sentry/react-native` import to foodDB.ts / openAI.ts / orchestrator.ts risks breaking existing tests that require the real module without a Sentry mock; the package is absent from jest's transformIgnorePatterns and every current capture-site test (connector/scheduler/persistErrors) explicitly `jest.mock`s it as a prerequisite.
      -> Material but narrower than claimed after verification. `context/NutritionContext/functions/__tests__/aiFunctions.test.ts` mocks the entire `openAI`/`foodDB` modules (no risk), `lib/openAI/__tests__/openAI.test.ts` is currently an empty file (no risk yet, but must be filled in with a mock as part of this fix), and no `orchestrator.test.ts`/`SyncWatchdog.test.tsx` exist yet (so new tests can mock from the start). The one real, confirmed risk is `lib/foodDB/__tests__/foodDB.test.ts`, which does `require('../foodDB')` directly with no Sentry mock. Amended: that test file must add `jest.mock('@sentry/react-native', ...)` before requiring the module, matching the codebase's existing convention.  (Safety / blast radius (rule 2) — must not break existing test callers; Convention fit (rule 3) — reuse the exact jest.mock pattern already established in connector.test.ts/persistErrors.test.ts.)
  - `forceSignOut()` has no exception object in scope (no try/catch, no caught error) to pass to `captureException`; it is a deliberate, non-throwing action, so the brief's stated 'captureException' call is a misuse of the API.
      -> Material — confirmed: accountFunctions.tsx:49-53 has no try/catch around the intentional action. Amended to `Sentry.captureMessage('force_sign_out_data_loss', {level:'warning', tags:{area:'force-sign-out-data-loss'}})` — the correct primitive for a deliberate event with no exception object.  (Correctness (rule 1) — the described API call does not match the code shape it targets; Convention fit (rule 3) — captureMessage is the SDK's intended tool for non-exception events.)
  - `forceSignOut()` can itself throw (via `clearLocalSession` -> `removeLocalAuthSession`, which throws 'Could not sign out on this device...' at accountFunctions.tsx:74 when offline and no fallback session-removal method exists); if capture is only added at forceSignOut's entry, that failure surfaces only as a generic Alert in profile.tsx with no telemetry — a failed data-loss attempt is invisible.
      -> Material — confirmed `removeLocalAuthSession` (called unconditionally, not wrapped in try/catch, inside `clearLocalSession`, accountFunctions.tsx:84-94) can throw past `forceSignOut` uncaptured. Amended: wrap forceSignOut's body in try/catch; on catch, `Sentry.captureException(e, {tags:{area:'force-sign-out-failed'}})` then rethrow unchanged so profile.tsx's existing Alert (L101-103) is unaffected.  (Correctness (rule 1) — closes a real remaining blind spot inside H4's own stated scope.)
  - Capturing the UploadFlushError in profile.tsx's handleSignOut catch (as the brief specifies, 'before offering force sign out') fires the capture before the user chooses Force/Cancel — so a user who taps Cancel (no data lost) still generates a data-loss-funnel event; separately, the offline branch is a normal state, not a failure, so every offline sign-out attempt would emit an event, inflating the exact signal H4 exists to make trustworthy.
      -> Material — confirmed: profile.tsx:88-91 is the normal offline/still-syncing control-flow branch, entered before the user's Force/Cancel choice. Amended: this branch gets `Sentry.addBreadcrumb` only (offline vs. timeout, for lead-up trail), not captureException. The actual capturable data-loss event now lives downstream at accountFunctions.forceSignOut (see above), which only fires if the user actually confirms Force sign out.  (Correctness (rule 1) — capturing a Cancel'd, no-data-lost path as a 'data loss' event is a functional misfire, not additive telemetry.)
  - profile.tsx's telemetry only covers the isUploadFlushError branch; the else branch (L109-111), which handles genuinely unexpected sign-out failures (e.g. supabase.auth.signOut error, disconnectAndClearPowerSync failure on the normal signOut path), is left uncaptured — the opposite of closing a silent funnel.
      -> Material — confirmed the else branch has no capture in the brief's plan. Added: `Sentry.captureException(error, {tags:{area:'sign-out-failure'}})` in the else branch before the existing generic Alert.  (Correctness (rule 1) — this branch is a genuinely uninstrumented failure funnel squarely inside H4's rootCause.)
  - The rootCause mischaracterizes lib/openAI/openAI.ts:44 as a 'swallowed' AI/edge failure; it is a `throw` that propagates and is re-thrown/surfaced to the user by aiFunctions.tsx (L153-155, L174-176). The actual swallow points are the bare `catch {}` fallbacks in aiFunctions.tsx (L43, L111) — by-design graceful-degradation paths, not real failures.
      -> Material as a rootCause-wording correction, not as a reason to abandon the fix. Verified: L44 throws and is surfaced to the user; L43/L111 are intentional 'keep the vision estimate' fallbacks, not silent user-facing failures. The capture at openAI.ts is still valuable for ops-side aggregation (users seeing an alert ≠ Sentry visibility), so it is kept, but the rootCause text is corrected to say 'invisible to ops, not to the user' rather than 'swallowed'. The by-design aiFunctions.tsx fallbacks are explicitly left uninstrumented (breadcrumb-only if anything), matching their intended degrade-gracefully behavior.  (Correctness (rule 1) — the brief must describe the code accurately even where the underlying fix is retained.)
  - The proposed openAI.ts capture site (L44, the `!res.ok` branch) never runs for offline/network failures, since `fetch` itself (L22) rejects first and that rejection is never caught locally — genuine offline AI-analysis failures would still emit zero telemetry.
      -> Material — confirmed `callEdgeFunction` has no try/catch around the `fetch` call; a network-level rejection at L22 propagates without hitting L31-44. Amended: wrap the whole `callEdgeFunction` body (fetch through the res.ok branch) in one try/catch; capture any failure except the 'refused' friendly-message case (preserving the brief's original intent to leave 'refused' unchanged), then rethrow always.  (Correctness (rule 1) — the fix must cover the funnel it claims to close, including its most common real-world trigger (offline).)
  - The foodDB funnel is only partially instrumented: the brief adds capture at getFoodDetails' swallowed `return null` (L74-76) but getFoodSearchResults (L56-58) re-throws its edge-function failure with no capture at all.
      -> Material — confirmed getFoodSearchResults' catch-and-rethrow (effectively a no-op catch) has no telemetry. Amended: move the capture into the shared `callEdgeFunction` helper (used by both getFoodSearchResults and getFoodDetails) so one instrumentation site covers both callers, rather than duplicating logic per-caller.  (Correctness (rule 1) for the coverage gap; Convention fit + Least code (rules 3-4) for instrumenting the shared helper once instead of twice.)
  - Instrumenting `Sentry.setUser` at two separate call sites (the initial getSession effect ~L21 and the onAuthStateChange listener ~L32) is more fragile/duplicative than a single `useEffect(() => Sentry.setUser(user ? {id:user.id} : null), [user])` keyed off the existing `user` state, which already captures every transition (initial load, sign-in, token refresh, sign-out) from one source.
      -> Material — verified both call sites exist and would need to be kept in sync manually; a single effect on `[user]` is strictly simpler and covers the same transitions with less code and no risk of drifting between the two sites.  (Convention fit + Least code (rules 3-4) — same behavior, smaller/more maintainable diff; does not touch correctness or safety.)

H5  (High)  [DONE]
