[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: No automated correctness verification exists anywhere in the pipeline. The only GitHub workflow is `.github/workflows/codeql.yml` (security scanning, build-mode `none` — no tests/typecheck/lint). `package.json:5-13` has no `typecheck` script and no `lint` script/config; `test:ci` is a bare `jest`. `eas.json` profiles have no pre-build verification hook, and EAS builds are triggered manually from a laptop. Any regression — including in the sync/data-loss-critical paths — ships unverified. Investigation (reproduced against the current tree): `tsc --noEmit` emits 25 errors, not 32 — 20 confined to `lib/supabase/functions/**` (Deno edge functions, never imported by client code) and exactly 5 real errors in shipped/test code (app/_layout.tsx:64, components/devTest/DevStatsModal.tsx:106 whose true source is lib/powersync/watchdogStatus.ts:40, context/BillingContext/index.tsx:105/114, context/NutritionContext/functions/__tests__/crudFunctions.test.ts:78, context/WorkoutContext/functions/__tests__/graphFunctions.test.ts:92). There are zero TS7016 errors — every react-test-renderer import already carries a `// @ts-ignore` (e.g. context/BillingContext/__tests__/billingContext.test.tsx:3), so no devDependency is needed there. `jest` reports 8 failing suites / 67 failing tests (confirmed). Two of those 8 (lib/foodDB/__tests__/foodDB.test.ts, lib/openAI/__tests__/openAI.test.ts) fail because lib/supabase/client.ts constructs the Supabase client at module-import time via `createClient(ENV.SUPABASE_URL!, ...)`, which throws when EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL is undefined (reproduced: 'supabaseUrl is required' from validateSupabaseUrl) — exactly the condition on a fresh GitHub Actions checkout, since .env/.env.local are gitignored and only .env.example is tracked, and neither test file mocks `@/lib/supabase/client` (unlike connector.test.ts:16, which does). One of the 8 (lib/powersync/__tests__/connector.test.ts) fails not because of a runtime Connector.ts defect but because its own supabase mock chain is missing `.select` on `.from()`'s return (`TypeError: _client.supabase.from(...).select is not a function`, confirmed at Connector.ts:133/200). Separately, context/BillingContext/index.tsx:105 is not a cosmetic type mismatch: `Purchases.addCustomerInfoUpdateListener` returns `void` (react-native-purchases v9 API), so `listener?.remove?.()` at line 114 is dead code today and the listener is never removed across effect re-runs (deps [authLoading, user?.id]) — a genuine leak that a type-only fix would leave in place.

Fix: Operational + small-config fix in two coupled parts. PART A (the gate): add `.github/workflows/ci.yml` triggered on `pull_request` and `push` to `main` — steps: actions/checkout@v4, actions/setup-node@v4 (node 20, `cache: npm`), `npm ci`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run test:ci` (optional non-blocking `npm run lint`). CRITICAL ADDITION: the job must supply an `env:` block with syntactically-valid EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL / EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY / EXPO_PUBLIC_POWERSYNC_URL (these are public client-side values, not secrets — dummy/test values are fine since the affected tests never make real network calls), OR add `jest.mock('@/lib/supabase/client', ...)` to foodDB.test.ts and openAI.test.ts matching connector.test.ts's existing pattern — without one of these, `lib/supabase/client.ts`'s import-time `createClient()` call throws in CI even after all other remediation, and the gate can never go green there. Then set the CI job as a required status check via branch protection on `main`. Add to `package.json` scripts: `"typecheck": "tsc --noEmit"`, change `test:ci` to `"jest --ci"`. Do NOT add `@types/react-test-renderer` — it fixes nothing (0 TS7016 errors exist; the imports are already `// @ts-ignore`'d) and would only introduce an unnecessary lockfile-sync risk for `npm ci`. PART B (make baseline green, mandatory prerequisite): (1) `tsconfig.json` add `"exclude": ["lib/supabase/functions/**"]` — clears 20 of 25 tsc errors (Deno edge functions never imported into the RN bundle); (2) fix the 5 remaining real type errors: `app/_layout.tsx:64` stale Sentry option; `lib/powersync/watchdogStatus.ts:40` change `return () => listeners.delete(listener)` to `return () => { listeners.delete(listener) }` (fixes the boolean-return error at its source, not by patching DevStatsModal.tsx's call site); `context/BillingContext/index.tsx:105/114` — real fix, not a type coercion: capture the listener callback in a local const, pass that const to `addCustomerInfoUpdateListener`, and call `Purchases.removeCustomerInfoUpdateListener(thatConst)` in the cleanup instead of `listener?.remove?.()`, which actually stops the leak; `crudFunctions.test.ts:78` and `graphFunctions.test.ts:92` mock/type fixes in test files; (3) triage the 8 failing jest suites: `connector.test.ts` needs its incomplete supabase mock completed (add `.select` to the `.from()` mock chain) — the runtime Connector.ts code is not at fault and must not be touched; `openAI.test.ts` needs a real test added or the stale empty-suite stub removed, not an 'assertion fix'; `foodDB.test.ts` and `openAI.test.ts`'s remaining failures are resolved once PART A's env/mock fix (above) is in place; the rest, fix assertions or skip-quarantine with a tracking note only if genuinely stale. Reuse existing `test:ci` script and jest config; do NOT add new abstractions or a lint ruleset beyond expo's default. Do NOT alter any runtime sync/persistence logic beyond the type/leak fixes explicitly listed above.

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    Purely additive infra plus a small number of tiny, targeted runtime fixes. The tsconfig `exclude` only narrows what tsc checks (Metro bundles by import graph; the Deno edge functions are never imported by client code), so the shipped bundle is byte-identical. `app/_layout.tsx:64` removes an option Sentry v8 already ignores. `lib/powersync/watchdogStatus.ts:40` has exactly one current consumer (DevStatsModal.tsx, dev-only) — confirmed by grep — so the fix is contained. `context/BillingContext/index.tsx:105/114` is NOT a no-op change (amended from the original brief): it deliberately fixes a genuine listener-leak by correcting the add/remove pairing to the real react-native-purchases v9 API; still a single function, no consumer-facing signature change, no other file touches this listener. The CI workflow runs on GitHub only; it changes nothing at runtime except requiring an env: block (dummy public values) that has no production effect.
  Edge cases: CI runs on Linux — watch path-casing in imports. `npm ci` requires the committed package-lock.json unchanged, which holds now that the @types/react-test-renderer devDep addition is dropped. `jest --ci` disables watch and makes snapshots deterministic. Without an env: block (or client mocks), foodDB.test.ts and openAI.test.ts will stay red in actual GitHub CI even if green on a developer's machine where a local .env is present — this was verified empirically and is the main hazard the original brief missed. connector.test.ts's failures are a test-mock defect, not a Connector.ts runtime issue — do not 'fix' Connector.ts itself. The 8 currently-failing suites and 25 tsc errors are the main hazard: enabling the required check before greening them would block all merges to main.
  Preserved:  No sync/queue, PowerSync Connector, FlushUploads, first-sync, or persistence logic is modified. Existing jest preset, transformIgnorePatterns, and tsconfig strict/paths are untouched. The BillingContext and watchdogStatus fixes correct genuine latent bugs to their intended behavior (a stopped leak, a corrected unsubscribe signature) rather than being pure type patches — this is a deliberate, narrow behavior change, not a preserved-behavior no-op, and is called out explicitly so it isn't mistaken for cosmetic.
  Tests:      The gate IS the test surface. Before flipping the required-status-check on: run `npm run typecheck` and confirm exit 0, run `npm run test:ci` and confirm 0 failing suites, AND confirm the workflow is actually green on a real GitHub Actions run (not just locally) — a local .env can mask the env-var gap that only manifests in CI. Prioritize restoring lib/powersync/__tests__/connector.test.ts (data-loss-critical) to green by completing its mock chain. Overlaps logic-correctness/code-structure domains for the 5 real type errors, the BillingContext leak fix, and the 8 failing suites — this brief owns the pipeline/gate, the tsconfig/scripts changes, and now explicitly the CI env-var/mocking gap and the two amended bug fixes (BillingContext leak, watchdogStatus source fix).

Adjudication decisions:
  - BillingContext:105 'type fix' (findings #1 and #5): addCustomerInfoUpdateListener returns void; listener?.remove?.() is dead code so every effect re-run (deps [authLoading, user?.id]) adds a new listener that is never removed — a real leak, not a cosmetic type mismatch.
      -> MATERIAL — accepted. Verified against node_modules/react-native-purchases/dist/purchases.d.ts:143/149: addCustomerInfoUpdateListener(...) => void, and removal requires Purchases.removeCustomerInfoUpdateListener(sameCallbackRef) => boolean. There is no .remove() method, so listener?.remove?.() at index.tsx:114 is provably a no-op today, and the leak is real (confirmed by reading the effect: previousUserIdRef/listener are re-created each time authLoading/user?.id changes). Brief amended: BillingContext fix is no longer a 'type-only' change — store the callback in a local const, pass it to addCustomerInfoUpdateListener, and call Purchases.removeCustomerInfoUpdateListener(callback) in cleanup instead of listener?.remove?.(). Still a single-function, ~4-line change with no consumer impact, so it stays in-scope for this brief; blastRadius text is amended to stop claiming 'no runtime behavior change' for this one file.  (Rule 1 (correctness, hard gate) — a fix that satisfies tsc while leaving a real listener leak does not resolve the actual defect and misrepresents blast radius.)
  - DevStatsModal.tsx:106's tsc error actually originates in lib/powersync/watchdogStatus.ts:40 (subscribeWatchdogStatus returns () => listeners.delete(listener), and Set.delete returns boolean); fixing at the shared source is equally minimal and more correct than patching the one call site.
      -> MATERIAL — accepted. Verified: watchdogStatus.ts:40 is exactly `return () => listeners.delete(listener)`; grep confirms DevStatsModal.tsx is the only current consumer, so blast radius is unchanged, but the fix target moves from the consumer to the shared helper (`return () => { listeners.delete(listener) }`). Brief's files[] entry for DevStatsModal.tsx:106 is replaced with lib/powersync/watchdogStatus.ts:40.  (Rule 2 (safety) / Rule 3 (convention fit) — root-cause fix in the shared helper is safer for any future consumer than a call-site patch; both are one line, so Rule 4 is a tie broken by Rules 2/3.)
  - Adding '@types/react-test-renderer' to package.json devDependencies without regenerating/committing package-lock.json will make the new ci.yml's `npm ci` step fail (lockfile out of sync). (Raised independently 4 times: findings #2, #4, #7, plus implicitly #9.)
      -> MATERIAL as raised, but resolved by dropping the underlying step entirely rather than by patching the lockfile — see next finding. Once the devDep addition is removed from the brief, PART A adds no new dependency and this concern is moot (no lockfile edit needed).  (Rule 4 (least code) — removing the unnecessary step is a smaller, cleaner fix than adding a lockfile-regeneration sub-step for a devDep that does nothing (see next finding).)
  - The brief's tsc-error accounting is wrong (claims 32 errors / 7 TS7016 cleared by @types/react-test-renderer); actual `tsc --noEmit` emits 25 errors and 0 TS7016 (findings #6, #8, #12), and react-test-renderer imports are already suppressed via `// @ts-ignore` (finding #9, e.g. context/BillingContext/__tests__/billingContext.test.tsx:3), so the devDep is inert.

Confirmed the @ts-ignore suppression is already in place.       -> MATERIAL — accepted, and the single most important correctness gap found. Reproduced empirically: with EXPO_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL unset, `npx jest lib/foodDB/__tests__/foodDB.test.ts` fails immediately with 'supabaseUrl is required' from validateSupabaseUrl at lib/supabase/client.ts:9, at import time, before any assertion runs — exactly what CI would see, since ci.yml as specified has no env: block and CI has no .env file. H6  (High)  [MERGED]
===============================================================================
[Consolidated multi-agent brief (not yet adjudicated)]

Root cause: SettingsContext, WorkoutContext, and AuthContext each pass a fresh inline object literal to `<Context.Provider value={{...}}>` on every render (SettingsContext/index.tsx:216-234, WorkoutContext/index.tsx:477-514, AuthContext/index.tsx:60-69). React context has no field-level selectors — it re-renders every consumer whenever the value *reference* changes, so any provider render, even one driven by state no consumer reads, forces an app-wide re-render pass.\n\nThis is worst in SettingsContext: `persistDirty`/`persistRetryNonce` (index.tsx:24-25, 148-191) are provider-local save-cycle state, not part of the exposed value, yet each settings write flips `persistDirty` true→false, producing two extra provider render passes and therefore two extra full-consumer sweeps per write for data nothing downstream reads. AuthContext has the same shape: `onAuthStateChange` (index.tsx:29-37) hands back a new `session` object on every token refresh, rippling a new value through the tree (including into Settings and Workout, both of which consume `useAuth()` for `userID`).\n\nCompounding the Settings case, `handleGetBodyWeightProgressData` (index.tsx:122) is a bare arrow recreated every render, not `useCallback` — and it is a listed dependency of app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:77-81's `topRawData` useMemo, so that chart memo is silently defeated on every SettingsProvider render even independent of the value-object issue.\n\nNutritionContext (index.tsx:149-170) and BillingContext (index.tsx:142) already wrap their provider value in `useMemo`; the hardening simply never landed in these three sibling copies — this is a consistency/de-duplication fix, not new machinery. (All four banked domain drafts for H6 independently converge on this identical root cause and fix; they are treated here as one reconciled brief, not three rival ones.)

Fix: Wrap each of the three providers' inline `value={{...}}` object literals in `useMemo` with an exhaustive dependency list (the pattern already shipping in NutritionContext/index.tsx:149-170 and BillingContext/index.tsx:142), and stabilize SettingsContext's one stray non-memoized callback first so it can serve as a dep:\n\n1. SettingsContext/index.tsx:122 — convert `handleGetBodyWeightProgressData` to `useCallback([bwProgress])` (this also un-defeats progress.tsx:77-81's `topRawData` memo, independent of the provider-value fix).\n2. SettingsContext/index.tsx:216-234 — wrap the value literal in `useMemo`, deps = every exposed field except the deliberately-internal `persistDirty`/`persistRetryNonce`.\n3. WorkoutContext/index.tsx:477-514 — wrap the value literal in `useMemo`, deps = the state values (handlers are already useCallback-stable).\n4. AuthContext/index.tsx:60-69 — wrap the value literal in `useMemo([user, session, loading, userID])`.\n5. Add `useMemo` to the `react` import in Settings (line 4) and Auth (line 4); Workout already has it (line 7).\n\nThis is memoization only — zero rendered/exposed values change; only how often the context value reference (and therefore every consumer) re-renders changes. Deliberately NOT touched: persist/retry effect bodies, handler implementations, the exposed-field set, and no `React.memo` added to leaf components (that is a separate follow-on once these value references are stable, and is co-sequenced with the H8 performance track — landing H6 first removes the re-render multiplier H8 flags as amplifying its own per-row cost, so sequence H6 before/alongside H8).

Files to change:
  - context/SettingsContext/index.tsx
      Line 4: add `useMemo` to the `react` import (currently imports `useCallback, useContext, useEffect, useRef, useState` but not `useMemo`). Line 122: convert `handleGetBodyWeightProgressData` from a bare arrow to `useCallback((onboardingCompletedAt?: Date) => getBodyWeightProgressData(bwProgress, onboardingCompletedAt), [bwProgress])` — this both stabilizes it as a value-memo dep and un-defeats progress.tsx:77-81's `topRawData` useMemo. Lines 216-234: wrap the Provider's inline object literal in `const value = useMemo(() => ({...}), [settings, setSettings, mode, setMode, bwProgress, handleUpdateBw, pendingGoalPrompt, dismissGoalPrompt, switchToMaintenance, acknowledgeGoalOvershoot, handleGetBodyWeightProgressData, calculateMacros, loaded, loadFailed, retryLoad, completeOnboarding])` and render `value={value}`. `persistDirty`/`persistRetryNonce` are intentionally NOT deps — they are provider-local and not exposed. `setSettings, setMode, handleUpdateBw, dismissGoalPrompt, switchToMaintenance, acknowledgeGoalOvershoot, retryLoad, completeOnboarding` are already useCallback/useState-setter/useAsyncLoad-stable; `calculateMacros` is a stable module import.
  - context/WorkoutContext/index.tsx
      Lines 477-514: wrap the Provider's inline object literal in `const value = useMemo(() => ({...}), [...])` listing all 26 exposed members (state: workouts, exercises, logs, fullExerciseLib, fullExerciseLibAsList, lastExercise, userExercises, loaded, loadFailed; already-stable: retryLoad, setUserExercises, setLastExercise, and every handleX/getFatigueFeedback useCallback; already-useMemo: trainedDaysThisWeek) and render `value={value}`. `useMemo` is already imported (line 7); no handler edits needed — every handler is already useCallback-stable so the memo effectively re-keys only on the state values listed.
  - context/AuthContext/index.tsx
      Line 4: add `useMemo` to the `react` import (currently `createContext, PropsWithChildren, useContext, useEffect, useState`). Lines 60-69: wrap the Provider's inline object literal in `const value = useMemo(() => ({...}), [user, session, loading, userID])` and render `value={value}`; `signInWithApple, signOut, deleteAccount` are stable module-level imports. Document (do not attempt to fix here) that `onAuthStateChange` (lines 29-37) hands back a new `session` object on every token refresh, so this memo still recomputes on refresh — the fix removes redundant renders from Auth's OWN re-renders, but does not fully insulate downstream consumers from a genuine refresh event; that requires a data-shape change and is out of scope for H6.
  - context/SettingsContext/__tests__/providerMemo.test.tsx (new)
      New provider-stability test, using the existing context/BillingContext/__tests__/billingContext.test.tsx as the render/mock template: render SettingsProvider with a probe consumer that captures the context value reference + render count; assert (a) a persistDirty-only pass (dirty flips true→false with `settings` unchanged) leaves the value reference and probe render count unchanged, and (b) a real `settings`/`bwProgress` mutation changes the reference and the new data flows to the consumer. Add lighter parallel checks for WorkoutContext (an internal lastExercise-save pass) and AuthContext (a same-userID token-refresh pass, asserting only `userID`-reading consumers are unaffected — per the documented refresh caveat).

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    Every consumer of useSettings() / useWorkout() / useAuth() app-wide: all (tabs) screens (index/progress/settings), onboarding, settingsScreens, nutrition + workout modals, GoalPromptHost, WorkoutComponents, and the Stack.Protected route-guard logic in app/_layout.tsx (useAuth is itself consumed by SettingsContext and WorkoutContext, so an Auth value churn today cascades into both). No import path, field name, or hook signature changes anywhere — only how often each consumer re-renders. Directly-named beneficiary: app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:77-81 `topRawData` useMemo, which lists `handleGetBodyWeightProgressData` as a dep — stabilizing it (SettingsContext/index.tsx:122) stops that memo from being defeated on every SettingsProvider render, independent of the value-object fix.
  Edge cases: (1) Dependency-completeness is the whole risk: every value-object member that can change must appear in its provider's useMemo dep array, or a consumer could read a stale value (a correctness regression, not just perf) — verified the enumerated lists below are exhaustive against the current field lists at SettingsContext:217-234 (16 fields), WorkoutContext:478-514 (26 fields), AuthContext:61-69 (7 fields). (2) Settings save cycle: `persistDirty`/`persistRetryNonce`/`persistRetryTimerRef` (index.tsx:24-25, 148-191) are deliberately EXCLUDED from the value memo's deps — their flips are the redundant churn being eliminated; the `settings` state they eventually produce IS a dep, so the real, settled change still propagates once the write lands. (3) Auth token refresh: `onAuthStateChange` (AuthContext/index.tsx:29-37) hands back a brand-new `session` object on every refresh even when the user is unchanged; since `session` is a required dep (raw session/user must stay correct for any consumer that reads them), the Auth value memo will still recompute — and therefore still ripple into Settings/Workout — on every refresh. The win here is real but partial: consumers that only read the `userID` string (the common case) are unaffected across a same-user refresh once Settings/Workout's own memos are in place, but AuthContext's own value continues to re-key on refresh. This is documented as an accepted, in-scope-noted limitation, not silently hidden — a deeper narrowing of what Auth exposes is out of scope for H6. (4) First-run / offline / null-userID: `loaded`, `loadFailed`, `userID` are deps in all three providers, so those legitimate state transitions still propagate normally. (5) Load failure / rollback: `reloadFromDisk` mutates `settings`/`bwProgress` (Settings) and `workouts`/`exercises`/`logs`/`userExercises` (Workout), all deps, so a rollback is still observed by consumers. (6) Concurrent writes / large history: this is the intended win — logging a set or editing one settings field no longer forces two-plus extra app-wide consumer passes; cost scaling with account age makes this a real (if non-blocking) perf issue, not a launch gate.
  Preserved:  Pure referential-equality optimization: `useMemo` returns byte-identical value contents and only reuses the reference when every listed dep is unchanged — no consumer ever observes a different value than it does today; rendered output is untouched. No handler body, effect timing, persist/backoff/retry logic, or exposed field is added, removed, or renamed. `handleGetBodyWeightProgressData` keeps its current always-fresh-closure semantics — `useCallback([bwProgress])` simply stops it from changing identity when `bwProgress` hasn't. The pattern mirrors what already ships in NutritionContext/index.tsx:149-170 and BillingContext/index.tsx:142, so this closes a hardening gap rather than introducing new machinery — {children} is already a stable element in all three providers, so a provider's own re-render already bails out of the child subtree; only the context value itself was propagating unnecessarily, and that is the only thing this fix touches.
  Tests:      Add one new provider-stability suite (template: context/BillingContext/__tests__/billingContext.test.tsx, which already exists) under context/SettingsContext/__tests__/providerMemo.test.tsx: render SettingsProvider with a probe consumer that records the received context-value reference and render count; (a) drive a persistDirty-only pass (e.g. a resolved save that flips dirty true→false without changing `settings`) and assert the value reference and probe render count are unchanged; (b) mutate `settings` for real and assert the reference changes and the new data flows. Add the parallel, lighter-weight assertions for WorkoutContext (an internal lastExercise-save pass) and AuthContext (a same-userID token-refresh pass — asserting only that `userID`-consumers are unaffected, per the documented partial-fix caveat). Re-run the existing logic suites unchanged to prove no behavior moved: context/SettingsContext/functions/__tests__/* (bodyWeightFunctions, computeBwUpdate, macroCalculation), context/WorkoutContext/functions/__tests__/* (fatigueFunctions, graphFunctions, volumeFunctions, workoutFunctions), context/AuthContext/functions/__tests__/*, and the full `npm run test:ci`. Manual smoke: log one set and edit a settings field in the same session; confirm the body-weight/1RM chart values and progress screen are unchanged and no dropped/duplicated renders occur.
  DevHub:     approval=na; plan={"applicable":false,"naReason":"Context-provider memoization is an internal render-performance fix with no new UI surface, screen, or dev-toggleable scenario to preview — verified via unit/render tests and manual smoke, not Dev Hub."}

===============================================================================
H7  (High)  [MERGED]
===============================================================================
[Consolidated multi-agent brief (not yet adjudicated)]

Root cause: Both context loaders eagerly hydrate a user's ENTIRE lifetime history into React state, unbounded by any date filter or LIMIT: `loadWorkoutData` (context/WorkoutContext/database/powersyncStore.ts:80-83) runs `SELECT * FROM logs WHERE user_id = ? ORDER BY date DESC, time DESC` and `loadNutritionData` (context/NutritionContext/database/powersyncStore.ts:114,118,134,138) does the equivalent for `nutrition_entries` + their `nutrition_entry_ingredients` (IN-list at :118) and `saved_nutrition_entries` + ingredients (:134,138). Every row is mapped into 2-3 `new Date()`/`parseDateKey` allocations (`rowToLog` :43-59, `rowToNutritionEntry` :12-38). This full-history cost is paid (a) on every cold start and user change (`useAsyncLoad` — WorkoutContext/index.tsx:439-448, NutritionContext/index.tsx:40-51, both gated behind `powerSync.waitForFirstSync()`), and (b) on EVERY failed single-row write, because the rollback path `reloadFromDisk` (WorkoutContext/index.tsx:52-64, NutritionContext/index.tsx:59-68, invoked via `reportPersistFailure(...,{reload})` from ~15 write catch-sites) re-runs the SAME unbounded full-history query just to roll back one optimistic row. Within each loader the independent reads also run serially (4 sequential `getAll`s in `loadWorkoutData` :70-88; 2 independent + 2 dependent in `loadNutritionData` :114-149). A ~2-year daily user therefore carries ~10k logs + ~3k entries (+ingredients) resident in RAM permanently, and both row count and per-row allocation cost grow linearly with account age — a day-one-fine, degrades-with-history profile, not a launch blocker (confirmed `launchBlocker: false` across all four domain drafts). SQLite itself is well-indexed (`logs`: user_id, exercise_id, date — AppSchema.ts:157-162; `nutrition_entries`: user_id, date — AppSchema.ts:~68) so the fix is JS-side hydration scoping only, not a schema change.

Verified against current code: powersyncStore.ts:70-88 (4 serial getAlls, exact line match to all drafts), :80-83 (logs query), NutritionContext powersyncStore.ts:114/118/134/138 (exact match), WorkoutContext/index.tsx:52-64 (reloadFromDisk) and :439-448 (useAsyncLoad initial load, one line lower than drafts' ":448" cite but same call), NutritionContext/index.tsx:40-51 and :59-68 (both exact), logsModal.tsx:56-68 filters the full context `logs` array by exerciseID and ALSO feeds `getDailyGoal`/`isGoalHitToday` from that same array (progressionFunctions.ts:50-67, confirmed sole caller is logsModal.tsx:120,126), progress.tsx:78/90 confirmed the 1RM chart (`handleGetOneRepMaxData` → graphFunctions.tsx:37 `getOneRepMaxData`, operates on the full in-memory `logs`) and the week-nav floor `minWeekStart` explicitly pages back to `settings.onboardingCompletedAt` (progress.tsx:90) — i.e. an all-time consumer that a naive fixed-window cutoff would silently truncate for veteran users.

Fix: Four domain drafts converge on the same two-part shape (parallelize now + window later) with only two real disagreements, both resolved below by the stated rubric (correctness > safety > convention-fit > least-code > testability).

STEP 1 — parallelize the independent reads (safe, ship first, zero behavior change).
In `loadWorkoutData`, the four `getAll`s (workouts :70-73, exercises :75-78, logs :80-83, user_exercises :85-88) are mutually independent → wrap in one `Promise.all`. In `loadNutritionData`, `entryRows` (:114) and `savedEntryRows` (:134) are independent of each other → `Promise.all` those two; their ingredient IN-list queries (:118, :138) depend on the resulting ids, so keep those as a second `Promise.all` stage. This only reorders await resolution — output arrays, ordering, and mapping are provably identical (all four drafts agree; no conflict).

STEP 2 — bound the two unbounded-growth tables (`logs`, `nutrition_entries`) with an indexed date window; leave the small/bounded tables (`workouts`, `exercises`, `user_exercises`, `saved_nutrition_entries`) untouched — they scale with exercise/meal-template count, not account age, so windowing them buys nothing and risks correctness.
- Add a single named constant `HISTORY_WINDOW_DAYS` (recommend 400 — the more conservative of the two values proposed across drafts, 180 vs 400; per the safety-over-least-code rubric, the larger margin costs nothing extra in query cost since both columns are already indexed, and it minimizes edge cases where a real consumer's "recent" lookback exceeds the window).
- Compute the lower bound as **whichever date is more recent** of `(today − HISTORY_WINDOW_DAYS)` and `settings.onboardingCompletedAt`, using `getDateKey`/`parseDateKey` (never `toISOString` — en-CA locale rule, per CLAUDE.md). For any account younger than the window this makes the bound equal to `onboardingCompletedAt`, i.e. a literal no-op returning identical rows to today; only accounts older than the window are actually bounded. Add `AND date >= ?` to the `logs` query (:80-83) and the `nutrition_entries` query (:114); the ingredient IN-list (:118) automatically follows the now-smaller entry set.
- Apply the SAME bound to both the initial load call sites (WorkoutContext/index.tsx useAsyncLoad :439-448; NutritionContext/index.tsx useAsyncLoad :40-51) and to `reloadFromDisk` (WorkoutContext/index.tsx:52-64; NutritionContext/index.tsx:59-68).

CONFLICT 1 — resolved: `reloadFromDisk` scoping. Two drafts (the "graph/perf-lens" one and the "logic-lens" one) proposed turning `reloadFromDisk` into a targeted single-row re-read; two drafts (the "structure-lens" and "coordinator-reconciled" ones) argued it must keep its full-reread-of-committed-disk-state semantics for correctness, and that windowing the underlying query already shrinks its cost automatically. Per correctness-then-safety-then-least-code: adopt the latter — `reloadFromDisk` keeps calling the same (now-windowed) loader, unchanged in shape, just bound identically to the initial load. A bespoke "re-read only the touched row(s)" path is a materially larger, riskier change (must track exactly which row(s) failed, across ~15 disparate call sites) for a cost that Step 2's window already collapses to a bounded read. Note it explicitly as an optional follow-up, not required for this fix.

CONFLICT 2 — resolved: fixed 180-day window vs. onboarding-floored window. A flat 180-day cutoff (one draft) would truncate the Progress tab's week-nav, which already pages back to `onboardingCompletedAt` (progress.tsx:90) for any user onboarded >180 days ago — a real regression. The onboarding-floored `max(today−N, onboardingCompletedAt)` bound (two drafts) avoids this for accounts inside the window, but for veteran accounts (the exact case H7 targets) the window still applies and those same screens (1RM "all" range, week-nav paging, per-exercise all-time list) still need history older than the window. All drafts therefore also require STEP 3 below — the window size doesn't remove the need for on-demand queries, it just determines how often they fire.

STEP 3 — restore all-time behavior for the three consumers a window would otherwise silently truncate:
(a) `logsModal.tsx:56-68` currently filters the in-memory `logs` array by `exerciseID` for BOTH the rendered set-history list AND (via `getDailyGoal`/`isGoalHitToday`, progressionFunctions.ts:50-67, called only from logsModal.tsx:120,126) the next-set suggestion. Replace this in-memory filter with one new indexed store query, `loadExerciseLogHistory(userId, exerciseId)` (uses the existing `exercise_id` index, AppSchema.ts:158), and feed its full (unwindowed) result to BOTH the rendered list and `getDailyGoal`/`isGoalHitToday`. This is smaller than the alternative some drafts proposed (a separate `loadLatestLogPerExercise` merge into global state) because logsModal already needs the full per-exercise history for its own list — reusing that same result for progression's anchor is a one-line change, not a second query path.
(b) `progress.tsx` (`handleGetOneRepMaxData` at :78 → `getOneRepMaxData`, graphFunctions.tsx:37) and the week-nav bar paging to `onboardingCompletedAt` (:90): when the user's selection/pagination reaches back past the window, fetch that exercise's/week's data on demand via the same `loadExerciseLogHistory`-style query rather than deriving from the windowed in-memory array.
(c) Mirror (b) for the nutrition macro graph if/when it is confirmed to page beyond 30 days (current graph functions operate within a 30-day lookback per the rootCause analysis — verify before adding a `loadMacroHistory` helper; only add it if a beyond-window consumer is actually found, to avoid speculative code).

SEQUENCING: Step 1 (Promise.all) is independent and can land alone with no risk. Step 2 (windowing) and Step 3 (on-demand restoration) must land together in the same change — a window without Step 3 silently regresses logsModal's all-time list, progression suggestions for dormant exercises, and progress-tab paging for veteran accounts, which is exactly the "naive windowing regresses rendered values" failure mode every draft flagged. Do not ship Step 2 without Step 3.

Deliberately NOT touched: SQLite schema/indexes (already adequate), the Connector/upload path, RLS/sync-rules, and the multi-context first-paint blocking in StackLayout (separate, already-tracked concern).

Files to change:
  - context/WorkoutContext/database/powersyncStore.ts
      Lines 70-88: wrap the 4 independent getAlls in one Promise.all (Step 1). Add HISTORY_WINDOW_DAYS constant and a sinceDateKey/onboardingCompletedAt-floored lower bound; add `AND date >= ?` to the logs query (:80-83) (Step 2). Add new exported `loadExerciseLogHistory(userId, exerciseId)` returning full unbounded per-exercise history for logsModal (Step 3a/3b). Keep rowToLog (:43-59) output shape unchanged.
  - context/NutritionContext/database/powersyncStore.ts
      Lines 114-149: Promise.all the two independent queries (entryRows :114, savedEntryRows :134), then Promise.all the two dependent ingredient IN-list queries (:118, :138) (Step 1). Add the same window bound to the nutrition_entries query (:114); the ingredient IN-list follows automatically (Step 2).   - context/WorkoutContext/index.tsx

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    loadWorkoutData: called from WorkoutContext/index.tsx useAsyncLoad (:439-448) and reloadFromDisk (:52-64) only. loadNutritionData: called from NutritionContext/index.tsx useAsyncLoad (:40-51) and reloadFromDisk (:59-68) only, the latter reached via reportPersistFailure's reload callback from ~15 write-failure catch sites in both contexts. H8  (High)  [DONE]
===============================================================================
[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: `getDateKey` (lib/utils/dateHelper.ts:25-27) is implemented as `date.toLocaleDateString("en-CA")` — an Intl-backed call that is 1-2 orders of magnitude slower than arithmetic string building. It is the calendar-day equality/ordering key on every hot path: the logsModal sort comparator (logsModal.tsx:56-68, already memoized on [logs, exerciseId], filtered to one exercise's logs, not full history) calls it twice per comparator invocation; `getDailyGoal`/`isGoalHitToday` (progressionFunctions.ts:51-62,74-75) re-filter all logs by it on every set add with no memoization (this full-history re-scan is the H6/H7 trigger, not something this fix removes — H8 only cuts the per-call cost); the Progress tab's per-render weekly transform `getSetsForWeek` (volumeFunctions.tsx:115-142, called via handleGetSetsForWeek) calls it once per log plus once per displayed day; and the nutrition home recomputes `todayEntries` by scanning every entry unmemoized in the render body (nutritionScreen.tsx:26-31). (Note: getVolumeData and getSetsData/handleGetSetsData in the same file are NOT currently consumed by any screen — they are effectively dead code today, so they benefit from this fix only if/when wired up, not as live jank sources.) Because these re-renders fire on every write (H6) and the history is fully hydrated (H7), one "+" tap costs many thousands of Intl calls at large log counts. The cost is JS-side and grows with account age.

Fix: Replace getDateKey's Intl call with arithmetic YYYY-MM-DD building (update its JSDoc to match, preserving the local-timezone rationale); memoize todayEntries in the nutrition home; add equivalence/ordering tests; and — before merging — manually verify on an actual iOS device/simulator (Hermes) that the new arithmetic output is byte-identical to the old `toLocaleDateString('en-CA')` output for representative dates, including a date already persisted in a test account's weight_progress/nutrition_entries rows, since getDateKey's output is written to disk and synced (weight_progress, nutrition_entries.date, settings.birth_date/onboarding_completed_at) and compared against previously-stored keys (e.g. `upsertWeightForDate`'s `WHERE date = ?`, unique on user_id+date).

Files to change:
  - lib/utils/dateHelper.ts:18-27
      Replace `return date.toLocaleDateString("en-CA")` with arithmetic build: `${date.getFullYear()}-${pad(date.getMonth()+1)}-${pad(date.getDate())}`. Update the JSDoc (do NOT leave it verbatim) to describe the arithmetic implementation and explicitly retain the rationale that it reads LOCAL calendar components (getFullYear/getMonth/getDate), not UTC — the exact contract CLAUDE.md flags as deliberate for negative-offset timezones.
  - app/nutritionScreens/nutritionScreen.tsx:26-31
      Wrap the `todayEntries` filter+sort in the already-imported `useMemo`, deps `[nutritionData, selectedDateKey]`, so the full-history scan runs only on data/date change, not every render.
  - lib/utils/__tests__/dateHelper.test.ts
      Add a describe for getDateKey: (a) equivalence — for a spread of dates (incl. single-digit month/day and a leap day) assert getDateKey(d) === d.toLocaleDateString('en-CA'); (b) lexicographic ordering preserved; (c) a DST spring-forward local date (new Date(2024,2,10)) still returns '2024-03-10'. Existing round-trip/single-digit/negative-offset tests must still pass unchanged. Note in the test file/PR description that this equivalence check runs under Jest/Node ICU only, and is supplemented by a required manual on-device (Hermes) verification step before merge, since getDateKey's output is a persisted/synced DB value, not just an in-memory comparison key.

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    getDateKey has ~135 occurrences across ~30 files (verified by direct repo grep excluding docs/graphify-out/.claude artifacts — the banked brief's '161 across 41 files' figure was inflated by counting generated docs/audit files as call sites). Real call sites span every date-bucketing path: workout logs sort/filter (logsModal.tsx, LogHistoryList.tsx), progression goals (progressionFunctions.ts), the Progress tab's live weekly transform (getSetsForWeek in volumeFunctions.tsx — getVolumeData/getSetsData in the same file are currently dead code, not live consumers), the workout graphFunctions.tsx (single call site at line 20, not lines 6/9 as originally cited), nutrition home + DailyIntakeCard + NutritionContext/functions/graphFunctions.tsx, calendars (Calendar.tsx, CalendarMonthGrid.tsx), useToday, notification builders, and the powersyncStore hydrators/writers for weight_progress, nutrition_entries, and settings (birth_date, onboarding_completed_at). Because the change is intended to be output-identical, all in-memory comparison sites are affected only in speed. The nutritionScreen.tsx memo touches only that screen's render (Entry list); no context or shared consumer is involved.
  Edge cases: Local-timezone/DST: arithmetic getFullYear/getMonth/getDate read the same local calendar components Intl uses, so no drift in negative-offset zones (the existing '1990-06-22' regression test still holds). Single-digit month/day: padStart matches en-CA zero-padding. Invalid Date is the only divergence (en-CA -> 'Invalid Date' vs arithmetic -> 'NaN-NaN-NaN'); no valid path passes an invalid Date here. Years are 4-digit so no era/padding difference. Empty history: filters/reduces over [] are unchanged. First render / selectedDate change: the new useMemo recomputes correctly because selectedDateKey is in deps. RESIDUAL RISK (not fully closed by this brief's test suite): getDateKey's output is persisted to disk and synced to Supabase (weight_progress.date — unique on user_id+date per CLAUDE.md Gotcha #1, nutrition_entries.date, settings.birth_date/onboarding_completed_at), and freshly-computed keys are compared against those stored strings (e.g. upsertWeightForDate's `WHERE date = ?`). The equivalence test only proves parity under Jest/Node's ICU, not the shipping Hermes/iOS runtime that originally wrote those persisted keys. en-CA's short-date format is CLDR-standard `yyyy-MM-dd`, so divergence is unlikely, but this must be confirmed on-device before shipping (see fix/tests), not merely asserted from the Node test. If ever found to diverge, a one-time key-format backfill/migration would be required rather than a silent redeploy.
  Preserved:  getDateKey's contract is 'a lexicographically sortable local-calendar-day key'; both implementations are intended to emit identical zero-padded YYYY-MM-DD for all real dates under standard CLDR en-CA formatting, so every equality check, `<`/`>` day comparison, Map bucket key, and sort comparator should produce the same result. This is verified in-memory by the Jest equivalence test and must additionally be confirmed on-device (Hermes) given the persisted/synced nature of the value — see edgeCases. The todayEntries memo returns the same filtered/sorted array it did before; memoization changes when it recomputes, never what it produces.
  Tests:      Run `npx jest lib/utils/__tests__/dateHelper.test.ts` (existing round-trip + negative-offset + DST suites, plus the new equivalence/ordering/DST cases). Run `npx jest context/WorkoutContext/functions/__tests__/volumeFunctions.test.ts context/WorkoutContext/functions/__tests__/graphFunctions.test.ts context/NutritionContext/functions/__tests__/graphFunctions.test.ts` and `components/NutritionComponents/__tests__/DailyIntakeCard.test.tsx` to prove downstream day-bucketing values are unchanged. REQUIRED before merge: manually verify on an actual iOS device/simulator (Hermes) that the new arithmetic build matches `toLocaleDateString('en-CA')` for a spread of dates, including at least one date already persisted in a test account's weight_progress/nutrition_entries rows. Manual: log a set on the logs modal and confirm ordering/highlight-scroll still land on the new row.

H9  (High)  [DONE]  [LAUNCH-BLOCKER]

[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: Item quantity is a multiplier, but persisting it loses precision relative to how the stored entry total was computed — and this SAME mismatch occurs independently for the per-unit item macros too, via the identical two call sites. Verified against actual code:

(a) QUANTITY: `context/NutritionContext/database/powersyncStore.ts:191` and `:232` persist ingredient rows via `sanitizeMacro(item.quantity)`, which rounds to 1 decimal (`lib/utils/number.ts:21-26`, `Math.round(n*10)/10`). `foodDBModal.tsx` (`parseNumericInput`, 'How many servings?') makes 0.25 a real, reachable quantity. The stored ENTRY total is computed by `sumItems` (`context/NutritionContext/functions/items.ts:6-24`) over the RAW quantity in `buildEntryFromItems`/`applyEdits` (`entryBuilders.ts:55,68`) — i.e. macro × 0.25 — but the persisted ingredient row stores 0.3 (+20%).

(b) PER-UNIT MACROS (confirmed by code read, NOT in the original banked brief): the SAME two call sites (`powersyncStore.ts:191`, `:232`) ALSO wrap `item.protein/carbs/fats/calories` in `sanitizeMacro` (1-decimal round), while `sumItems`/`buildEntryFromItems` computed the stored entry total from the RAW per-unit macro × quantity. Real shipped data routinely carries 2 decimals (`lib/foodDB/popularFoods.ts:10` Chicken Breast protein 29.55, `:18` Greek Yogurt 22.79/13.59, `:24` Almonds 0.26) and FatSecret search results are the same shape. At quantity 2, protein 29.55 → stored total 59.1, but the persisted item row stores `sanitizeMacro(29.55)=29.6`; on reload, `rowToNutritionEntry` reads back 29.6, and opening `editEntry` and tapping Save re-runs `applyEdits`→`sumItems(29.6*2)=59.2`, silently rewriting the total with NO user edit and NO fractional quantity involved. This reproduces at whole-number quantities ≥ 2, i.e. the ordinary multi-serving path, not a corner case.

Both (a) and (b) are the same root defect: a value is rounded before storage while the entry total it must reconcile with was computed from the unrounded value. Fixing only (a), as the banked brief did, leaves (b) fully intact and the stated symptom ('no-op Save silently rewrites macros/calories') still fires for the common case of any foodDB/FatSecret item at quantity != 1.

Fix: Fix both instances of the same defect, not just the quantity one:

1. Add `sanitizeQuantity(n: number): number` to `lib/utils/number.ts`: `return Number.isFinite(n) ? n : 1` (precision-preserving; explicit 0 preserved for the 'excluded item' marker at `editEntry.tsx:147`; NaN/Infinity fall back to the neutral multiplier 1, matching `items.ts:3-5`'s 'missing quantity = 1' contract).
2. ALSO add a precision-preserving macro helper with `sanitizeMacro`'s EXISTING non-finite fallback (0, not 1 — a macro amount has no 'neutral' 1): e.g. `sanitizeExactMacro(n: number): number { return Number.isFinite(n) ? n : 0 }`. (Convention-fit: factor both through one shared `preserveFinite(n, fallback)` internal helper if that reads cleanly, to avoid duplicating the `Number.isFinite` check three times — either shape is acceptable, this is not prescriptive.)
3. In `powersyncStore.ts:191` and `:232`: replace `sanitizeMacro(item.quantity)` with `sanitizeQuantity(item.quantity)` AND replace `sanitizeMacro(item.protein)`, `sanitizeMacro(item.carbs)`, `sanitizeMacro(item.fats)`, `sanitizeMacro(item.calories)` with the new exact-macro helper. Update the import on line 6. Both call sites (191 for entries, 232 for saved entries) must change together, for the quantity arg AND all four macro args — a partial change re-creates a divergence between the entry and saved-meal paths.
4. Do NOT touch `sumItems`, `nutritionEntryToRow`/`savedNutritionEntryToRow`'s entry-level total columns, or anywhere else `sanitizeMacro` is used for a genuinely-rounded, displayed total. Only the ingredient-row per-item columns change precision, because (and only because) they are the values `sumItems` re-consumes to reconstruct the total on every subsequent Save — exactly like quantity. The banked brief's claim that per-unit macros are 'genuine macro values [where] rounding them is the intended behavior' is incorrect for these specific columns and is retracted.

Files to change:
  - lib/utils/number.ts
      Add `sanitizeQuantity(n)` (fallback 1, full precision, preserves explicit 0) AND a second exact-precision helper for macros with fallback 0 (e.g. `sanitizeExactMacro`), so item macro columns stop rounding without changing the non-finite-input contract `sanitizeMacro` already has.
  - context/NutritionContext/database/powersyncStore.ts
      Lines 191 and 232 (both ingredient INSERT arg lists): quantity arg -> `sanitizeQuantity(item.quantity)`; protein/carbs/fats/calories args -> the new exact-macro helper. Update the import on line 6. Entry-level row totals (`nutritionEntryToRow`/`savedNutritionEntryToRow`) stay on `sanitizeMacro`, unchanged.
  - lib/utils/__tests__/number.test.ts
      Add `sanitizeQuantity` cases (0.25->0.25, 0.333->0.333, 0->0 preserved, NaN->1, Infinity->1) AND new exact-macro helper cases (29.55->29.55 unchanged, 0->0, NaN->0, Infinity->0 — fallback differs from sanitizeQuantity by design).
  - context/NutritionContext/functions/__tests__/items.test.ts
      REPLACE the originally-proposed reconciliation test (it was circular: comparing sumItems(items) to sumItems(items with only quantity re-sanitized) is an identity for finite values and passes both before and after any fix). New test must model the ACTUAL persist transform on BOTH dimensions together: build an item with quantity 2 (or 0.25) and a 2-decimal per-unit macro (e.g. protein 29.55, mirroring popularFoods.ts); show that applying the OLD transform (sanitizeMacro on both quantity and macros) then re-running sumItems diverges from the entry total sumItems produced on the raw item (proves the bug is real and reachable pre-fix, on the macro axis specifically, not just quantity); then show the NEW transform (sanitizeQuantity + exact-macro helper) reconciles exactly.
  - context/NutritionContext/database/__tests__/powersyncStore.test.ts
      Add a new test that actually exercises the write path this bug lives in (`upsertNutritionEntry` and/or `upsertSavedNutritionEntry`), not just the pure mappers. `powerSync.writeTransaction`/`execute` are already `jest.fn()` in this suite — invoke the transaction callback with a mock `tx` object, capture the bind-params array passed to the ingredient INSERT for an item with quantity 0.25 and protein 29.55, and assert both values are stored at full precision. This closes the gap where no existing or proposed test actually drives the INSERT SQL args at lines 191/232.

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    Unchanged from the banked brief: `upsertNutritionEntry`/`upsertSavedNutritionEntry` are the only writers of ingredient rows, reached via the same CRUD layer behind saveEntry/save-meal flows (editEntry, foodDBModal, savedNutritionModal, AI-analysis save). Widening the diff to also change the macro args at the SAME two call sites does not add new callers or new files beyond what was already being edited.
  Edge cases: All originally-listed edge cases hold (fractional quantity persists exactly, explicit-0 exclusion marker preserved via Number.isFinite not `n||1`, NaN/Infinity->1 for quantity, whole-number quantities round-trip identically). ADDED: per-unit macros with 2+ decimals (the norm for foodDB/FatSecret data, not the exception) now also persist exactly, so quantity>=2 with realistic multi-decimal macros no longer drifts on reload+Save. NaN/Infinity per-unit macros still degrade to 0 (matching sanitizeMacro's existing contract for bad macro data) rather than incorrectly falling back to 1.
  Preserved:  SQLite REAL columns already store arbitrary precision; no schema/migration change. Entry-level (row) totals are still computed by sumItems and still rounded via sanitizeMacro at the entry-row mappers — unaffected. Legacy rows written under the old rounding (both quantity and macro) are not auto-repaired; they simply stop drifting further on subsequent Saves (backfill remains out of scope, as originally noted, now for both dimensions).
  Tests:      `npx jest lib/utils/__tests__/number.test.ts` (sanitizeQuantity + new exact-macro helper cases), `npx jest context/NutritionContext/functions/__tests__/items.test.ts` (revised two-dimension reconciliation test — must fail pre-fix on the macro axis specifically, not just quantity), `npx jest context/NutritionContext/database/__tests__/powersyncStore.test.ts` (existing mapper suite plus new insert-path test asserting raw bind params).

Adjudication decisions:
  - Per-unit item macros (protein/carbs/fats/calories) are ALSO persisted via sanitizeMacro at powersyncStore.ts:191/232, while sumItems/buildEntryFromItems computed the stored entry total from the UNROUNDED per-unit macro x quantity — this is a second, independent, and commonly-reachable source of the exact same items-vs-totals reconciliation bug the brief attributes solely to quantity. Concrete reproductions given (Chicken Breast protein 29.55 at qty 2, Almonds 0.26 at qty 10, generic 10.04/12.34/0.04 examples) were verified directly against lib/foodDB/popularFoods.ts and confirmed present in the actual insert code at powersyncStore.ts:191/232 and entryBuilders.ts:55,68 / items.ts:11-22.
      -> MATERIAL — accepted, correctness gate. Verified by direct code read: powersyncStore.ts:191/232 do wrap item.protein/carbs/fats/calories in sanitizeMacro, and buildEntryFromItems/applyEdits compute the stored total from the raw (pre-round) items via sumItems. The banked brief's claim that 'rounding [per-unit macros] is the intended behavior; only the multiplier was wrong' and that the fix makes Save 'a true no-op' is factually false for the ordinary quantity>=2 + multi-decimal-macro case (the norm for foodDB/FatSecret data). Rule 1 (correctness, HARD GATE) forces extending the fix beyond what the banked brief scoped: added a second precision-preserving helper for item macros (fallback 0, matching sanitizeMacro's existing non-finite contract) applied at the same two call sites, alongside sanitizeQuantity. This extension was not explicitly proposed by any reviewer (they identified the gap but not a fix), so per instructions I note explicitly that I am going beyond what was raised because rule 1 requires it.  (Rule 1 (correctness, hard gate))
  - The proposed items.test.ts reconciliation test (sumItems(items) vs sumItems(items with only quantity mapped through sanitizeQuantity)) is circular/trivial: sanitizeQuantity is an identity for finite inputs, so both sides are always equal, before and after any fix — it does not exercise sanitizeMacro on the quantity or macro columns and cannot fail today as claimed, giving false confidence that reconciliation is restored.
      -> MATERIAL — accepted. Verified: sanitizeQuantity(n)=n for any finite n, so the described assertion is a mathematical identity regardless of the fix; it never invokes the actual buggy sanitizeMacro call at powersyncStore.ts:191. Replaced with a test that applies the OLD sanitizeMacro-based transform to BOTH quantity and per-unit macros (showing pre-fix divergence on the macro axis specifically, using realistic 2-decimal values) and the NEW transform (showing post-fix reconciliation on both axes).  (Rule 5 (testability) — a regression test that cannot fail pre-fix provides no protection and would ship false confidence on a launch-blocker issue)
  - No proposed test actually drives the INSERT path (upsertNutritionEntry/upsertSavedNutritionEntry) with a fractional quantity and reads back the stored value; the plan only re-runs the existing powersyncStore.test.ts, which exclusively covers the pure mappers (nutritionEntryToRow/rowToNutritionEntry) and never touches the write transaction at lines 191/232 where the bug actually lives.
      -> MATERIAL — accepted. Confirmed by reading context/NutritionContext/database/__tests__/powersyncStore.test.ts: it only imports and tests nutritionEntryToRow/rowToNutritionEntry/savedNutritionEntryToRow/rowToSavedNutritionEntry, never upsertNutritionEntry/upsertSavedNutritionEntry. Since powerSync.writeTransaction/execute are already jest.fn() mocks in that suite, added a new test that invokes the transaction callback with a mock tx, captures the bind-params for the ingredient INSERT, and asserts full precision is stored for a 0.25-quantity / 29.55-protein item — directly closing the gap.  (Rule 5 (testability) / Rule 1 (correctness must be verified, not merely asserted) — the fix's actual effect at the cited line numbers was otherwise unverified by any test in the plan)

H10  (High)  [MERGED]
===============================================================================
[Consolidated multi-agent brief (not yet adjudicated)]

Root cause: The "normalize today → bucket values by dateKey while tracking earliestDate/hasData → calculateStartDate → walk oldest→newest for daysBetween(start,today)+1 days, gap-filling each day → push {day: formatDateMinimal(key), value}" scaffolding was copy-pasted into 4 graph builders instead of being factored out once: getMacroDataForGraph (context/NutritionContext/functions/graphFunctions.tsx:24-82), getVolumeData (context/WorkoutContext/functions/volumeFunctions.tsx:9-57), getSetsData (same file:64-107), getBodyWeightProgressData (context/SettingsContext/functions/bodyWeightFunctions.tsx:74-122). Verified against the current source: all four call the shared calculateStartDate/daysBetween and re-implement an identical loop, and only the per-day aggregation legitimately differs. Because the walk itself was hand-duplicated rather than shared, per-metric details drifted: getVolumeData rounds (`Math.round(volume)`, volumeFunctions.tsx:52) while macros/sets/body-weight don't; body-weight parses date keys by hand (`new Date(dateKey + 'T00:00:00')`, bodyWeightFunctions.tsx:85) instead of the existing `parseDateKey` helper (lib/utils/dateHelper.ts:37-40); and body-weight seeds `lastKnownWeight = 0` (bodyWeightFunctions.tsx:101) instead of the earliest recorded weight. That last drift is a live, user-visible correctness bug, not just cosmetic duplication: whenever the window (calculateStartDate) starts before the first real weigh-in — the normal case, since the window is anchored to onboardingCompletedAt or the earliest of a 365-day cap while the first weight_progress row is usually written days later — every day before that weigh-in is emitted as value 0. That leading 0 becomes `values[0]` in GraphStats.computeStats (components/GraphComponents/GraphStats.tsx:36-38: `first = values[0] ?? 0; change = last - first`), so the body-weight "Change" stat reports the user's entire body weight instead of the true delta, and the 0-floor crushes the chart's y-scale. Three of the four existing bodyWeightFunctions.test.ts assertions currently codify this as expected behavior (result[0].value === 0 at lines 123, 136-138, 163, 268), i.e. the bug is pinned by the test suite rather than caught by it. One draft additionally flagged a possible off-by-one in calculateStartDate's default/cap branch (dateHelper.ts:244-246: `startDate.setDate(today - maxDays)` then callers do `daysBetween(start,today)+1`, yielding maxDays+1 points, confirmed live by bodyWeightFunctions.test.ts:257-271 asserting length 366 for a 365-day cap) — this is real but is NOT corroborated by the other three independent passes, changes the point-count of all four graphs simultaneously (broader blast radius than H10's stated duplication/leading-zero story), and its "correct" semantics (is "last N days" inclusive of today making N+1, or exactly N?) is a product-intent call, not a pure bug fix. It is called out below as a separate, out-of-scope finding for the coordinator rather than folded into this merge.

Fix: Consolidate the duplicated walk into one shared helper and, in the same pass, fix body-weight's leading-zero seed and hand-rolled key parse — these are not separable edits, because migrating bodyWeightFunctions.tsx onto the shared helper IS the natural place to supply the correct carry-forward seed (the alternative, patching the bug in place and then re-diffing it into the helper migration later, is strictly more work). Add `buildDailySeries(valuesByDate, opts)` to a new `lib/utils/graphSeries.ts`, built purely from existing dateHelper exports (`calculateStartDate`, `daysBetween`, `getDateKey`, `parseDateKey`, `formatDateMinimal` — no changes to dateHelper.ts itself). It owns: today-normalization, the calculateStartDate call, `daysToShow` computation, the oldest→newest walk, fill strategy (`'zero'` default vs `'carryForward'`), an optional numeric `seed` for the carry-forward start value (default 0), and an optional `round` flag. Migrate all four builders to delegate to it, keeping their aggregation loops untouched and threading each one's existing per-metric behavior through options (volume: `round:true`; macros/sets: no round; body-weight: `fill:'carryForward'`, `maxDays:365`, and — the correctness fix — `seed` = the earliest recorded weight captured during the existing key scan, not 0). Update the 5 existing bodyWeightFunctions.test.ts assertions that currently pin the 0-seed bug to expect the seeded first weight, add one new regression test for the leading-gap/Change-stat case, and add a new graphSeries.test.ts pinning the shared contract directly. Re-run graphFunctions.test.ts and volumeFunctions.test.ts UNCHANGED as the equivalence proof that the extraction is otherwise behavior-preserving.

Files to change:
  - lib/utils/graphSeries.ts
      NEW. Export buildDailySeries(valuesByDate: Map<string, number>, opts: { maxDays: number; onboardingCompletedAt?: Date; earliestDate: Date | null; hasData: boolean; fill?: 'zero' | 'carryForward'; seed?: number; round?: boolean }): Array<{day:string; value:number}>. Implements the single shared walk: normalize today, call calculateStartDate(today, maxDays, onboardingCompletedAt, earliestDate, hasData), daysToShow = daysBetween(start, today) + 1, walk oldest→newest via getDateKey, gap-fill per `fill` (zero, or carryForward starting from `seed ?? 0`), apply Math.round when `round` is true, label via formatDateMinimal. Depends only on existing lib/utils/dateHelper exports (calculateStartDate, daysBetween, getDateKey, parseDateKey, formatDateMinimal) — dateHelper.ts itself is NOT modified. Add the required one-line comment above the function per project convention.
  - context/SettingsContext/functions/bodyWeightFunctions.tsx
      getBodyWeightProgressData (lines 74-122). In the existing key-scan loop (83-90), also capture earliestWeight = bwProgress[key] whenever a new earliest date is found (scanning ALL of bwProgress, including entries older than the 365-day display window — that entry is still the true last-known value at window start). Replace the hand-rolled `new Date(dateKey + 'T00:00:00')` (line 85) with `parseDateKey(dateKey)`, adding `parseDateKey` to the dateHelper import (line 1). Build a `Map<string, number>` from `Object.entries(bwProgress)` and replace the calculateStartDate/daysToShow/walk block (93-119) with `buildDailySeries(map, { maxDays: 365, onboardingCompletedAt, earliestDate, hasData, fill: 'carryForward', seed: earliestWeight ?? 0 })`. This is the correctness fix: leading days before the first weigh-in now carry the earliest known weight instead of a fake 0. Leave computeBwUpdate/isGoalReached/withRegeneratedTargets/goalReachedBannerCopy untouched.
  - context/NutritionContext/functions/graphFunctions.tsx
      getMacroDataForGraph (lines 24-82): keep the macrosByDate aggregation and earliestDate tracking (28-49); replace the calculateStartDate/daysToShow/walk block (51-79) with buildDailySeries(perMacroMap, { maxDays: 30, onboardingCompletedAt, earliestDate, hasData: nutritionData.length > 0, fill: 'zero' }), where perMacroMap is macrosByDate reduced through the existing macroExtractor for the requested macroType.   - context/SettingsContext/functions/__tests__/bodyWeightFunctions.test.ts
      Update the 4 assertions that currently codify the leading-zero bug to expect the seeded first/earliest weigh-in instead of 0: line 123 ('should start from onboarding date...', bwProgress={daysAgo(10):180.0}, onboarding=daysAgo(15)) → expect 180.0; lines 136 and 138 ('should carry forward last known weight...', onboarding=daysAgo(5), bwProgress={daysAgo(3):180.0}) → both expect 180.0 (days 5 and 4 ago now carry the eventual first weigh-in backward); line 163 ('Onboarding more than 30 days ago (within a year)', bwProgress={daysAgo(35):180.0, daysAgo(10):182.0}, onboarding=daysAgo(45)) → expect 180.0; line 268 ('should clamp to the last 365 days...', bwProgress={daysAgo(400):175.0, daysAgo(10):182.0}) → expect 175.0 (the 400-day-old entry is outside the display window but is still the true last-known value at window start). Do NOT change lines 225/235 ('should return single day when no data exists' / empty bwProgress) — those have no data at all, so seed correctly stays 0. Add a new regression test 'body-weight series never leads with 0 when a weigh-in exists inside the window' (onboarding predates the first weigh-in; assert result[0].value === the first weigh-in, and that the Change stat i.e. last-first no longer equals the user's raw body weight) — fails before the fix, passes after.

This is the regression net that keeps the 4 call sites from drifting again.

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    buildDailySeries has exactly 4 producers, all verified: NutritionContext/index.tsx:140 (getMacroDataForGraph, feeds progress.tsx macro graphs), WorkoutContext/index.tsx:388 (getSetsData) plus getVolumeData (verified dead — imported only by its own test file, no index.tsx wiring), SettingsContext/index.tsx:122 (handleGetBodyWeightProgressData, feeds progress.tsx body-weight chart + components/devTest/LineChartTest.tsx dev preview). All four public function signatures are unchanged, so every wrapper and downstream consumer of the returned {day,value}[] — progress.tsx, components/GraphComponents/Graph1.tsx, and GraphStats.tsx (computeStats: first=values[0], change=last-first, loggedAvg filtering value>0) — is unaffected by the structural extraction itself. The only intentional output CHANGE is body-weight's pre-first-weigh-in days (0 → seeded earliest weight); every other caller/consumer receives byte-identical arrays.
  Edge cases: Empty input (hasData:false) → calculateStartDate returns today → single point with value = seed ?? 0 (matches current 'no data' behavior for all 4 metrics, verified at bodyWeightFunctions.test.ts:220-226/231-236 and volumeFunctions.test.ts:301-308). Onboarding/earliest date in the future → clamped to today by calculateStartDate's existing guards (dateHelper.ts:216,228), unchanged. Body weight WITHOUT onboardingCompletedAt: startDate already equals earliestDate, so result[0] is already the first weigh-in and the seed fix is a no-op there (bodyWeightFunctions.test.ts:187-199). Interior gaps after the first weigh-in continue to carry forward exactly as before (test lines 140-146, 249-254) — the seed only changes LEADING days, never interior/trailing ones. Entries older than the display window still count toward `earliestWeight` even though they're outside the visible range (test 257-271: the 400-day-old 175.0 entry seeds the window even though it's not itself displayed) — this is the correct 'last known truth' semantics, not a new bug. DST: daysBetween is already DST-safe (dateHelper.ts:144-148, rounds the ms diff); parseDateKey and the old `new Date(key+'T00:00:00')` both yield local midnight for a valid YYYY-MM-DD key, so the parse-method swap is behavior-identical. volume/sets skip predicates (weight/reps<=0) stay in each caller's own aggregation loop, not in the shared helper. The calculateStartDate off-by-one (maxDays+1 points on the default/cap branch, confirmed at dateHelper.ts:244-246 and pinned by volumeFunctions.test.ts:247/295 and bodyWeightFunctions.test.ts:266) is explicitly NOT touched by this merge — flagged separately below.
  Preserved:  The walk is a literal, byte-identical extraction: same calculateStartDate call, same daysToShow formula, same oldest→newest loop, same formatDateMinimal labels — so array length and x-axis windowing are unchanged for macros/volume/sets, and unchanged for body-weight EXCEPT the explicitly-intended leading-value fix. Per-metric differences are preserved as explicit options rather than flattened: volume keeps round:true, macros/sets/body-weight keep no-round; body-weight alone keeps fill:'carryForward' + maxDays:365, everyone else keeps fill:'zero' + maxDays:30. Week-variant functions (getMacroForWeek, getSetsForWeek) and non-graph functions (computeBwUpdate, isGoalReached, withRegeneratedTargets, getNutritionStreakState) are explicitly out of scope and untouched. graphFunctions.test.ts and volumeFunctions.test.ts must pass UNCHANGED post-migration — that green run is the proof the consolidation itself is behavior-preserving, isolated from the one intentional body-weight correctness change.

H11  (High)  [MERGED]  [LAUNCH-BLOCKER]
===============================================================================
[Consolidated multi-agent brief (not yet adjudicated)]

Root cause: Expo Router registers every file under `app/` as a navigable, deep-linkable route. `app/nutritionScreens/nutritionScreen.tsx` and `app/workoutScreens/workoutScreen.tsx` are not standalone screens — they are the two body components embedded by `app/(tabs)/index.tsx:1-2` inside the home tab (with ModeSwitcher + Fab). Because they live under `app/` but are NOT declared as `<Stack.Screen>` inside any `<Stack.Protected>` group in `app/_layout.tsx:159-236`, Expo Router still auto-registers them as routes `/nutritionScreens/nutritionScreen` and `/workoutScreens/workoutScreen`, and Stack.Protected guards only govern *declared* screens — so these two are reachable via deep link with no auth/onboarding/paywall gate, exposing the core logging UI (and bypassing the paywall gate that normally sits in front of `(tabs)`). Separately, the onboarding guard at `_layout.tsx:162` reads `guard={!settings.onboardingComplete}` and omits the session check the sibling `(tabs)` group has (`_layout.tsx:175` uses `!!session && ...`), so onboarding screens register even when `session` is null (settings defaults `onboardingComplete` falsey), making `/onboardingScreens/goals` deep-linkable pre-auth.

Fix: See rootCause/fix fields.

Files to change:
  - app/workoutScreens/workoutScreen.tsx
      Move file to components/WorkoutComponents/WorkoutScreen.tsx (removes it from the app/ route tree; internal @/ imports unchanged).
  - app/nutritionScreens/nutritionScreen.tsx
      Move file to components/NutritionComponents/NutritionScreen.tsx (removes it from the app/ route tree; internal @/ imports unchanged).
  - app/(tabs)/index.tsx
      Lines 1-2: update imports to '@/components/NutritionComponents/NutritionScreen' and '@/components/WorkoutComponents/WorkoutScreen'.
  - app/_layout.tsx
      Line 162: change onboarding guard to guard={!!session && !settings.onboardingComplete} so onboarding screens require an authenticated session.
  - app/__tests__/routeManifest.test.ts
      New test: enumerate every route file under app/ and assert each (excluding _layout/index/group dirs) is declared as a <Stack.Screen name=...> in app/_layout.tsx — fails today on the two undeclared bodies, passes after the move.

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    The two moved files are imported only by app/(tabs)/index.tsx:1-2 (verified via grep across **/*.{ts,tsx}: no other importer). Both files consume only context hooks and helpers via the @/ alias (useWorkout, useAuth, DraggableList, Log / useNutrition, DailyIntakeCard, Entry, dateHelper, entryRouting), so relocation needs no in-file path edits and no provider reshuffling — they still render inside the same provider stack via index.tsx. The _layout guard change touches only which screens register in the onboarding branch; the `session` value is already destructured at _layout.tsx:117.
  Edge cases: Pre-auth deep link (`/workoutScreens/workoutScreen`, `/nutritionScreens/nutritionScreen`, `/onboardingScreens/goals`): after the fix the first two routes no longer exist (Expo Router 404 -> falls back to the anchored `(tabs)` route, itself gated to login/onboarding), and onboarding routes only register with a session. First-run/no-session: settings defaults leave onboardingComplete falsey, so the added `!!session` prevents onboarding screens registering before login. Cold deep-link before contexts load: StackLayout still blocks on AppLoadingScreen (line 133) until allContextsLoaded, unchanged. No offline/DST/concurrency surface.
  Preserved:  index.tsx renders WorkoutScreen/NutritionScreen exactly as before — same components, same props (none), same position under ModeSwitcher/Fab inside the protected (tabs) group; only their file location and two import strings change. Onboarding still shows whenever a signed-in user hasn't completed onboarding (the common case), because a signed-in pre-onboarding user has a session so `!!session` is true — behavior for real users is identical; only the pre-auth deep-link hole closes. No runtime logic inside either moved component is altered.
  Tests:      Add app/__tests__/routeManifest.test.ts asserting no orphan (undeclared) route files exist under app/ outside the declared Stack — this fails on `nutritionScreen`/`workoutScreen` before the move and passes after. Run existing suite (`npm run test:ci`) to confirm no import resolves to the old paths. Manual App-Review-facing verification: attempt deep links to `/workoutScreens/workoutScreen`, `/nutritionScreens/nutritionScreen`, and `/onboardingScreens/goals` while signed out and confirm each redirects to login rather than rendering content.
