H12  (High)  [DONE]  [LAUNCH-BLOCKER]
===============================================================================
[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: Icon-only interactive controls on the core logging flows were built with bare Pressable/TouchableOpacity wrapping a lucide/Ionicons glyph and no independently-reachable text, so VoiceOver has nothing meaningful to announce. The team already knows the correct pattern (cameraScreen.tsx, progress.tsx pass accessibilityRole+accessibilityLabel+accessibilityState) but applied it unevenly: the FAB, mode switcher, entry/log edit pencils, set-delete trash, archive restore/delete, staged-item remove, camera flash toggle, and loading-screen retry link ship unlabeled or under-labeled. Two additional real gaps, missed by the original audit and confirmed during review, must be folded in: exerciseScreen.tsx's Fab action buttons and SavedEntry.tsx's add/delete buttons are the same icon-only pattern on the same core flows. A second, distinct defect class was found in review: Log.tsx nests the edit-pencil TouchableOpacity inside an outer TouchableOpacity that iOS VoiceOver treats as accessible-by-default, so simply labeling both (as originally planned) would collapse the pencil into the outer node and leave the pencil unreachable to screen readers — the fix must explicitly break that grouping.

Fix: Add accessibilityRole/accessibilityLabel/accessibilityState to each unlabeled control, reusing the cameraScreen.tsx/progress.tsx pattern, with three corrections from the original plan: (1) Log.tsx requires an accessibility-tree restructuring, not just two independent labels, to keep the pencil reachable; (2) controls that already have a visible Text child (Entry breakdown footer, AppLoadingScreen retry) get accessibilityRole only — do not override their working accessible name with a new label; (3) labels describing pencils that open a multi-option Alert (Entry, Log) must say 'Options for X', not 'Edit X'. Per-control semantics:
1. Fab.tsx:93 (toggle) — accessibilityRole="button", accessibilityLabel={isOpen ? 'Close actions menu' : 'Open actions menu'}, accessibilityState={{expanded:isOpen}}. Wrapping Animated.View (77-89) — accessibilityElementsHidden={!isOpen} + importantForAccessibility={isOpen?'auto':'no-hide-descendants'}.
2. index.tsx:28,33,55,60,65,70 — accessibilityRole="button" + labels 'Add workout','View archived workouts','Add meal','Scan food with camera','Search food database','Saved meals'.
3. ModeSwitcher.tsx:30,39 — accessibilityRole="button", accessibilityLabel={isLift?'Workout mode':'Nutrition mode'}, accessibilityState={{selected:isActive}}.
4. Entry.tsx:43 pencil — accessibilityRole="button" + accessibilityLabel={`Options for ${name}`} (corrected from 'Edit', since onEditPress opens an Edit/Save/Delete Alert). Entry.tsx:66 breakdown footer — accessibilityRole="button" ONLY; do not set/override accessibilityLabel (existing visible Text 'Tap to see the breakdown' already composes a correct accessible name).
5. Log.tsx — outer TouchableOpacity (line 25): add accessible={false} (prevents VoiceOver from collapsing the row into one opaque node; does not change touch/press behavior). The existing inner 'content' View (lines 40-49, wraps text+subtitle, no structural change needed) gets accessible={true}, accessibilityRole="button", accessibilityLabel={subtitle ? `${text}, ${subtitle}` : text} — no accessibilityHint (the originally-proposed 'long press for options' is factually wrong: onMenuPress is wired to `drag`, a reorder gesture, in both consumers, not an options menu; making drag-reorder itself VoiceOver-operable is a separate, larger a11y gap, out of scope here). Log.tsx:52 pencil — accessibilityRole="button" + accessibilityLabel={`Options for ${text}`} (now independently focusable since the outer subtree is no longer collapsed, and reflects the Rename/Duplicate/Archive/Delete Alert it actually opens).
6. LogHistoryList.tsx:64 (delete trash, not nested in an accessible parent — no grouping issue) — accessibilityRole="button" + accessibilityLabel={`Delete set: ${item.weight} ${weightUnit} for ${item.reps} reps`}.
7. archiveModal.tsx:63/:68 — role + labels using item.name.
8. AppLoadingScreen.tsx:95 retry — accessibilityRole="button" ONLY; do not override the existing visible 'Tap to retry' Text with a new label.
9. cameraScreen.tsx:208 flash toggle — role + 'Toggle camera flash' + accessibilityState={{selected:flashEnabled}}.
10. editEntry.tsx:163 remove trash + :181/:193 steppers — role + labels, unchanged from original plan.
11. NEW — app/workoutScreens/exerciseScreen.tsx:100,103,106 (Fab's three action buttons on the exercise-drilldown flow, a real consumer the original blast radius incorrectly excluded) — accessibilityRole="button" + labels 'Add exercise', 'View archived exercises', 'Add notes'.
12. NEW — components/NutritionComponents/SavedEntry.tsx:62 (Plus) and :68 (Trash2), feeding the 'Saved meals' FAB flow this issue already targets — accessibilityRole="button" + accessibilityLabel={`Add ${name}`} / {`Delete saved meal: ${name}`}.
Explicitly NOT touched: settings.tsx theme toggle, camera scan-kind/capture/flip/library (already labeled).

devHubPlan (amended):
- Components under test: FloatingActionMenu (Fab.tsx) + action buttons (home AND exercise-screen variants), ModeSwitcher, Entry, Log/LogHistoryList, archiveModal, AppLoadingScreen retry, editEntry rows, cameraScreen flash, SavedEntry.
- Test pages: reuse existing (entryRow, logHistory, loadingRetry, editEntry). NEW home-chrome harness: components/devTest/HomeChromeTest.tsx + app/devTest/homeChrome.tsx route stub, registered as a new <Stack.Screen name="devTest/homeChrome"> inside the EXISTING app/_layout.tsx devTest block (there is no separate app/devTest/_layout.tsx — that file does not exist; all devTest routes live in the single root layout), plus a GROUPS entry in DevHub.tsx.
- Scenarios (light+dark, VoiceOver on): (a) FAB collapsed/expanded label+expanded-state announcements; (b) ModeSwitcher selected-state; (c) Entry pencil announces 'Options for <name>, button'; (d) Log row announces '<name>, <subtitle>, button' and the pencil is INDEPENDENTLY focusable and announces 'Options for <name>, button' (this is the corrected, previously-broken behavior — verify explicitly that the pencil is reachable, not swallowed by the row); (e) set-delete trash full sentence; (f) retry link announces as a button using its existing visible text; (g) exerciseScreen Fab buttons and SavedEntry add/delete buttons announce correctly.
- Expected result: meaningful label + correct role for every control; Log's pencil is confirmed independently reachable (not grouped into the row); no hint claiming long-press opens an options menu; no visual change in either scheme. Drop the '>=44pt target' assertion — actual hit areas (~40pt Entry/Log pencils, ~36pt LogHistoryList delete) are unchanged and below 44pt; this is a pre-existing, separate touch-target-sizing issue, not remediated by this fix, and should be filed as its own follow-up (parallel to how H13 covers CTA contrast separately).
- devHubApproval: pending (human flips after VoiceOver walkthrough).

(Submitted 3x with identical substance.)
      -> MATERIAL. Confirmed by direct file read — app/devTest/_layout.tsx does not exist; ls of app/devTest/ shows only route-stub .tsx files, no _layout.tsx. Amended: files entry 'app/devTest/_layout.tsx' is replaced with 'app/_layout.tsx' — add one new <Stack.Screen name="devTest/homeChrome" .../> line following the existing pattern (e.g. near the other headerShown:true entries, title 'Home Chrome — a11y (H12)').  (Rule 1 (correctness, hard gate) — a plan step that can't execute as written fails to deliver the stated devHub verification path.)
  - Log.tsx: the brief adds accessibilityRole/accessibilityLabel independently to BOTH the outer row TouchableOpacity (line 25) and the nested edit-pencil TouchableOpacity (line 52), expecting VoiceOver to focus each separately. On iOS, an accessible touchable (accessible defaults true) collapses its whole subtree into one VoiceOver node, so the nested pencil's label is inert and the 'only edit path' stays unreachable to a screen reader. Contrast with Entry.tsx, whose outer wrapper is a plain View, so its pencil stays independently focusable. (Submitted ~9x with equivalent substance across different citation lines.)
      -> MATERIAL — verified against actual Log.tsx (outer <TouchableOpacity onPress={onPress} onLongPress={onMenuPress}> at line 25 wraps the entire row including the pencil <TouchableOpacity> at line 52) and Entry.tsx (outer is a plain <View>, confirming the asymmetry). This is a real, well-documented RN/iOS behavior, and it defeats the brief's own explicitly stated goal for Log ('the only edit path'). This is a correctness-gate failure for the Log.tsx portion of the fix specifically. AMENDED fix for Log.tsx: (1) outer TouchableOpacity (line 25) gets accessible={false} instead of a role/label (stops it from swallowing descendants — touch/press behavior for sighted/VoiceOver-synthetic-tap users is unaffected since accessible only controls VO grouping, not gesture dispatch); (2) the existing 'content' View (lines 40-49, already wraps text+subtitle, no restructuring needed) gets accessible={true}, accessibilityRole="button", accessibilityLabel={subtitle ? `${text}, ${subtitle}` : text} — this also folds in the 'subtitle gets dropped' finding below by explicitly composing subtitle into the label; (3) the pencil (line 52) keeps its own independent role + label (now genuinely reachable since the outer subtree is no longer collapsed).  (Rule 1 (correctness, hard gate) forces the amendment; the fix chosen (accessible={false} + labeling the existing 'content' View) is the smallest change that achieves it, satisfying rule 4 (least code) without any visual/layout change, satisfying rule 2 (safety/behavior preserved).)
  - The proposed accessibilityHint 'Double tap to open, long press for options' misdescribes the long-press: onMenuPress is wired to `drag` (DraggableList reorder) in both workoutScreen.tsx:36 and exerciseScreen.tsx:84, not an options menu — the real options Alert is behind the edit pencil (handleEdit). Telling a VoiceOver user 'long press for options' is factually wrong. (Submitted 3x, including the missed-second-consumer variant.)
      -> MATERIAL — verified: workoutScreen.tsx:36 `onMenuPress={drag}`, handleEdit (39-68) is a separate Alert with Rename/Duplicate/Archive/Delete, confirming onMenuPress is reorder-drag, not an options menu. A misleading hint is worse than none. AMENDED: drop the accessibilityHint entirely from the Log content label (no hint text describing long-press). Making drag-to-reorder itself VoiceOver-operable (custom accessibilityActions for reorder) is a real, separate, materially larger a11y gap — noted explicitly as an out-of-scope follow-up, mirroring the brief's own H13 carve-out, not folded into this fix.  (Rule 1 (correctness) — an inaccurate hint is a defect, not a nitpick; rule 4 (least code) — removing the false claim is simpler than trying to make an inherently-timed gesture VoiceOver-operable inside this issue's scope.)
  - Setting accessibilityLabel={text} on the outer Log row overrides the default children-composed announcement, silently dropping the subtitle (e.g. exercise count / muscle group) that VoiceOver currently reads. (Submitted 2x.)
      -> MATERIAL — confirmed Log.tsx renders subtitle as a sibling Text (lines 44-48) inside the same row; an explicit accessibilityLabel on an ancestor does override the auto-composed announcement. Resolved by the amendment above: label is now `${text}, ${subtitle}` when subtitle is present, so no information is lost.  (Rule 2 (safety — no regression in existing behavior, here 'existing behavior' = what's currently announced to AT users).)
  - Entry.tsx breakdown footer (line 66-69) already renders a visible Text child ('Tap to see the breakdown'), which VoiceOver already announces; the brief's plan to add accessibilityLabel='See item breakdown' replaces that string with one that doesn't contain the visible text, breaking voice-control label-in-name matching (WCAG 2.5.3). The rootCause's blanket 'ships unlabeled' claim is also inaccurate for this control (and, by the same logic, for AppLoadingScreen's retry link, which also has a visible Text child). H13  (High)  [DONE]  [LAUNCH-BLOCKER]

[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: The dark palette deliberately reuses the neon brand accents as the solid/gradient CTA fill. context/ThemeContext/colors.ts:87-97 documents white text/icons on nutritionGradient (top stop #00BD48) running only ~2.2-3.5:1 (below the 3:1 large-text floor) as an intentional 'vibrancy override.' This affects any user whose resolved color scheme is dark — whether via an explicit 'dark' preference, the default 'system' preference following an OS set to dark, or (rarely) the unresolved-OS-scheme fallback (`resolveColorScheme` returns `osScheme ?? 'dark'`, colors.ts:123-125; defaultThemePreference='system', line 105). Dark is NOT unconditionally the default for all users — correcting the banked brief's overstatement — but it remains an extremely common resolved scheme on iOS, so this stays launch-blocking. Every nutrition-mode primary CTA that routes through the nutritionGradient token via LinearGradient renders white content on this failing fill: 'Use Photo' (cameraScreen.tsx:176), 'Add meal/generate' (addNutritionModal.tsx:115,180), FAB actions (Fab.tsx:94, and the FAB circle buttons directly in app/(tabs)/index.tsx:85,101), the mode switcher (ModeSwitcher.tsx:18,31), 'Add N Items'/'Save changes' in foodDBModal/savedNutritionModal/editEntry, and DateSheet's Confirm button (via DatePickerPopup's accentGradient prop). The workout-blue fill (#2f80ed, ~3.9:1) clears the 3:1 large-text floor for most of these CTAs and is not the primary blocker — EXCEPT verified instances below where it fails too because the text is smaller than 'large text.' Root cause is therefore mostly token-level (one over-bright dark CTA fill) plus three additional call sites that bypass the token entirely by using the raw solid `colors.nutrition`/`colors.workout` accent with forced white text: cameraScreen.tsx:304, ScanBackdrop.tsx:102 (identical control, shown to every non-premium/first-run/permission-denied user), and app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:192 (range chips, where the small 13px text means BOTH nutrition and workout modes fail contrast).

Fix: 1. In context/ThemeContext/colors.ts, deepen ONLY the dark-scheme nutritionGradient (line 97) and workoutGradient (line 96 — now REQUIRED, not optional, because of the progress.tsx chip finding below) so white text/icons clear the 3:1 large-text floor with margin (target >=3.5:1 for green; verify the blue deepening covers the smaller-text progress.tsx chip too, target >=4.5:1 there). Anchor green to light's proven family (e.g. #0E7A38-#158440 range), keep the 143deg hue. Do NOT touch the neon nutrition/workout/nutritionInk/workoutInk tokens themselves — those stay vibrant for accents/tints.
2. Add a small carve-out pair, e.g. nutritionRingGradient/workoutRingGradient (or equivalent), holding the CURRENT pre-fix neon stops, and repoint ProgressWheel.tsx:46 to it instead of nutritionGradient/workoutGradient. This is required because ProgressWheel builds its ring directly from the token being deepened, and the brief's own design goal ('charts, ProgressWheel... must stay vibrant') would otherwise be silently violated on a core home-screen surface.
3. Repoint THREE solid white-on-neon controls (not one) to the deepened fill: (a) app/nutritionScreens/cameraScreen.tsx:304 modeButtonActive; (b) components/NutritionComponents/ScanBackdrop.tsx:~101-102 modeButtonActive — identical control shown to every non-premium/first-run/permission-denied user, must be kept in sync with (a) per the file's own 'keep in sync' comment; (c) app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:192 selected range chip, in BOTH the nutrition and workout mode branches (line 101 `accent`), since its 13px text means workout-blue's usual 3:1 exemption does not apply here.
4. Update the palette comment block (colors.ts:3-8,87-99) so it no longer sanctions a sub-3:1 CTA fill, and no longer implies the workout-blue exemption is universal (it is per-instance, contingent on text size).
5. Do not claim 'no other call site needs editing' — the token-deepen still auto-fixes the ~15+ gradient-consuming CTAs with zero per-site edits (Fab.tsx, ModeSwitcher, addNutritionModal, foodDBModal, savedNutritionModal, editEntry, DateSheet's Confirm button, home FAB circles, etc.), but the three solid-color controls above require manual repoints, and ProgressWheel requires the ring-token carve-out to avoid regressing.

Root cause: Verified against the real code (renameModal.tsx, addWorkoutModal.tsx, editEntry.tsx, foodDBModal.tsx, savedNutritionModal.tsx, items.ts, entryBuilders.ts, useSubmitOnce.ts). The "add/edit" modal twins were hardened one copy at a time, so protections landed unevenly: (1) addWorkoutModal.tsx (lines 20, 34-37, 77) wraps its commit in `useSubmitOnce` and disables its CTA while `submitting`; its ~95%-identical twin renameModal.tsx does NOT — `handleRename` (lines 32-44) commits `handleRenameWorkout(workoutId, trimmed); router.back()` (lines 42-43) with no guard, and the Rename button (line 83) is `disabled={!isValid}` only. A double-tap during the modal's ~300ms close animation — the exact window `useSubmitOnce`'s header doc describes — fires the rename twice and calls `router.back()` twice, popping an extra screen. (2) editEntry.tsx's `handleSave` (lines 119-122) is likewise unguarded, and its Save button (line 245, `<TouchableOpacity onPress={handleSave} activeOpacity={0.85} style={styles.saveWrap}>`) has no `disabled` prop at all — same double-write/double-pop risk. (3) editEntry.tsx:71 does a bare `const raw = JSON.parse(entryStr)` with no try/catch — a malformed/deep-linked `entry` route param throws during render (red-screen crash); the file already has a working pattern for the empty-param case immediately above it (lines 66-69: `if (!entryStr) { router.back(); return null }`), it just isn't extended to cover invalid JSON. (4) The staged macro pills in foodDBModal.tsx (macroPill block, lines ~271-283: `Math.round(item.calories*q)`, `Math.round(item.fats*q*10)/10`, etc.) and savedNutritionModal.tsx (macroPill block, lines ~142-155: same shape using `s.calories`, `s.fats`, etc.) hand-roll per-row scaled item math instead of routing through `sumItems` in `context/NutritionContext/functions/items.ts` (verified: the file's own header comment calls it the "single owner of item math," rounds calories to an integer and macros to 1 decimal — identical to the hand-rolled expressions today, but only by coincidence). If that rounding contract ever changes (the risk flagged in H9), the staged preview will silently diverge from what `buildEntryFromItems`/`handleAddAll` actually commits. Both `foodDBModal.tsx:345` and `savedNutritionModal.tsx:239` already wrap their "Add All" CTA in `guardSubmit` — confirmed in code — so those commit paths are NOT part of this gap. (5) The workout duplicate-name predicate is hand-copied twice, differing only by an exclude-self clause: `addWorkoutModal.tsx:27` (`workouts.some((w) => !w.archived && w.name.trim().toLowerCase() === trimmed.toLowerCase())`) and `renameModal.tsx:35-37` (same plus `w.id !== workoutId`). `addExerciseModal.tsx:59-61` has a superficially similar check but is confirmed structurally different (scoped by `workoutId` over `exercises`, matched on `item.title`, different Alert copy) — it is an intentional non-duplicate and stays untouched.

M1  (Medium)  [DONE]
===============================================================================
[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: Sign-out drains only the PowerSync upload queue (`flushUploadsOrThrow`) before wiping SQLite. Settings edits reach SQLite only through the deferred, `persistDirty`-gated persist effect in `SettingsContext/index.tsx:148-191`. A settings change that is still in-memory-dirty at sign-out time (mid-flight upsert, in backoff retry, or wedged by C3's stuck `persistSavingRef`) has never reached SQLite, so it is not in the upload queue; `flushUploadsOrThrow` sees an empty queue and returns, and the subsequent wipe silently discards the dirty in-memory settings. Confirmed unchanged from the banked brief after adjudication — the root-cause diagnosis was not disputed by any reviewer finding.

Fix: Force any dirty in-memory SettingsContext state down to SQLite BEFORE the upload-queue flush, and abort sign-out (no wipe) if that forced write fails. Architecture unchanged from the banked brief (registry + pre-flush hook + new error subclass); the SettingsContext-side implementation is amended to close a real stale-closure hole the reviewers found:

1. `lib/powersync/pendingWrites.ts` (NEW): `registerPendingFlush(fn) -> unregister` and `flushPendingLocalWrites()` that awaits all registered flushers, wrapping any rejection in `PendingWritesFlushError`.
2. `lib/powersync/FlushUploads.ts`: add `PendingWritesFlushError extends UploadFlushError` next to the existing family so `isUploadFlushError` / the sign-out escape hatch cover it automatically.
3. `context/AuthContext/functions/accountFunctions.tsx` `signOut()`: insert `await flushPendingLocalWrites()` immediately before `flushUploadsOrThrow(...)`. A rejection here aborts signOut before any teardown, exactly as a pre-flush `flushUploadsOrThrow` throw already does.
4. `context/SettingsContext/index.tsx` (AMENDED from the banked brief): register a flusher once (mount effect, unregister on unmount) that, when invoked, unconditionally does `if (loadedRef.current && userIDRef.current) await upsertSettings(userIDRef.current, settingsRef.current)` — with NO gate on `persistDirty` / `persistDirtyDuringSaveRef`. Add `settingsRef` and `loadedRef` alongside the existing `userIDRef`, all kept current via assignment on every render, so the mount-once closure never reads stale React state. Dropping the dirty-gate is deliberate: `persistDirty`/`loaded` are plain `useState`, and a flusher registered once at mount would otherwise close over their creation-time values forever, silently no-op-ing in the plain-dirty and backoff-retry interleavings the root cause names. `upsertSettings` is documented as always exactly one local DB op, so calling it unconditionally at sign-out is cheap and requires no additional serialization machinery — PowerSync's `writeTransaction`/`writeLock` already serializes writes in FIFO call order (verified through `@powersync/common` -> `RNQSDBAdapter` -> `@journeyapps/react-native-quick-sqlite`'s native `requestLock`), so if the deferred effect's own upsert (older snapshot) is still in-flight when the forced flusher runs, the forced upsert is queued strictly after it and is guaranteed to land last with the freshest data — no extra 'wait for in-flight save' ref is needed.

Deliberately NOT touched: the C3 retry/`persistSavingRef` machinery itself (separate issue, and now bypassed by construction since the flusher no longer reads it); `deleteAccount` (server already deleted the data); `forceSignOut` (its whole documented purpose is to skip flushing and accept data loss — offline force-sign-out still wipes the just-forced write if the user chooses that path, unchanged pre-existing behavior, out of scope); the workout-note debounce (separate, smaller window, flagged to coordinator); profile.tsx's sign-out error copy (optional low-priority follow-up, not required — see blastRadius.edgeCases).

Files to change:
  - c:\Users\aadik\Downloads\App\lib\powersync\pendingWrites.ts
      NEW small module: registerPendingFlush(fn) -> unregister, and flushPendingLocalWrites() that awaits all registered flushers and wraps failures in PendingWritesFlushError. Unchanged from banked brief.
  - c:\Users\aadik\Downloads\App\lib\powersync\FlushUploads.ts

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    signOut() is called only from app/settingsScreens/profile.tsx:86 (via useAuth). The new pre-flush step runs on that single path; deleteAccount and forceSignOut are untouched. flushPendingLocalWrites is invoked only by signOut; the registry is written only by SettingsProvider (one live provider). accountFunctions.test.ts asserts the existing ordering (flush -> auth.signOut -> disconnect -> clearUserStorage), so the new step slots in ahead of it without disturbing those expectations.
  Edge cases: Not dirty / nothing changed: flusher still performs one cheap local upsertSettings call (by design, no dirty-gate); common sign-out flow otherwise unchanged. No userID or settings not yet loaded: flusher no-ops via loadedRef/userIDRef guards. Offline: the forced upsert is a LOCAL SQLite write (no network) so it still succeeds and lands the change; flushUploadsOrThrow then still throws UploadFlushNotConnectedError as before. CORRECTED from banked brief: the 'safely in SQLite for next launch' benefit holds only if the user cancels or later retries while online; if the user instead taps the offered 'Force sign out', forceSignOut -> clearLocalSession -> disconnectAndClearPowerSync wipes SQLite unconditionally (verified accountFunctions.tsx:49-53/84-91), destroying the just-forced write exactly as today — forceSignOut's pre-existing, documented data-loss trade-off, out of scope for this fix. C3 wedge: flusher calls upsertSettings directly and never reads persistSavingRef, so the wedged change is captured. Concurrent write with the deferred persist effect's own in-flight upsert (older snapshot): verified this is NOT a race — PowerSync's writeTransaction/writeLock (traced through @powersync/common -> RNQSDBAdapter -> @journeyapps/react-native-quick-sqlite's native requestLock) serializes write-lock grants in FIFO call order, so the already-in-flight older-snapshot upsert (requested earlier) always completes and commits before the forced flusher's later-requested upsert can begin; the freshest data always lands last in SQLite. No additional serialization code is required. Forced upsert failure (e.g. DB locked): flusher rejects -> signOut aborts before wipe -> no silent loss. Local-write failure surfaced via profile.tsx: a PendingWritesFlushError falls into the existing 'Still syncing' / 'uploading your data' branch (imprecise copy for a local-write failure) rather than a bespoke message; the Cancel/Force-sign-out choice and its data-loss warning remain accurate and unchanged — treated as an optional low-priority copy fix, not required.
  Preserved:  When nothing is dirty the added flush is a single cheap local DB no-op, so the common sign-out flow is functionally unchanged. The new error is a subclass of UploadFlushError, so profile.tsx's existing isUploadFlushError branch already handles it (offers force sign out) with no structural UI change (copy precision is a separate, optional nit). The existing 'skip teardown when flush cannot drain' behavior is reused verbatim. No schema, no migration, no change to the C3 retry/persistSavingRef machinery, and no change to forceSignOut's documented behavior.
  Tests:      Add to context/AuthContext/functions/__tests__/accountFunctions.test.ts: 'flushes pending local writes before draining the upload queue' (assert call order) and 'aborts sign-out without wiping when a pending local write fails' (mock flushPendingLocalWrites reject -> expect supabase.auth.signOut/disconnectAndClearPowerSync/clearUserStorage NOT called). Add lib/powersync/__tests__/pendingWrites.test.ts for registry semantics. Add context/SettingsContext/__tests__/index.test.tsx (new) asserting the registered flusher forces the latest settings to SQLite even when persistDirty reads false in a stale-closure scenario — this pins the amendment and would fail against the banked brief's original persistDirty-gated design. Run: npx jest context/AuthContext/functions/__tests__/accountFunctions.test.ts lib/powersync/__tests__/pendingWrites.test.ts context/SettingsContext/__tests__/index.test.tsx.

Adjudication decisions:
  - persistDirty is React useState (SettingsContext/index.tsx:24), not a ref. The brief's Step 2 only adds settingsRef/userIDRef and gates the forced upsert on `persistDirty || persistDirtyDuringSaveRef.current`. A flusher registered once at mount ('effect, unregister on unmount') closes over persistDirty's creation-time value; since persistDirty stays true across the plain-dirty and backoff-retry interleavings (cleared only inside the deferred effect on success, line 165), a stale mount-time closure reading false would cause the flusher to silently no-op in exactly 2 of the 3 named triggering scenarios. (Raised independently ~7 times in the review batch, all pointing at index.tsx:24.)
      -> MATERIAL — correctness gate (rule 1). Verified against actual code: persistDirty is `useState` (line 24) and is never mirrored into a ref anywhere in the file; the brief only specifies settingsRef/userIDRef. As literally scoped, the fix would not force the write in the plain-dirty and backoff-retry cases, defeating its own purpose. AMENDED the brief: drop the persistDirty/persistDirtyDuringSaveRef dirty-gate entirely. The flusher unconditionally calls `upsertSettings(userIDRef.current, settingsRef.current)` whenever `loadedRef.current && userIDRef.current` (mirroring the existing effect's own guard minus the dirty check) — upsertSettings is documented 'Always exactly 1 DB op,' so an unconditional call is cheap and removes the entire class of closure-staleness bugs. Added `loadedRef` (same pattern as the existing userIDRef) since `loaded` is also plain useState and would suffer the identical staleness if read directly in a mount-once closure.  (Rule 1 (correctness, hard gate) — fix as specified would not resolve the root cause in 2 of its 3 named scenarios; amendment restores full correctness with less code (rule 4) than the alternative of adding a persistDirtyRef.)
  - A materially simpler approach exists: have the flusher unconditionally upsertSettings whenever userID is present, instead of gating on persistDirty — sidesteps the stale-closure hazard entirely (upsertSettings is documented as a single cheap idempotent DB op).
      -> ACCEPTED and adopted as the resolution for the finding above. Verified powersyncStore.ts:60-64 doc comment ('Always exactly 1 DB op (UPDATE or INSERT)') and that the write is purely local (no network), so calling it unconditionally at sign-out is safe and cheap. Folded the `loaded` guard in alongside (to mirror the deferred effect's existing invariant of never writing before the real settings are loaded from disk) rather than dropping all guards.  (Rule 4 (least code) resolves rule 1's gap with less new machinery than a persistDirtyRef would require, without any safety regression (rule 2).)
  - The blastRadius's 'concurrent write ... idempotent single-row upsert of identical data (last-writer-wins), harmless' claim is false: the deferred effect's in-flight upsertSettings call closes over an OLDER settings snapshot (S1, captured when that effect run started) while the forced flusher upserts the latest snapshot (S2). If the older in-flight upsert resolves AFTER the forced upsert, stale settings would sit in SQLite and get drained to Supabase — reverting the newer edit. (Raised ~4 times, citing index.tsx:156/159.)
      -> Verified the underlying concurrency model rather than take the claim at face value: `upsertSettings` -> `powerSync.writeTransaction` -> `writeLock` -> (react-native adapter) `RNQSDBAdapter.writeLock` -> `@journeyapps/react-native-quick-sqlite`'s native `requestLock(dbName, id, WRITE)` (setup-open.ts:152-179), which grants a single exclusive write lock per request in FIFO call order. Because the deferred effect's `upsertSettings(S1)` call is already in-flight BEFORE signOut / the forced flusher can even be invoked, the forced flusher's `upsertSettings(S2)` request is necessarily queued AFTER it and cannot begin executing until S1's transaction commits and releases the lock. So S2 (the freshest data) is guaranteed to land last, not S1 — there is no non-deterministic race. IMMATERIAL as a call for new code (no persistInFlightRef / extra serialization needed — would be unnecessary complexity per rule 4). MATERIAL only as wording: the brief's 'identical data' phrasing is imprecise (the two payloads genuinely differ), so the blastRadius text is corrected to explain the real safety mechanism (write-lock FIFO ordering) instead of the incorrect 'identical data' framing.  (Rule 1 (correctness) — claim verified false against actual node_modules lock semantics, so no code amendment required; only the brief's justification text is corrected for accuracy (documentation fix, not a code change).)
  - The offline edge-case claim ('the forced write is safely in SQLite for next launch') is overstated: if the user is offline and taps the 'Force sign out' button the UI actually offers, `forceSignOut` -> `clearLocalSession` -> `disconnectAndClearPowerSync` wipes SQLite unconditionally (accountFunctions.tsx:49-53, 84-91), destroying the just-forced write. The benefit only holds if the user cancels/waits, not on the offered destructive action. (Raised ~4 times.)
      -> MATERIAL as a documentation-accuracy issue, not a code defect. Verified: `forceSignOut` (line 49) calls `clearLocalSession` (line 84) which unconditionally runs `disconnectAndClearPowerSync` (line 88) — confirmed the wipe happens regardless of what was just forced to SQLite. This is `forceSignOut`'s explicitly documented, pre-existing, intentional trade-off ('This can lose unsynced local data'), and the banked brief itself lists `forceSignOut` under 'Deliberately NOT touched.' The fix was never meant to change that documented escape hatch's behavior — so this does not fail rule 1 for the actual code change. Corrected the blastRadius.edgeCases wording to scope the offline benefit accurately.  (Rule 1 correctness is unaffected (scope of the fix never included forceSignOut); amendment is confined to correcting the brief's own overstated narrative.)
  - A PendingWritesFlushError (a local SQLite write failure) will render through profile.tsx's existing non-offline branch ('Still syncing' / "We're still uploading your data..."), which is semantically wrong for a local write failure, contradicting the brief's 'no UI change' claim — though the Force sign out escape hatch still appears. (Raised ~3 times, citing profile.tsx:89-91.)
      -> MATERIAL but low-severity/cosmetic. Verified profile.tsx:88-91: `offline = error instanceof UploadFlushNotConnectedError`; anything else (including the new PendingWritesFlushError) falls into the 'Still syncing' branch. The safety behavior is unaffected — the user is still offered the same Cancel / Force-sign-out choice with an accurate generic warning ('unsynced data may be lost') — so this does not reopen the data-loss root cause (rule 1 holds) and does not change blast radius (rule 2). Not required as a blocking fix; noted as an optional low-priority follow-up rather than mandating a new UI branch, per rule 4 (least code).  (Rules 1/2 (correctness/safety) are not implicated, so under rule 4 (least code) this is recorded as an optional polish item, not a required amendment.)

M2  (Medium)  [DONE]

[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: Two independent gaps sit under one "no backup/DR story" heading. (1) OPERATIONAL: PowerSync treats Supabase Postgres as the single source of truth — it down-syncs the server state over the local SQLite replica (orchestrator.ts connect path) and disconnectAndClear() (orchestrator.ts:100) deliberately wipes the local copy on sign-out. Nothing in the repo configures or documents Supabase Point-in-Time Recovery (PITR) or a restore runbook, so a Supabase project loss/corruption permanently destroys every user's data with no recovery lever. (2) CODE: meal-photo capture produces an ImageManipulator output URI in the OS cache directory (camera path: lib/openAI/mealImage.ts:48-88 processCameraCapture; library-pick path: mealImage.ts:33-42 processPickedImageUri — CORRECTED citation, the banked brief mis-anchored the camera path to the picker function). That cache-dir file:// path is written to photo_uri (powersyncStore.ts:56,100) and synced to Postgres (AppSchema.ts:64,95), but the image bytes are never uploaded anywhere, so the synced value is a device-local, purgeable reference that dies on cache purge/restore/second device — even though today (CONFIRMED: zero photoUri/Image references in Entry.tsx, SavedEntry.tsx, editEntry.tsx) no shipped UI renders that stored value back, so this is a latent/unobserved risk rather than a live user-visible break.

Fix: OPERATIONAL (unchanged core, runbook content corrected for accuracy):
1. Enable Supabase PITR on the production project (dashboard action, not a repo change).
2. Add docs/runbooks/backup-dr.md documenting: (a) Postgres is the sole durable store; (b) PITR retention window; (c) restore procedure — restore Postgres, redeploy sync rules, clients reconnect; (d) CORRECTED per findings: explicitly state the residual RPO gap — ops that were already uploaded and acknowledged (Connector.uploadData calls transaction.complete(), clearing them from the local CRUD queue) are NOT recoverable by client re-push once they predate the restore point; and on reconnect, PowerSync down-syncs the restored (older) server state over local replicas, which can silently overwrite local data newer than the restore point — do not claim 'clients re-hydrate automatically' resolves recovery; instruct incident responders to quiesce writes / communicate the exact RPO cutoff to affected users.

CODE (narrowed scope — offline queue removed, call site corrected, key scheme corrected, delete-orphan policy made explicit):
3. Upload processed photo bytes to a private Supabase Storage bucket keyed by a decoupled `photoId` (uuid, NOT `{entryId}`) — `meal-photos/{userId}/{photoId}.jpg` — and store the object path in `photo_uri`. Trigger point is `analyzeAndAddPhoto` in context/NutritionContext/functions/aiFunctions.tsx, AFTER `nutritionEntryError` validation passes and immediately alongside `addNutrition` — NOT in `runPhotoAnalysis` (shared, non-persisting dev-harness core per its own comment — uploading there would push dev/test photos to the production bucket and upload before the persist/validation/cancel gates run) and NOT in cameraScreen.tsx's `usePhoto` (CONFIRMED by code: usePhoto only does `router.push` to analyzingModal with the raw param — no entry or entryId exists at that point; the entry is built later in aiFunctions.tsx after a successful AI call).
4. Drop the offline pending-upload/queue state machine entirely for the AI-photo path: CONFIRMED that `runPhotoAnalysis` requires a successful synchronous `askOpenAIVision` network call (30s timeout) before any entry object exists, so there is no genuine 'photo captured offline' window for this path — the upload can happen synchronously in the same online session as analysis, and `photo_uri` needs only one value (no dual local+object-path fields, no pending marker).
5. Add a new migration lib/supabase/migrations/nutrition_photos_bucket.sql: private bucket + owner-scoped RLS, recorded in the migration ledger.
6. Delete/unsave lifecycle (forced by confirmed fan-out defect): saved-meal 'add back' (savedNutritionModal.tsx:109) copies `photoUri` verbatim into a brand-new entry with a different id — an entryId-keyed object path breaks the instant that happens, which is why the key must be a decoupled `photoId` (item 3) copied by reference, not re-derived per entry. For v1, `deleteNutrition`/`unsaveNutrition` (crudFunctions.tsx) do NOT attempt Storage cleanup — since a photoId can be referenced by both an original entry and N saved-meal copies, safe deletion requires a reference check that is out of scope for this pass. Explicitly document this as an accepted, known leak (private bucket, low volume, revisit with a refcount/GC pass later) rather than leaving it silently unhandled.
7. Drop the 'render through a signed/public URL' implementation task from this pass — CONFIRMED no shipped code currently reads the persisted `photo_uri` back for display (Entry.tsx, SavedEntry.tsx, editEntry.tsx have zero photoUri/Image references; the only renders are ephemeral in-flight params in analyzingModal.tsx/cameraScreen preview, which never touch the DB column). Building signed-URL resolution now would be speculative, untested-by-necessity code with no consumer; leave a code comment noting that a future render feature must resolve a signed URL from the object path, and do not ship the resolver itself in this change.

      NEW — DR runbook, amended per findings to explicitly state the residual RPO gap (already-acknowledged ops are not re-pushable; reconnect down-syncs restored/older state and can overwrite newer local data) instead of asserting 'clients re-hydrate automatically' solves recovery.

Existing entries with file:// photo_uri continue to round-trip unchanged through the mapper. The DR runbook makes no code changes and touches no shipped path.

(Correctness (hard gate) — a DR runbook that overstates recovery guarantees is itself a defect; ops would rely on it during a real incident.)
  - On reconnect after a PITR restore, PowerSync down-syncs the (older) restored server state over the local replica, which can silently overwrite locally newer data — the runbook's 'clients re-hydrate automatically' phrasing doesn't flag this (orchestrator.ts:62, finding lines 68-71).
      -> MATERIAL and CONFIRMED by reading ensurePowerSyncConnected (calls powerSync.connect(new Connector()), and PowerSync's down-sync-is-authoritative design is already established elsewhere in the same brief). Amended the runbook to document this overwrite risk and recommend quiescing writes / notifying affected users during a restore.  (Correctness (hard gate) — same reasoning as the prior RPO finding; documentation must not overpromise.)

M3  (Medium)  [DONE]
===============================================================================
[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: The backend has no migration project: `lib/supabase/migrations/` is a non-standard path holding nine loose SQL files (schema.sql plus eight incremental files) applied by hand through the Supabase dashboard, with no ledger recording which have been applied to prod and in what order. `schema.sql` is itself a mixed-era snapshot, not a clean starting point — it already bakes in `nutrition_entries.calories REAL` / `saved_nutrition_entries.calories REAL` (the post-`nutrition_calories_real.sql` state) AND `logs.time BIGINT` (the post-`log_timeAttribute.sql` state), while prod's `nutrition_entries.calories` is still INTEGER until `nutrition_calories_real.sql` runs — so the 'reference schema' is aspirational for calories specifically, and re-applying it against prod as-is would fail (`CREATE TABLE` on existing tables) or lie about history. Ordering is only informally documented in a comment (`powersync_setup.sql:6-9`). Edge functions (`lib/supabase/functions/{deleteAccount,fetchFoodDB,fetchOpenAI}`) sit outside any CLI layout, so deployed copies can silently drift from the repo with nothing to diff them. Net effect: no one can answer 'what is actually in prod right now?' from the repo, which is exactly what turned C2 into a silent data-loss window and what will let the next schema-ahead-of-migration slip through.

Fix: This is primarily an OPERATIONAL / repo-hygiene fix (no app runtime code changes), with a small documentation deliverable. Adopt the Supabase CLI project layout so the CLI's built-in `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations` table becomes the authoritative ledger and `supabase db diff` can detect drift.

Steps (exact):
1. `supabase init` at repo root -> creates `supabase/config.toml`. Add `.supabase/` (local temp) to `.gitignore`.
2. Convert the nine files in `lib/supabase/migrations/` into an ordered, forward-only migration set under `supabase/migrations/`, timestamp-prefixed in dependency + chronological order: (1) baseline = schema.sql BUT with `nutrition_entries.calories` and `saved_nutrition_entries.calories` restored to INTEGER only (ingredient tables were always REAL and stay untouched), then (2) rls_policies.sql, (3) powersync_setup.sql, then the remaining eight files by real date: log_timeAttribute.sql, nutrition_calories_real.sql, user_cascade.sql, support_request.sql, settings_goal_intent_flags.sql, ingredient_brand.sql. NOTE: schema.sql already bakes in `logs.time` (log_timeAttribute.sql's change), so the baseline is a mixed-era snapshot, not a clean single point in time — replaying log_timeAttribute.sql's `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` against it is a harmless no-op, but this means the ordered set reproduces prod's current END-STATE (verifiable via `db diff`), not an exact chronological replay of history; document this distinction in the README (step 5) so it isn't overclaimed.
3. CRITICAL seeding step so this cannot re-run destructive DDL on live prod: mark the already-applied migrations as applied in the ledger with `supabase migration repair --status applied <version>` for EVERY file confirmed live — do not treat any non-baseline file as automatically low-risk. Verify per file, not per assumed idempotency class: schema.sql (bare CREATE TABLE), rls_policies.sql (bare CREATE POLICY x~40), support_request.sql (bare CREATE TABLE + CREATE INDEX + CREATE POLICY), and powersync_setup.sql (CREATE ROLE + CREATE PUBLICATION) are all NON-idempotent and will error on re-run if mis-marked pending. Only log_timeAttribute.sql, settings_goal_intent_flags.sql's two `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` statements, and ingredient_brand.sql are true additive-safe migrations. nutrition_calories_real.sql (`ALTER COLUMN ... TYPE real`) is idempotent only incidentally (a real->real re-cast is a no-op); settings_goal_intent_flags.sql's trailing unconditional `UPDATE settings SET updated_at = now()` is NOT side-effect-free on re-run — it re-touches every settings row and forces PowerSync to re-replicate all settings rows to every client. `ingredient_brand.sql` status is unknown — verify against prod, do NOT assume. This per-file verification is the whole point of the fix: establish ground truth for 'applied vs pending.'
4. Move `lib/supabase/functions/*` -> `supabase/functions/*` so `supabase functions deploy` is the source of truth and `supabase functions download` can diff drift. The RN client does NOT import these at runtime (only doc-comment references), so the Expo/EAS build is unaffected.
5. Add `supabase/migrations/README.md` (or `docs/DEPLOY.md`) as the human-readable ledger + deploy-order checklist: table of migration -> applied-to-prod? -> notes; the rule 'client AppSchema.ts / Connector opData columns must not ship ahead of their migration'; a note that the baseline is a mixed-era snapshot (bakes in logs.time) so 'reproduces prod' means end-state equivalence, not exact historical replay; and a note on why user_cascade.sql can safely run either before or after support_request.sql (support_request.sql already declares `ON DELETE CASCADE` inline, so user_cascade's catalog-driven repair has nothing to fix there regardless of order).

Deliberately NOT touched: the runtime deploy-order guard (that is C2's job — note overlap; this brief supplies the process ledger that guard reads against). Do NOT rotate/edit the `powersync_role` password here — that is H14; the placeholder `CHANGE_ME_TO_SECURE_PASSWORD` string is committed as-is and is understood to NOT match prod's actual password (see blastRadius/tests caveat below). Keep table names legacy (`*_ingredients`) per CLAUDE.md. This is a de-duplication/single-source-of-truth problem, so introducing the CLI project IS the fix, not gratuitous new abstraction.

Files to change:
  - supabase/config.toml
      NEW — created by `supabase init`; establishes the CLI project so `schema_migrations` ledger and `db diff`/`db push` become available.
  - supabase/migrations/<timestamp>_baseline.sql
      NEW — schema.sql content moved here but with nutrition_entries.calories and saved_nutrition_entries.calories set back to INTEGER only (ingredient tables' calories stay REAL, untouched) so the baseline reflects prod's calories state; note in-file that logs.time is already baked in from log_timeAttribute.sql (mixed-era snapshot, not a clean start).
  - supabase/migrations/<timestamp>_*.sql (x8)

  - lib/supabase/migrations/
      REMOVED after contents relocated; kills the non-standard path the audit flags.

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    Zero RN runtime callers. The client app reads env via lib/env.ts and talks to Supabase over the network; it never imports lib/supabase/migrations/* or lib/supabase/functions/* into the Metro bundle (confirmed: only doc-comment references in deleteAccount/index.ts:26 and user_cascade_test.sql:2). Edge functions run on Supabase, deployed out-of-band, so relocating their source does not change the running system. Consumers of this change are humans/CI running `supabase db push` / `functions deploy`, not the app. Historical documents (docs/PRODUCTION_READINESS_AUDIT.md, .superpowers/sdd/*.md task briefs) also reference the old paths but are point-in-time records, not live consumers — deliberately left unedited.
  Edge cases: (1) Re-running DDL on live prod is the failure mode this fix must avoid. VERIFIED: non-idempotent files are NOT limited to the baseline — schema.sql (CREATE TABLE), rls_policies.sql (~40 bare CREATE POLICY), support_request.sql (bare CREATE TABLE + CREATE INDEX + CREATE POLICY), and powersync_setup.sql (CREATE ROLE + CREATE PUBLICATION) all error on re-run if mis-marked pending. Only log_timeAttribute.sql, settings_goal_intent_flags.sql's two ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS statements, and ingredient_brand.sql are genuinely additive-safe. nutrition_calories_real.sql's `ALTER COLUMN TYPE` is idempotent only incidentally (no-op real->real cast); settings_goal_intent_flags.sql's trailing unconditional `UPDATE settings SET updated_at = now()` is NOT side-effect-free on re-run (re-touches every row, forces PowerSync to re-replicate all settings to all clients). Step 3 must verify EVERY file against prod individually — none may be assumed low-risk by category. (2) Unknown application status of ingredient_brand.sql — must be verified against prod before marking applied. (3) Edge-function drift: if deployed copies already diverged from repo, `functions download` will surface the diff — reconcile before adopting the repo as source of truth. (5) `supabase db diff`/`db reset` reliably validate table/column DDL, but roles are cluster-global and publications/cross-schema grants are not reliably captured by schema-diff tooling — treat 'db diff empty' as end-state proof for tables/columns only, and manually cross-check CREATE ROLE / CREATE PUBLICATION / auth grants separately.
  Preserved:  No behavior change to the shipped app or the live database when done correctly — the migrations describe state prod already has (end-state, not exact chronological history, since schema.sql is a mixed-era snapshot); the ledger only records it. RLS isolation, PowerSync publication, and cascade behavior are unchanged (same SQL, reordered; user_cascade.sql's placement relative to support_request.sql is order-independent because support_request.sql already declares ON DELETE CASCADE inline). Existing DB tests still apply: `lib/supabase/tests/user_cascade_test.sql` and `lib/supabase/__tests__/authInternals.test.ts` continue to pass since neither depends on file locations.

Prove it by: (a) `supabase db reset` on a scratch/branch DB and confirm applying the ordered migrations reproduces current prod's TABLE/COLUMN schema (`supabase db diff` returns empty against a dump of prod) — this validates DDL only, not roles/publications/grants; (b) separately, manually cross-check `powersync_role`'s existence/privileges (`\du powersync_role`) and the `powersync` publication (`SELECT * FROM pg_publication`) against prod, understanding the committed password is a placeholder and will not match prod's real one (H14's concern, unaffected here); (c) run the existing `lib/supabase/tests/user_cascade_test.sql` against the reproduced DB to confirm cascade + RLS survive the reorder; (d) `supabase functions download` and diff each of the three functions against the relocated source to confirm zero drift before flipping the repo to source-of-truth. Add a CI step running `supabase migration list` / `db lint` so future schema changes must land as a tracked migration. NOTE overlaps: C2 (runtime deploy-order guard consumes this ledger), H14 (BYPASSRLS role password rotation lives in powersync_setup.sql being relocated here) — flag for coordinator consolidation.

      -> MATERIAL. This false generalization undersells the risk of step 3 (migration repair / applied-vs-pending marking), which is the entire safety mechanism the fix depends on. M4  (Medium)  [DONE]

[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: Two gaps under one issue, corrected from the banked brief's mechanism after code verification. (1) app/_layout.tsx currently calls assertRequiredEnv() (line 60) before Sentry.init (line 62) at module scope. The banked brief's proposed fix — simply swapping their order within _layout.tsx — cannot work for 2 of the 3 required vars: package.json's "main" is "expo-router/entry", which transitively requires app/_layout.tsx; that file's own import block (line 5: AuthProvider) eagerly pulls in lib/supabase/client.ts, which calls createClient(ENV.SUPABASE_URL!, ENV.SUPABASE_ANON_KEY!, ...) at MODULE scope (client.ts:6-9). ES import evaluation always completes before any of the importing module's own top-level statements run, so if SUPABASE_URL or SUPABASE_ANON_KEY is missing, supabase-js throws during import evaluation — strictly before _layout.tsx line 60 OR line 62 ever execute, regardless of their order. Only POWERSYNC_URL (read lazily inside Connector.fetchCredentials(), Connector.ts:38) actually reaches assertRequiredEnv/Sentry.init as currently structured. So the real root cause is that no in-file reorder inside app/_layout.tsx can guarantee telemetry for an eager-import-time crash from a transitively-imported module — only a wrapper at the app's true entry point can. (2) eas.json's build profiles (development/preview/production) have no explicit environment linkage, so which backend/DSN each profile builds against is invisible and unverifiable from the repo.

Fix: Part A (corrected): create a new root entry file `index.js` that calls Sentry.init({ dsn: ENV.SENTRY_DSN }) as its first executable statement, then requires 'expo-router/entry' via an explicit `require()` call (not `import`, so Babel doesn't hoist it above Sentry.init) wrapped in try/catch — on throw, call Sentry.captureException(err) then rethrow. Point package.json's "main" at this new file instead of "expo-router/entry" directly. This guarantees Sentry has already initialized before ANY of the app's own module-scope code (including the eager Supabase client creation and assertRequiredEnv()) runs, so a missing SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_ANON_KEY, or POWERSYNC_URL is captured to Sentry before the crash, regardless of which one is missing or where in the import graph the throw originates. Remove the now-duplicate Sentry.init(...) call and the dead enableInExpoDevelopment option from app/_layout.tsx (lines 62-65) — keep assertRequiredEnv() at line 60 and Sentry.wrap(RootLayout) at line 67 unchanged. Part B (corrected): add an `"environment": "development"|"preview"|"production"` string field to each eas.json build profile, referencing the EAS-dashboard-managed variable-group of the same name, NOT literal env key/value blocks — this gives explicit, verifiable in-repo backend/DSN separation without committing secrets. Explicitly OUT of scope for M4 (deferred to L8 and M5 per the audit doc's own item boundaries): the Sentry `environment` tag, tracing/tracesSampleRate, and removing enableInExpoDevelopment as a 'dev-pollution fix' — none of those changes actually stop dev-client sessions from reporting into the same prod DSN (no `enabled: !__DEV__` gate exists), so they don't belong bundled into this brief's claims.

  - c:\Users\aadik\Downloads\App\package.json
      "main": "expo-router/entry" -> "main": "./index.js" (or "index.js").
  - c:\Users\aadik\Downloads\App\app\_layout.tsx

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    index.js becomes the new Metro/bundle entry point (resolved via package.json "main"); it does nothing but init Sentry then immediately delegate to the existing 'expo-router/entry', so route registration and a healthy launch are byte-identical to today. app/_layout.tsx's Sentry.init call is deleted outright, not duplicated — Sentry.wrap(RootLayout) still operates against the SDK instance already initialized in index.js (Sentry.init is a module-level singleton call; calling it once anywhere before use is sufficient and correct). assertRequiredEnv is still called exactly once, at the same call site, with an unchanged named-error contract. eas.json's new `environment` field is read only by the EAS build service at build time — no runtime code path reads eas.json, so this cannot regress `expo start`, Jest, or the running app.

A build whose entire env group is unconfigured (all three required vars AND the DSN missing) still crashes silently — call this out to the team as an operational risk alongside the eas.json `environment` linkage in Part B. Also run `eas build --profile preview` and `--profile production` and confirm each resolves against its own EAS Environment (verify the Supabase URL / Sentry DSN baked into each built bundle differ appropriately). [Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: Sentry.init in app/_layout.tsx:62-65 passes only dsn and enableInExpoDevelopment:false. No environment is set, so dev-client, EAS preview, and App Store production all report into one undifferentiated stream in the single 'lifttritionapp' Sentry project. No tracesSampleRate, so tracing is off. enableInExpoDevelopment is a dead sentry-expo option (project uses @sentry/react-native ^8.3.0) and is a no-op — this same leftover is tracked separately as audit issue L8 ('enableInExpoDevelopment... can send dev-client sessions into prod Sentry'; official prescribed fix: delete the dead option), not H4 as an earlier draft mis-cited. Source-map upload is wired at build level (metro.config.js uses getSentryExpoConfig; app.json registers @sentry/react-native/expo, org na-mvc, project lifttritionapp) but the plugin only uploads when SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN is present in the build env. That token lives only in gitignored .env.local and is wired into no eas.json profile, so — unless it has separately been added as an EAS secret/dashboard env var (unverifiable from the repo) — EAS cloud builds may ship un-symbolicated stack traces; this specific sub-problem is an operational/secrets-management gap that code changes alone cannot close or verify.

Fix: Code change (app/_layout.tsx:62-65): expand Sentry.init to add `environment` (from a new build-time ENV.APP_ENV, defaulting to 'development' at the read site), add `tracesSampleRate: 0.2` (conservative default; its quota/cost impact should be sanity-checked against the team's actual Sentry plan — not asserted as risk-free), gate init on ENV.SENTRY_DSN presence to preserve the existing self-disable contract, and delete the no-op enableInExpoDevelopment line. This is a tag-and-differentiate fix (matching both this issue's root cause and L8's own official prescribed remedy of 'delete the dead option') — it does not suppress dev-session reporting, only labels it; an `enabled: !__DEV__` style suppression gate is a reasonable future enhancement but is out of scope here. Do not hand-set release/dist — getSentryExpoConfig plus the Expo plugin auto-inject those from the app version when the auth token is present. Env plumbing: add `APP_ENV: process.env.EXPO_PUBLIC_APP_ENV` to ENV in lib/env.ts (kept optional — do not add to assertRequiredEnv, which would break local `expo start`); additionally add a lightweight non-fatal safeguard — when `!__DEV__` and ENV.APP_ENV is unset, emit a console.warn/breadcrumb — so a misconfigured production EAS profile is visible in logs instead of silently degrading to environment=development. Add `EXPO_PUBLIC_APP_ENV=` to .env.example under the optional block. Operational (eas.json): add an env block ONLY to the `preview` and `production` profiles (EXPO_PUBLIC_APP_ENV=preview / production respectively) — do NOT add one to the `development` profile, because that profile's developmentClient:true build loads its JS from local Metro at runtime, not from the EAS build-time env block, so an env entry there is inert; local/dev-client tagging is handled entirely by lib/env.ts's 'development' default. Then register SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN as an EAS secret (`eas secret:create --scope project --name SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN --value TOKEN`) so the plugin uploads source maps at cloud-build time — but treat this specific step as an unverifiable-from-code, standing release-checklist item (confirm a symbolicated stack trace in the next EAS preview/production build) rather than a claim this diff fully resolves. Do not commit the token. Documentation: correct the brief's overlap cross-reference from 'H4' to the correct audit id 'L8'.

M6  (Medium)  [DONE]

[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: expo-updates is not installed and app.json declares no `updates` block or `runtimeVersion`, so the app has no over-the-air (OTA) update runtime. Every JS/asset-level hotfix therefore requires a full native rebuild and App Store review cycle (typically 24-48h+), lengthening the incident window for the JS/application-layer portions of the C2/H2 sync/data-loss classes (e.g. sync-queue logic, retry/backoff, Connector-level bugs). Scope correction: this benefit does NOT extend to fixes that require changing the native `@powersync/react-native` or quick-sqlite layer (package.json:19) — those still need a full rebuild + review regardless of this change. This is an operational/deploy-discipline gap, not a code defect.

Fix: 1) Run `npx expo install expo-updates` to add the dependency at an SDK-54-compatible version. 2) Run `eas update:configure` (the canonical Expo CLI command) rather than hand-editing config — it derives `updates.url` from the existing `extra.eas.projectId` (app.json) and wires `runtimeVersion` (policy: fingerprint) plus the `build.preview`/`build.production` `channel` fields in eas.json automatically, eliminating the typo/drift risk of hand-typing the `u.expo.dev` URL and channel names. Verify the generated diff matches expectations; set `updates.fallbackToCacheTimeout` to a small value (e.g. 0) explicitly if the CLI doesn't already. 3) Write an operational runbook (docs/OTA_HOTFIX.md) covering: how to publish (`eas update --branch <channel>`) and roll back (`eas update:rollback`); the corrected safety model below (no technical claim beyond what's true); the Sentry observability prerequisite; and the preview/production isolation caveat.

Files to change:
  - c:\Users\aadik\Downloads\App\package.json
      Add expo-updates via `npx expo install expo-updates` (let expo resolve the SDK-54-compatible version; do not hand-edit).
  - c:\Users\aadik\Downloads\App\app.json
      Run `eas update:configure` to add the `updates` block (url derived from extra.eas.projectId) and `runtimeVersion: { policy: "fingerprint" }`; confirm/add `fallbackToCacheTimeout: 0` so a failed/slow update check never blocks boot. Do not hand-type the u.expo.dev URL.
  - c:\Users\aadik\Downloads\App\eas.json
      Let `eas update:configure` add `channel` to build.preview and build.production. Separately (documented as a pre-existing gap, not solved by this fix): eas.json has no `env` blocks on any profile, so preview/production backend isolation is unverified — flag this as a prerequisite to trust preview-channel staging for any schema-affecting OTA (tracked as its own audit item, no env separation).
  - c:\Users\aadik\Downloads\App\docs\OTA_HOTFIX.md (new runbook)
      Document: (1) publish/rollback commands; (2) CORRECTED safety model — `runtimeVersion: fingerprint` only forces a full rebuild for changes that alter native deps/config; PowerSync AppSchema.ts and Connector.ts are pure TypeScript (verified: `column`/`Table`/`Schema` from @powersync/react-native's JS API) and ship via OTA under ANY runtimeVersion policy — fingerprint provides ZERO technical enforcement against shipping a client schema/Connector change ahead of its Postgres migration; the ONLY guard is the manual process rule below; (3) hard process rule: never publish a client schema/Connector change to the `production` channel until the matching Supabase migration is confirmed deployed — this is a checklist/code-review discipline, not a technical control; (4) Sentry prerequisite: do not rely on OTA as an incident-response channel until per-update source-map upload is wired (Sentry.init currently sets only dsn/enableInExpoDevelopment, no release/dist, and SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN is unreferenced in code — this is tracked as a separate Sentry config gap; OTA'd hotfix crashes will be unsymbolicated until it's fixed); (5) preview-staging caveat: 'stage on preview first' only isolates the OTA channel, not necessarily the backend — verify preview/production point at different Supabase projects (currently unverified, no env blocks in eas.json) before trusting it as a safety gate for schema-affecting changes; (6) native-code caveat: OTA cannot patch the native @powersync/react-native/quick-sqlite layer — a wedged-sync fix requiring native changes still needs a full rebuild; (7) speed caveat: updates apply on next cold start (no `Updates.reloadAsync()` exists in the codebase today), so this is not instant for a user already wedged mid-session.

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    No app code imports/calls expo-updates in this change; no runtime call sites are touched. Corrected: the app boots visually/functionally identically (no blocking, no UI change — fallbackToCacheTimeout:0 prevents any spinner regression) but gains a new background network dependency on u.expo.dev on every cold start once the updates block is added. Affected surfaces are build/deploy config and the CI/release process; downstream consumers are the EAS build pipeline and the on-call engineer running hotfixes. ZERO already-installed 1.0.4 users gain OTA until they install a fresh binary built after this change.
  Edge cases: Cold start with no network / bad update: fallbackToCacheTimeout:0 launches from the embedded/cached bundle immediately and fetches in background — no infinite-spinner regression. First-run (no cached update) always uses the embedded bundle. runtimeVersion `fingerprint` correctly forces a full build when NATIVE deps/config change, but — corrected — does NOT force a build for JS-level PowerSync AppSchema/Connector changes, which remain fully OTA-deliverable under any runtimeVersion policy; the schema-vs-migration ordering has no technical enforcement, only the manual runbook rule. Native-layer @powersync/react-native/quick-sqlite fixes are never OTA-deliverable, full stop.
