M14  (Medium)  [DONE]
===============================================================================
[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: useToday (lib/hooks/useToday.ts:8-17) recomputes the date key only inside an AppState 'change' listener that fires on foreground/background transitions. There is no timer tracking the actual midnight boundary. If the app stays foregrounded across local midnight (never backgrounded), no 'active' event fires, so `check()` is never called and `today` stays on yesterday's key. Every consumer that derives "Today" from this hook — progress.tsx streaks/week grouping (todayKey), NutritionContext date grouping and selectedDate default (todayKey), Calendar.tsx, logsModal.tsx, nutritionScreen.tsx — stays a day stale until a background/foreground cycle. (Write-time entry dates are unaffected because those call getDateKey(new Date()) directly at write time.)

Fix: Add a self-rescheduling timer to the same useEffect that fires at the next local midnight, alongside the existing AppState listener. Compute the next-midnight delay via wall-clock local construction (`new Date(y, m, d + 1, 0, 0, 0, 0)` minus now — DST-safe since it targets a calendar boundary, not a fixed ms offset), schedule `setTimeout(onMidnight, delay + ~1000ms buffer)`. On fire, run the existing `check()` (already no-ops via the `prev === key` guard when unchanged) and reschedule the next midnight timer. Keep the AppState 'active' listener exactly as-is as the catch-up path for iOS suspending JS timers while backgrounded. Cleanup must run both `sub.remove()` and `clearTimeout(timerRef)`. Reuse `getDateKey`; no polling/setInterval; no consumer signature changes.

Files to change:
  - lib/hooks/useToday.ts
      In the existing useEffect (lines 8-17), factor the recompute into `check()`, add `scheduleMidnight()` that setTimeouts to next local midnight (+1s buffer), calls `check()` then reschedules on fire; store the timeout id and clear it in cleanup alongside sub.remove(). Keep the AppState 'active' -> check() listener unchanged. Effect dependency array stays [].
  - lib/hooks/__tests__/useToday.test.tsx
      Add a test 'rolls over at midnight while foregrounded (no AppState event)': setSystemTime to 2026-07-13 23:59, mount, advance system time to 2026-07-14 00:00 and run jest fake timers past the scheduled delay (jest.advanceTimersByTime), asserting the last rendered value flips to '2026-07-14' WITHOUT firing any AppState listener. Fails before the fix, passes after. Keep existing 5 tests.

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    useToday is consumed by app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:34 (streaks, week grouping), context/NutritionContext/index.tsx:28 (todayKey for grouping + default selectedDate), components/NeutralComponents/Calendar.tsx:2, app/workoutScreens/logsModal.tsx:24, app/nutritionScreens/nutritionScreen.tsx. All receive the same string return type; no signature changes. IMPORTANT CORRECTION (verified against context/NutritionContext/index.tsx:33-35): NutritionContext is NOT a passive re-render-only consumer. It runs `useEffect(() => setSelectedDate(new Date()), [todayKey])`, which actively mutates `selectedDate` whenever todayKey changes. Today this reset only reaches the user after a background->foreground cycle, which already interrupts their session. After this fix, the same reset can fire live at local midnight while the app is foregrounded and the user is actively viewing/editing a past-dated entry (surfaced in app/nutritionScreens/nutritionScreen.tsx, which reads `selectedDate`), silently snapping their view to today mid-interaction with no app-state transition to explain it. This is a newly-reachable, real behavior change, not merely 'the same mechanism as the existing AppState path.'
  Edge cases: DST: tomorrow's midnight is built via new Date(y,m,d+1,0,0,0,0) (wall-clock local), so spring-forward/fall-back still lands on the correct next calendar midnight rather than a fixed 86.4M ms offset. iOS timer suspension: JS setTimeout may not fire while backgrounded — the retained AppState 'active' listener catches up on resume. Timezone change while running is out of scope (also unhandled today). Delay is always <24h so no 32-bit setTimeout overflow. The +1s buffer prevents a pre-midnight fire reading the stale day. NEW: a user actively browsing a non-today date in the nutrition screen at the exact local-midnight instant, with the app foregrounded, will now have selectedDate silently reset to today mid-session (previously required a background/foreground cycle first). This is an accepted, documented consequence of correctly fixing the root cause, not a fix defect — but product/UX should be told about it explicitly. If silently bumping an actively-viewed past date at midnight is judged undesirable, that is a separate, narrowly-scoped follow-up in NutritionContext (e.g., only auto-follow today when the user hasn't manually navigated away), not part of this ticket's mandate to repair useToday's midnight rollover.
  Preserved:  The AppState listener and its same-day no-op (prev===key guard) are untouched, so existing tests 'does not update on same-day foreground', 'updates when active fires after midnight', 'ignores background/inactive', and 'removes subscription on unmount' all still pass. Cleanup now clears the timer too, avoiding a leaked timeout/setState-after-unmount. Write-time entry dates were never sourced from this hook, so nothing about persisted data changes. NutritionContext's todayKey-keyed selectedDate reset is pre-existing, deliberate behavior — this fix does not add it, it only makes it reachable via a new (foregrounded, no-transition) trigger path, which is now explicitly documented above rather than denied.
  Tests:      Add the new 'rolls over at midnight while foregrounded' test in lib/hooks/__tests__/useToday.test.tsx (fails before, passes after). Run npx jest lib/hooks/__tests__/useToday.test.tsx to confirm no regression, and verify unmount clears the timer (assert clearTimeout / no pending fake timers after unmount). No NutritionContext test change is required for this ticket, but the corrected blast-radius note above should be relayed to whoever owns NutritionContext/nutritionScreen UX in case a follow-up guard is wanted.

Adjudication decisions:
  - NutritionContext/index.tsx:33-35 runs `useEffect(() => setSelectedDate(new Date()), [todayKey])` — a live state mutation, not a passive re-render — so after the fix a user viewing/editing a past date gets snapped to today mid-session at local midnight while foregrounded, with no app-state transition. The brief's blast radius claims consumers merely re-render and 'do NOT change any consumer.'
      -> Material. Verified directly against context/NutritionContext/index.tsx:33-35 — the effect exists exactly as described. The core fix in lib/hooks/useToday.ts remains correct and necessary (rule 1 unaffected), but the brief's blast-radius/edgeCases/preserved sections misstate a real, newly-reachable consumer-visible behavior change (rule 2, safety). Amend the brief's documentation to accurately describe this consequence rather than deny it. Do not expand the fix into NutritionContext's selectedDate-reset logic — that is an existing, deliberate design (todayKey change -> reset to today) outside this ticket's mandate, and altering it would add unrelated scope/risk (rule 4, least code) without being required for correctness. Flag as an accepted known consequence, with a one-line suggestion that product/UX may want a follow-up ticket if silently bumping an actively-viewed past date at midnight is undesirable.  (Rule 2 (safety/blast radius) for the documentation gap; rule 1 (correctness) confirms the fix itself does not need to change; rule 4 (least code) against expanding scope into NutritionContext.)
  - Duplicate of the above (second phrasing): same claim, same file:line, same underlying defect in blast-radius characterization.
      -> Same ruling as above — merged, not double-counted.  (Rule 2 (safety/blast radius))
  - Duplicate of the above (third phrasing): same claim, same file:line.
      -> Same ruling as above — merged, not double-counted.  (Rule 2 (safety/blast radius))
  - Duplicate of the above (fourth phrasing): same claim, same file:line.
      -> Same ruling as above — merged, not double-counted.  (Rule 2 (safety/blast radius))
  - Duplicate of the above (fifth phrasing, adds that nutritionScreen.tsx:25/34 reads selectedDate): same underlying claim, additional caller citation.
      -> Same ruling as above. The additional citation (nutritionScreen.tsx reading selectedDate) is consistent with and reinforces the same finding rather than raising a new one — folded into the amended blast-radius text as the concrete user-facing surface where the snap is visible.  (Rule 2 (safety/blast radius))

M15  (Medium)  [AUTHORED]
===============================================================================
[Author brief]

Root cause: Three central persistence state machines have zero direct tests, even though the repo already has an established, working pattern for testing exactly this class of code:

1. `context/SettingsContext/index.tsx:148-191` — the debounce-free "persistDirty" save effect. It encodes non-trivial re-entrancy rules purely in comments/refs: `persistSavingRef` guards overlap, `persistDirtyDuringSaveRef` re-queues an edit that lands mid-save (lines 159-164), a consecutive-failure counter drives `SETTINGS_ALERT_AFTER`-gated alerting + disk rollback (166-177), and `persistBackoffMs` schedules retries via `persistRetryNonce` (179). None of this is exercised by a test — only the *pure* helpers it calls (`computeBwUpdate`, `settingsToRow`/`rowToSettings`) are tested (`context/SettingsContext/functions/__tests__/*`, `context/SettingsContext/database/__tests__/powersyncStore.test.ts`).
2. `lib/powersync/orchestrator.ts:23-30,113-141` — the `mutexChain` promise-chain serializer (`enqueue`) that every connect/disconnect/kick call goes through, plus `kickPowerSync`'s foreground/throttle gate (`MIN_KICK_GAP_MS`). Zero test file exists for this module at all.
3. `context/WorkoutContext/database/powersyncStore.ts:117-312` — raw multi-statement `writeTransaction` order-bump logic (`insertWorkoutWithOrderBump`, `insertExercisesWithOrderBump`, the upsert-vs-insert branch duplicated once for workouts and once for exercises). Sibling stores (`SettingsContext/database`, `NutritionContext/database`) at least get row-mapping tests; this file — the largest of the three — has none.

This is both a logic-correctness risk (the interleaving rules above are exactly the kind of thing a future refactor breaks silently — e.g. collapsing the two upsert branches, or "simplifying" the mutex — with no test catching it) and a code-structure inconsistency (test coverage boundaries are drawn inconsistently across three parallel context/database pairs: two get DB-mapping tests and none get state-machine tests; the third gets nothing at all). The repo already has the two patterns needed to close this cheaply: a `Probe`-component-plus-`react-test-renderer` `act()` harness for provider effects (`context/BillingContext/__tests__/billingContext.test.tsx`, `lib/hooks/__tests__/useAsyncLoad.test.tsx` — the latter already tests the analogous "stale run" race for loads), and a mocked-`powerSync`-module harness for raw SQL (`context/SettingsContext/database/__tests__/powersyncStore.test.ts`).

Fix: Add three new test-only files reusing the two patterns above; make no production-code changes unless a test written this way actually fails (see the one candidate race called out below, which should be verified, not assumed).

**1. `context/SettingsContext/__tests__/persistEffect.test.tsx` (new)** — mock `../database/powersyncStore` (`upsertSettings`, `loadSettingsAndBw`, `upsertWeightForDate`), `@/lib/powersync/persistErrors` (`reportPersistFailure`, real `persistBackoffMs`), `@/context/AuthContext` (`useAuth` → fixed `userID`), and `@/lib/powersync/system` (`waitForFirstSync` resolved). Use a `Probe` component capturing `useSettings()` inside `SettingsProvider`, mounted via `react-test-renderer`'s `create`/`act` (mirrors `billingContext.test.tsx`).
   - *Re-entrancy*: make `upsertSettings` return a controllable promise. Call `setSettings` once (dirty), let the effect start saving, then call `setSettings` again while the first save is in flight. Resolve the first promise. Assert `upsertSettings` is called a second time with the *second* edit's payload and `persistDirty` ultimately settles to `false` (exercises `persistDirtyDuringSaveRef`, index.tsx:159-164 — currently untested, so a refactor could drop the re-queued edit and it would still pass CI today).
   - *Retry/backoff threshold*: `jest.useFakeTimers()`; make `upsertSettings` reject repeatedly; call `setSettings` once; advance timers through `persistBackoffMs(0)` then `persistBackoffMs(1)` (each retry re-fires the effect via `persistRetryNonce`). Assert `reportPersistFailure` is called exactly once, only after the 3rd consecutive failure, with `severity: 'high'` (index.tsx:174-177, `SETTINGS_ALERT_AFTER`).
   - *Onboarding keeps retrying past the alert*: same setup with `onboardingComplete: false`. Assert a 4th backoff timer is still scheduled after the alert fires (falls through instead of `return`ing at index.tsx:176) — this is the branch that distinguishes "stop and roll back" from "stay silent and keep retrying" and is the one piece of behavior a diff could invert without any test noticing.
   - *Rollback-vs-new-edit race*: after the 3rd failure triggers `reloadFromDisk` (mock `loadSettingsAndBw` to return a controllable promise), call `setSettings` again with a new edit *before* resolving that promise, then resolve it. Assert final `settings` reflect the new edit, not the stale disk snapshot. This is the same class of "stale async result overwrites a newer synchronous update" race `useAsyncLoad.test.tsx` already guards for the load path (`reports isStale() true to a run whose deps changed mid-flight`); nothing today guards the analogous rollback-read path in `reloadFromDisk` (index.tsx:62-74). **Write this test first and check whether it currently fails** — if it does, that's a real bug to route back through this same M15 ticket (or a fast-follow), not something to paper over in the test.

**2. `lib/powersync/__tests__/orchestrator.test.ts` (new)** — mock `@/lib/powersync/Connector` and `@/lib/powersync/system` (`powerSync.connect`/`disconnect`/`currentStatus`), and stub `AppState.currentState`.
   - *Mutex serializes overlapping calls*: call `ensurePowerSyncConnected(...)` and `disconnectPowerSync(...)` back to back without awaiting the first; make the underlying `powerSync.connect` resolve on a controllable promise. Assert `powerSync.disconnect` is not invoked until the `connect` promise resolves (the `mutexChain` contract at orchestrator.ts:21-30).
   - *Mutex survives a rejected task*: make the first enqueued task's underlying call reject. Assert a subsequently enqueued task still runs (the `.then(() => undefined, () => undefined)` recovery at lines 25-28) instead of the chain staying permanently rejected.
   - *Kick throttle*: call `kickPowerSync('watchdog_disconnected')` twice within `MIN_KICK_GAP_MS` with `AppState.currentState = 'active'`. Assert the first returns `'ok'` and calls `connect`/`disconnect` once each; the second returns `'throttled'` and touches neither (lines 118-122).
   - *Kick while backgrounded*: set `AppState.currentState = 'background'`; assert `kickPowerSync` returns `'background'` and never calls `powerSync.connect`/`disconnect` (lines 114-116).

**3. `context/WorkoutContext/database/__tests__/powersyncStore.test.ts` (new)** — mock `@/lib/powersync/system` exactly as `SettingsContext/database/__tests__/powersyncStore.test.ts` does; capture the callback passed to `powerSync.writeTransaction` and invoke it with a fake `tx` (`{ execute: jest.fn(), getAll: jest.fn() }`) to assert exact SQL/param sequencing.
   - *Order-bump ordering*: for `insertWorkoutWithOrderBump`, assert the `UPDATE ... archived = 0` bump executes before the `INSERT` within the same transaction call (powersyncStore.ts:117-130).
   - *Upsert branch selection — both copies*: with `tx.getAll` mocked non-empty, assert only the `UPDATE` executes; with it empty, assert only `INSERT` executes. Run this once against `upsertWorkout` (135-154) and once against `upsertExercise` (238-257) — **these are two hand-duplicated copies of the same fork, not shared code, so both must be tested and must change together** if this logic is ever refactored; a fix to one copy alone is not a complete fix.
   - *Batch bump uses the batch length, not a hardcoded 1*: for `insertExercisesWithOrderBump`, assert the bump `UPDATE`'s bound parameter equals `exercises.length` (lines 217-233) — this is the one function that diverges from the single-insert "+1" pattern used everywhere else in the file, so it's the one a copy-paste "harmonization" would most easily break silently.

No production code is modified by this brief unless the rollback-race test in (1) fails against current behavior, in which case that specific finding should be escalated as its own fix rather than folded into this test-coverage item.

Files to change:

M16  (Medium)  [DONE]

[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: The Dev Hub save-failure fault-injection switch (throwIfSaveFailureArmed('workout'), gated by components/devTest/ForceSaveFailureControls.tsx) is supposed to let a developer verify the alert + reload-rollback policy (reportPersistFailure -> reloadFromDisk) for every workout write. In reality guard coverage across context/WorkoutContext/database/powersyncStore.ts is inconsistent, not a clean store-vs-inline split as originally framed: only 3 of the 11 store-layer mutators call the guard (insertWorkoutWithOrderBump L118, insertExerciseWithOrderBump L200, insertLog L278). Seven other store functions that ARE already in the store layer have no guard at all (upsertWorkout L135 — rename/note; updateWorkoutOrders L159 — workout reorder; insertDuplicateWorkout L175 — duplicate; insertExercisesWithOrderBump L217 — bulk add, called directly by handleAddExercises, not 'implicitly via callers' as originally claimed; upsertExercise L238 — already dead/unreferenced elsewhere; updateExerciseOrders L262 — exercise reorder; upsertUserExercise L292 — create user exercise). On top of that, six delete/archive handlers in context/WorkoutContext/index.tsx embed raw powerSync.writeTransaction/execute SQL directly in the provider (L94-98 delete workout cascade, L109-117 archive/unarchive workout, L211-214 delete exercise cascade, L232-241 & L255-264 archive/unarchive exercise, L327 delete log, L354 delete user exercise) and never call the guard either. Net effect: essentially all workout writes except add-workout/add-single-exercise/add-log are unexercisable by the Dev Hub save-failure switch — a materially larger gap than 'delete/archive only'. The drift also left `upsertExercise` imported at index.tsx:18 but never called from production code (only reference was the unused import); this is already flagged separately in docs/PRODUCTION_READINESS_AUDIT.md's dead-code cluster and is not something to resolve inside M16.

Fix: Consolidate all writes behind the store layer AND close the guard-coverage gap end-to-end, in two parts. (1) Relocation, unchanged from the original plan for the 6 provider-inline SQL blocks: move them verbatim into new named store functions — deleteWorkoutCascade(id), setWorkoutArchived(id, userID, archived), deleteExerciseCascade(id), setExerciseArchived(id, workoutID, archived), deleteLogRow(id), deleteUserExerciseRow(userID, name) — each opening with `if (__DEV__) await throwIfSaveFailureArmed('workout')`, exactly like the existing insert functions; the provider awaits these inside its existing try/catch (reportPersistFailure + reloadFromDisk unchanged). IMPORTANT correction: for setWorkoutArchived and setExerciseArchived, copy the existing `if (archived) { ...unarchive SQL... } else { ...archive SQL... }` block bodies byte-for-byte from index.tsx (L110-117 and L232-264) — do NOT re-derive the branch logic from the function name. `archived=true` in the existing code means 'this item IS currently archived, so run the unarchive branch' (confirmed by archiveWorkout in workoutFunctions.tsx: `archived: !archived`); add an inline comment stating this explicitly so an implementer wiring by name doesn't invert both twin SQL pairs. (2) Scope expansion required for correctness: add the same one-line `if (__DEV__) await throwIfSaveFailureArmed('workout')` as the first line of the 7 already-existing store functions that currently lack it — upsertWorkout, updateWorkoutOrders, insertDuplicateWorkout, insertExercisesWithOrderBump, upsertExercise, updateExerciseOrders, upsertUserExercise. This is a trivial, __DEV__-gated, no-op-in-production one-line insertion per function — no SQL, parameter, or transaction-boundary changes — and it is what actually closes the harness-coverage gap the issue is about (rename, note, duplicate, bulk-add, both reorders, create-user-exercise become exercisable, matching the 6 relocated delete/archive paths). Do not delete or rewire upsertExercise's zero-caller status in this issue — flag it for the coordinator instead (see blastRadius). Do not alter any SQL text, parameter order, or datetime('now') usage anywhere.

M17  (Medium)  [DONE]
===============================================================================
[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: context/NutritionContext/database/powersyncStore.ts maintains the entry pipeline and the saved-meal pipeline as two parallel copies. The two ingredient tables (nutrition_entry_ingredients, saved_nutrition_entry_ingredients) are structurally identical except for the parent-FK column name (verified AppSchema.ts:72-84 vs 103-115), so three blocks were copy-pasted instead of parameterized: (1) the items.map item-mapping (27-35 vs 76-84, byte-identical), (2) the load-time child-row fetch + group-into-Map with its null-parent-FK guard (116-127 vs 136-147), and (3) the upsert child DELETE + re-INSERT loop (185-193 vs 226-234). A column addition to ingredients must therefore land in up to four places, and any missed copy makes entry vs saved-meal behavior silently diverge. The divergence in these three blocks is purely the parent-FK identifier; there is no intended behavioral difference. (Note: crudFunctions.tsx:19,55 also hardcode DELETE-by-parent-id against these two tables, but those lines carry no column list and are unaffected by column-addition duplication — out of scope for this fix, flagged for awareness only.)

Fix: Consolidate the three duplicated blocks into private helpers inside the same file (no new module/dependency): (1) rowsToItems(rows: (NutritionEntryIngredientRecord | SavedNutritionEntryIngredientRecord)[]): Item[] replacing the inline items.map at 27-35 and 76-84; (2) a GENERIC loadItemsGrouped<T extends Record<string, unknown>>(childTable: string, parentColumn: string, parentIds: string[]): Promise<Map<string, T[]>> replacing the IN-clause fetch + grouping at 118-127 and 138-147 — the helper itself checks `parentIds.length === 0` and returns an empty Map with no query (guard lives INSIDE the helper, not at the call site, so callers invoke it unconditionally: `await loadItemsGrouped<NutritionEntryIngredientRecord>('nutrition_entry_ingredients', 'nutrition_entry_id', entryIds)` / `await loadItemsGrouped<SavedNutritionEntryIngredientRecord>('saved_nutrition_entry_ingredients', 'saved_nutrition_entry_id', savedEntryIds)`); the helper must also skip any row where `item[parentColumn]` is falsy before grouping, preserving the existing null-parent-FK guards (lines 122/142) exactly; (3) replaceItems(tx, childTable, parentColumn, parentId, items) replacing the DELETE + INSERT loop at 185-193 and 226-234 (single home for uuid()/datetime('now')/sanitizeMacro item-write — this is a pure refactor, the sanitizeMacro(item.quantity) call is carried over verbatim, not changed, by this fix). Deliberately do NOT merge the parts with intended differences: rowToNutritionEntry (parses row.date, real time) vs rowToSavedNutritionEntry (date:new Date(), time:0); the two ToRow parent column lists (entry has date/time, saved omits them); and the two parent SELECTs whose ORDER BY differ (date DESC,time DESC vs created_at DESC). Public signatures of loadNutritionData/upsertNutritionEntry/upsertSavedNutritionEntry/rowTo*/…ToRow are unchanged. Add one-line comment above each new helper per CLAUDE.md convention.

Files to change:
  - context/NutritionContext/database/powersyncStore.ts
      Add private helpers rowsToItems (item mapping, replaces 27-35 & 76-84), generic loadItemsGrouped<T> (child fetch+group with internal empty-parentIds guard and internal null-parent-FK skip, replaces the Map-building in 116-149), and replaceItems (child DELETE+INSERT, replaces 185-193 & 226-234, sanitizeMacro(item.quantity) call carried over unchanged); rewrite the four call sites to delegate, instantiating loadItemsGrouped's generic with the concrete record type at each call site (no casts, no union types reaching rowToNutritionEntry/rowToSavedNutritionEntry). Leave parent mappers, parent SELECTs+ORDER BY, and parent UPDATE/INSERT column lists per-table (intended divergence).
  - context/NutritionContext/database/__tests__/powersyncStore.test.ts
      Extend: add a loadNutritionData grouping test (mock powerSync.getAll) proving ingredients route to the correct parents and null-parent-FK rows are skipped; add a replaceItems/upsertNutritionEntry test asserting per-item DELETE-then-INSERT still fires. Do NOT add a direct rowsToItems test — it stays private/unexported and is already pinned indirectly via the existing rowToNutritionEntry/rowToSavedNutritionEntry brand round-trip tests (110-130).

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    Public signatures unchanged, so all consumers untouched: context/NutritionContext/index.tsx:47,62 (loadNutritionData), :77,95,132 (upsertNutritionEntry), :114 (upsertSavedNutritionEntry), plus the test file imports of rowToNutritionEntry/rowToSavedNutritionEntry/nutritionEntryToRow/savedNutritionEntryToRow. Grep across app/ components/ lib/ context/ confirms no other importers of these four functions. New helpers are private (unexported), adding zero external surface. Separately noted for completeness: context/NutritionContext/functions/crudFunctions.tsx:19,55 also hardcode DELETE-by-parent-id against the same two child tables, but do not call into powersyncStore.ts and carry no column list, so they are unaffected by this refactor and out of scope.
  Edge cases: Empty items ([].map -> []; replaceItems runs DELETE then zero-iteration loop = current behavior). No entries: the empty-parentIds guard now lives inside loadItemsGrouped itself (returns empty Map, no query), so no empty IN() clause is possible regardless of call-site discipline. Null parent-FK rows: loadItemsGrouped must replicate the existing item[parentColumn]-truthy guard before grouping (currently at lines 122/142) so malformed/partially-synced rows are still skipped, not keyed under undefined. Null brand/quantity fallbacks (?? null, ?? 1) preserved verbatim. Decimal calories: real columns + sanitizeMacro at what becomes replaceItems' single call site are carried over unchanged by this refactor and must not regress C2's decimal-calorie fix. Offline/concurrent-write: replaceItems runs inside the existing writeTransaction, no transaction boundary change. Generic typing: loadItemsGrouped<T> must be instantiated with the concrete record type at each call site so rowToNutritionEntry/rowToSavedNutritionEntry continue to receive correctly-typed (non-union) arrays under TS strict.
  Preserved:  Both ingredient tables have byte-identical item columns (AppSchema.ts:72-84 vs 103-115), so parameterizing on childTable/parentColumn yields identical SQL and identical mapped objects. The intentionally-divergent parts (date/time handling, ORDER BY, parent column lists) are left un-merged. The two rowTo* and two *ToRow functions keep their signatures, so existing brand round-trip (test 110-130) and sanitization (test 26-80) tests act as the behavior pin and must pass unchanged.
  Tests:      Run npx jest context/NutritionContext/database/__tests__/powersyncStore.test.ts (existing brand round-trip + sanitization cases pin item-mapping and toRow, and now also indirectly pin the private rowsToItems helper). Add: (1) loadNutritionData grouping test via mocked powerSync.getAll, covering both the empty-parentIds short-circuit and the null-parent-FK skip (newly covers the untested load path); (2) replaceItems/upsertNutritionEntry test asserting per-item child DELETE+INSERT. COORDINATOR OVERLAPS: H9 (docs/PRODUCTION_READINESS_AUDIT.md:73-75, tagged logic-correctness) requires removing sanitizeMacro from item.quantity at powersyncStore.ts:191,232 — the exact lines this fix centralizes into replaceItems; items.ts is unrelated to H9 and must not be cited when coordinating (items.ts is a read-side item-math aggregator, not touched by H9's write-path fix). This M17 refactor makes no behavior change to that call (carries it over verbatim), but whoever lands H9 second should expect to edit inside replaceItems rather than at two separate line pairs. C2's decimal-calories fix touches the same centralized item-write lines — coordinate so fixes don't collide. Dead-code cluster (audit 127): loadNutritionData's unused hasData return is in the same function but is a separate issue — flag, don't fold in.

Adjudication decisions:
  - Test plan is self-contradictory: it prescribes a direct unit test for `rowsToItems` while also declaring the helper private/unexported ('zero external surface'); the test file only imports the four public mappers, so no import path exists for a direct case. (Duplicate findings at lines 3 and 3 of the test file.)
      -> MATERIAL. Amended: drop the 'direct rowsToItems unit test' item from the test plan. rowsToItems stays private/unexported (as designed) and is exercised indirectly through the existing rowToNutritionEntry/rowToSavedNutritionEntry brand round-trip tests (powersyncStore.test.ts:110-130), which already pin identical item-mapping behavior for both record types. No new export is introduced solely to satisfy a test.  (Rule 5 (testability) triggered a genuine internal contradiction in the brief that would block a competent implementer from following the plan as written; corrected without changing the fix's public surface.)
  - Internal tension in loadItemsGrouped spec: the fix folds the IN-clause fetch into the helper while separately requiring the caller to 'keep the non-empty-parentIds guard' beside a fetch expression that no longer exists at the call site — risks an implementer producing a malformed `IN ()` on an empty parentIds array.
      -> MATERIAL. Amended: the empty-parentIds guard moves INSIDE loadItemsGrouped itself (check `parentIds.length === 0` first, return an empty Map without querying). Call sites invoke `await loadItemsGrouped(...)` unconditionally — no external ternary required. This removes the ambiguity entirely rather than relying on caller discipline.  (Rule 2 (safety) — an empty IN() clause would be a runtime SQL error; centralizing the guard inside the helper is also the smaller/cleaner change (rule 4).)
  - The shared loadItemsGrouped is specified to return Map<string, rows[]> over a union of NutritionEntryIngredientRecord | SavedNutritionEntryIngredientRecord, but its consumers (rowToNutritionEntry / rowToSavedNutritionEntry) require non-union, table-specific array types under TypeScript strict — the brief never specifies generics or a cast, so the refactor as literally described would fail to typecheck. (Duplicate findings at lines 12 and 12.)
      -> MATERIAL. Amended: loadItemsGrouped is specified as a generic function — `function loadItemsGrouped<T extends Record<string, unknown>>(childTable: string, parentColumn: string, parentIds: string[]): Promise<Map<string, T[]>>` — instantiated at each call site with the concrete record type (`loadItemsGrouped<NutritionEntryIngredientRecord>(...)` and `loadItemsGrouped<SavedNutritionEntryIngredientRecord>(...)`). This satisfies rowToNutritionEntry/rowToSavedNutritionEntry's existing typed parameters with zero casts and zero type erosion.  (Rule 1 (correctness, hard gate) — a fix description that does not compile does not fix the issue; the union-vs-generic gap had to be closed.)
  - The load-path grouping currently skips rows whose parent FK is null before using it as a Map key (item.nutrition_entry_id / item.saved_nutrition_entry_id guards at lines 122/142); the brief's description of loadItemsGrouped does not call out preserving this null-parent guard when the key becomes a dynamic item[parentColumn] lookup.
      -> MATERIAL. Amended: fix text now explicitly requires loadItemsGrouped to skip any row where `item[parentColumn]` is falsy before grouping, preserving the exact behavior of the current per-table null guards rather than leaving it implicit / at risk of being dropped during parameterization.  (Rule 2 (safety) — dropping the guard would key a Map on undefined for partially-synced rows, a real regression risk on a currently-correct behavior.)
  - The coordinator-overlap note mislabels the co-owned audit item as 'H9/items.ts single-owner (audit line 74 flags 191,232 under item-math lens)'. Verified against docs/PRODUCTION_READINESS_AUDIT.md:73-75: H9 is tagged (logic-correctness), cites powersyncStore.ts:191,232 + lib/utils/number.ts + context/NutritionContext/functions/entryBuilders.ts, and requires REMOVING sanitizeMacro from item.quantity because it silently corrupts data on re-save. items.ts (verified: 'Single owner of item math' comment at items.ts:3) is a read-side totals/scaling aggregator never mentioned by H9. The brief's edgeCases also states 'sanitizeMacro at 191/232 unchanged' — directly at odds with what H9 requires on those same lines. (Three duplicate findings covering this same defect.)
      -> MATERIAL. Amended: coordinator-overlap note corrected to read: 'H9 (audit:73-75, logic-correctness) requires removing sanitizeMacro from item.quantity at the exact lines (191, 232) this fix centralizes into replaceItems; items.ts is unrelated to H9 and must not be cited. M17 should preserve whatever sanitizeMacro(item.quantity) behavior exists at merge time verbatim inside replaceItems (a pure refactor, no behavior change) — sequencing with H9 is a coordination matter for whoever lands second, not a guarantee that the call stays forever.' This removes the false items.ts citation and removes the brief's own pre-commitment to keep quantity-rounding 'unchanged' that conflicts with H9's mandate.  (Rule 2 (safety) — an inaccurate coordination pointer risks misdirecting whoever resolves H9 to the wrong file, and the brief's own 'unchanged' language directly contradicts a known, verified, in-scope sibling fix on the same lines.)
  - crudFunctions.tsx (deleteNutrition:19, unsaveNutrition:55) also hardcodes cascade DELETEs against the same two ingredient tables by parent FK, a call-site pairing not enumerated in the brief's blast radius or root-cause 'up to four places' count.
      -> IMMATERIAL to the fix itself — dismissed from the code change, but added as a one-line blastRadius disclosure. Verified: these two lines only ever reference the table name and FK column in a DELETE-by-id, with no item column list, so they are unaffected by the column-addition duplication risk the root cause is actually about (adding a column to `items` never touches these two lines). Folding them into replaceItems' scope would expand blast radius for no correctness benefit and violate the brief's own 'no new module/dependency, minimal change' framing.  (Rule 4 (least code) governs once rule 1 confirms these lines are outside the actual root cause (column duplication) — the finding is valid as a disclosure/completeness note (added to blastRadius text) but must not be folded into the code change.)

[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: Two separate type holes let unchecked shapes flow into typed state. (A) RevenueCat offerings are declared `any` in the BillingContext interface, the provider state, and the two package-picker helpers, so `offerings.current.availablePackages` is never checked against the SDK's real `PurchasesOfferings` shape — a future SDK rename compiles fine and only fails at runtime in the paywall's package lookup. (B) The PowerSync row->domain mappers non-null-assert several identity/name columns. CORRECTED SCOPE (per adjudication): PowerSync's generated `RowType<T>` intersects `{...columnMap} & { id: string }`, so `id` is force-typed non-null and is not a real hole — `row.id!` is a cosmetic no-op. The load queries in both loaders are `WHERE user_id = ?`-scoped, so a returned row's `user_id` can never be null either — that guard is dead code on these call sites. The genuinely reachable nulls are: (1) `name` on nutrition entries, saved entries, workouts, and exercises (declared `column.text`, i.e. `string | null`), (2) the ingredient/item `name` in BOTH item maps — rowToNutritionEntry (line 28) and rowToSavedNutritionEntry (line 77), not just the latter, and (3) `workout_id` on rowToExercise, which — unlike logs (already protected by the existing `workoutIds.has(...)` Set-filter at loadWorkoutData lines 94-96) — has no downstream filter today, so a null FK exercise silently flows into app state.

Fix: Part A unchanged: replace `any` with `PurchasesOfferings | null` across BillingContext/types.ts, index.tsx, and functions/billingFunctions.tsx. Part B narrowed and corrected: default `name` (row-level, all four mappers) and `item.name` (BOTH item maps, lines 28 and 77) to `''` instead of asserting; drop the null-id/null-user_id guards from scope entirely (unreachable, per adjudication); add a `.filter` after the exercises `.map(rowToExercise)` at line 91 to drop rows with a null `workout_id` (the one real unguarded FK); for rowToLog's workout_id/exercise_id, fix the type-level assertion for honesty but rely on the existing lines 94-96 Set-membership filter rather than adding new drop+filter logic (already redundant). Export rowToWorkout/rowToExercise/rowToLog from WorkoutContext/database/powersyncStore.ts (matching the existing NutritionContext convention of exporting its mappers) so the new test file can call them directly.

Adjudication decisions:
  - Brief's fix list for NutritionContext/database/powersyncStore.ts only patches the item.name! non-null assertion at line 77 (rowToSavedNutritionEntry's item map) and omits the identical item.name! at line 28 (rowToNutritionEntry's item map) — reported 4x by different reviewer passes.
      -> MATERIAL. Verified both occurrences in the actual file: line 28 `name: item.name!` in rowToNutritionEntry and line 77 in rowToSavedNutritionEntry are byte-identical holes over the same nullable `column.text` ingredient-name field. Fixing only one leaves the root cause half-addressed and the two mappers behaving inconsistently for the same corrupt-row scenario. Final brief's file-change entry is amended to explicitly cover both: 'Item map (28 and 77): item.name ?? ""'.  (Rule 1 (correctness — fix must resolve the stated root cause fully, not half of it))
  - The proposed new WorkoutContext test file plans to directly call rowToWorkout/rowToExercise/rowToLog to 'pin' their null-drop behavior, but these are declared as module-private `function` (no `export`) at lines 14, 28, 43 — only loadWorkoutData is exported.
      -> MATERIAL. Confirmed by reading the file: none of the three mappers has an `export` keyword, unlike the sibling NutritionContext/database/powersyncStore.ts where rowToNutritionEntry/rowToSavedNutritionEntry ARE exported and directly unit-tested today. Amending the fix to add `export` to all three WorkoutContext mappers (matching the existing NutritionContext convention) is the minimal change that makes the brief's own test plan executable, rather than requiring a powerSync mock through loadWorkoutData.  (Rule 5 (testability) and Rule 3 (convention fit — mirror the already-exported NutritionContext mapper pattern))
  - RootCause claims 'every column is T | null' and lumps `row.id!` in with genuinely-nullable columns, but PowerSync's generated `RowType<T>` is `{...columnMap} & { id: string }` — id is force-typed non-null regardless of columnMap, and it is not declared in any of the app's Table columnMaps (checked AppSchema.ts) so it's purely the intersected primary key. `row.id!` is a redundant no-op assertion, not a real hole.
      -> MATERIAL. Verified in node_modules/@powersync/common/lib/db/schema/Table.d.ts:31-35 (`RowType<T> = {...} & { id: string }`) and Column.d.ts:17 (`text: BaseColumnType<string | null>` — applies only to declared columns, not id). RootCause narrative is amended to state id is guaranteed non-null by the generated type (and by PowerSync's PK semantics), so `row.id!` is cosmetic-only. The 'return null for null id' guard and its paired test case are dropped from scope — they would pin a state the type system already makes unreachable, which is dead/misleading test surface.  (Rule 1 (correctness of the diagnosis) and Rule 4 (least code — don't add guards/tests for impossible states))
  - The proposed 'drop row on null user_id' guard is dead code on these specific load paths: both loadNutritionData and loadWorkoutData select with `WHERE user_id = ?` (and the saved-entries/exercises/logs queries do the same), and SQL's NULL-inequality semantics guarantee a returned row's user_id always equals the non-null bound parameter.
      -> MATERIAL. Verified all four queries (nutrition_entries, saved_nutrition_entries, workouts, exercises, logs, user_exercises) are `WHERE user_id = ?`-scoped, and grep confirms the private mappers are called only from these loaders (no other call sites exist). The null-user_id branch can never fire today. Dropped from fix scope; the final brief keeps only the genuinely reachable nulls (name, and the specific FK case below).  (Rule 4 (least code — remove unreachable branch) and Rule 5 (testability — don't add a test for a state that can't occur))
  - rowToLog's proposed workout_id!/exercise_id! null-drop guard duplicates existing protection: loadWorkoutData already does `logRows.map(rowToLog).filter((log) => workoutIds.has(log.workoutID) && exerciseIds.has(log.exerciseID))` (lines 94-96), and Set.has(null) is false, so a log with a null FK is already excluded from the returned array today.
      -> MATERIAL but low-severity. Verified the filter exists exactly as described and already produces the correct end result even though it currently works 'by accident' (the `!` assertion lies to the type system, but the Set-membership check still evaluates correctly on the real null value). Ruling: keep a type-honest fix in rowToLog (replace the `!` assertions with a safe typed default) for TS correctness, but do NOT add new return-null-and-re-filter machinery for logs — the existing filter at 94-96 is sufficient and already covers this; no new test case is required to 'pin' this specific path since current behavior is already correct and unchanged.  (Rule 4 (least code — don't duplicate existing protection) and Rule 2 (safety — smallest blast radius, avoid touching working logic))
  - rowToExercise's `workoutID: row.workout_id!` is the one FK-null case with NO downstream protection (unlike logs) — exerciseRows.map(rowToExercise) at line 91 has no subsequent .filter, so an exercise row with a null workout_id flows unfiltered into app state today.
      -> MATERIAL — this is the actual reachable defect on the workout side, more precise than the brief's original blanket 'id/user_id/FK' framing. Verified: line 91 `const exercises = exerciseRows.map(rowToExercise);` has no filter, unlike logs. This must remain in scope and is now the headline Part-B fix for WorkoutContext: rowToExercise should drop (or the loader should filter) exercises with a null workout_id, since a group-by-workout view or a rename/dedupe keyed on workoutID would otherwise silently misbehave on a corrupt/partial sync.  (Rule 1 (correctness — this is the one FK hole the fix must actually close))
  - Changing rowToNutritionEntry/rowToSavedNutritionEntry's return type to `NutritionEntry | null` breaks the existing (non-added) 'brand round-trip' tests at powersyncStore.test.ts:112-128, which dereference `entry.items[0].brand` directly on the mapper's return value with no null-narrowing — this fails under strict `tsc` even though jest-expo (Babel) won't catch it at runtime.
      -> MATERIAL. Verified: lines 112-128 call `rowToNutritionEntry(entryRow, ...)`/`rowToSavedNutritionEntry(savedRow, ...)` and immediately do `entry.items[0].brand` with no guard, and the project's tsconfig (strict: true, includes `**/*.ts`) covers test files. This is a real, unmentioned consumer the brief's blastRadius missed. Amended: the test-file change item for NutritionContext must also update these four existing assertions to null-narrow (e.g. `expect(entry).not.toBeNull(); expect(entry!.items[0].brand)...`) alongside adding the new null-id/name-default cases.  (Rule 1 (correctness — don't break existing typed callers) and Rule 2 (safety/blast radius))

[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: Four `lib/` modules reach upward into `context/`, inverting the intended layering (lib = bottom, context = above it). Two distinct mistakes: (1) `lib/notifications/scheduler.ts` and `lib/notifications/builders.ts` type-import pure domain shapes `NutritionEntry`/`NutritionStreakState` from `context/NutritionContext/types.ts` — these are plain data shapes with no context dependency, simply filed too high; (2) `lib/hooks/useNotificationScheduler.ts` (imports `useNutrition()`) and `lib/hooks/useCombineName.ts` (imports `joinItemNames` from `context/NutritionContext/functions/entryBuilders`) are nutrition-domain hooks that legitimately consume context — they don't belong in generic `lib/hooks/`. Fix is directional: move the data types DOWN into `lib/`, move the two hooks UP into `context/`.

Fix: Split the four files by which direction resolves the inversion. Do NOT collapse entryBuilders.ts's single-owner name-rules contract; do NOT touch the ~17 files that reference the nutrition types via the context path.

TYPES DOWN:
- Create `lib/nutrition/types.ts` holding the three pure shapes currently at context/NutritionContext/types.ts:9-39 (`Item`, `NutritionEntry`, `NutritionStreakState`); they reference only primitives/each other, so this file has zero context imports and creates no cycle.
- In `context/NutritionContext/types.ts`: (a) add `import type { Item, NutritionEntry, NutritionStreakState } from '@/lib/nutrition/types'` at the top so `NutritionContextInterface` (which uses NutritionEntry at lines 42/43/49/51/52 and NutritionStreakState at line 58) keeps a valid local binding — a bare `export type {...} from '...'` re-export does NOT create one, so this import is REQUIRED, not optional; (b) delete the three interface bodies; (c) add a local `export type { Item, NutritionEntry, NutritionStreakState }` (no `from` clause needed, since step a already imported them) so all existing importers of `@/context/NutritionContext/types` keep resolving unchanged.
- Repoint lib/notifications/builders.ts (type-only import, line 1) and lib/notifications/scheduler.ts (type-only import, line 1) to `@/lib/nutrition/types`.
- Repoint the two colocated tests: lib/notifications/__tests__/builders.test.ts LINE 1 (imports both NutritionEntry and NutritionStreakState) and lib/notifications/__tests__/scheduler.test.ts LINE 11 (imports NutritionStreakState only — this file's context-type import sits after a jest.mock('expo-notifications') block on lines 1-9, so line 2 is jest.mock content, not the import).

M25  (Medium)  [DONE]
===============================================================================
[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: Full-screen builder modals are registered with `presentation: 'modal'` + `gestureEnabled: true` (app/_layout.tsx modalPresentation, applied at lines 177-190) so a downward swipe (or hardware back) pops them immediately with no confirm. This affects at least 7 staged/typed-input screens, not the 3 the banked brief scoped: foodDBModal (addedItems), addExerciseModal (selectedItems), editEntry (edited name/rows), addNutritionModal (hasContent — mealName/macros, already computed at line 37), savedNutritionModal (addedItems via StagedSection), updateBWModal (typed weight), and renameModal (typed workoutName). Additionally, the banked brief's proposed mechanism — a hand-rolled `navigation.addListener('beforeRemove', ...) + e.preventDefault()` — is not proven anywhere in this codebase to actually cancel an interactive native-stack modal swipe: its two cited precedents don't demonstrate it (subscription.tsx's screen inherits gestureEnabled:false so its guard never faces a swipe; analyzingModal's listener never calls preventDefault at all), and react-navigation's own native-stack package explicitly warns that plain beforeRemove-based prevention 'is not fully supported in native-stack', recommending `usePreventRemove` instead — which is also the only API that wires the native `preventNativeDismiss` flag that actually cancels the in-flight swipe gesture on iOS.

Fix: Build `lib/hooks/useDiscardGuard.ts` on top of react-navigation's `usePreventRemove(shouldPrevent, callback)` (not a manual `addListener`), and apply it to all 7 affected screens (the original 3 plus addNutritionModal, savedNutritionModal, updateBWModal, renameModal).

Hook shape: `useDiscardGuard(isDirty: boolean): { markSaved: () => void }`.
Internals:
- `const [saved, setSaved] = useState(false)`
- `const router = useRouter()`
- `const discardingRef = useRef(false)` — cheap re-entrancy guard for the Discard path
- `usePreventRemove(isDirty && !saved, (e) => { if (discardingRef.current) { discardingRef.current = false; navigation.dispatch(e.data.action); return } Alert.alert('Discard changes?', 'You have unsaved items. Leaving now will discard them.', [{ text: 'Keep editing', style: 'cancel' }, { text: 'Discard', style: 'destructive', onPress: () => { discardingRef.current = true; navigation.dispatch(e.data.action) } }]) })`
- `useEffect(() => { if (saved) router.back() }, [saved])` — the intentional pop is deferred into an effect keyed off `saved` so it only fires after React commits the render where `usePreventRemove`'s boolean has actually flipped to false. (Setting a bypass ref and calling `router.back()` synchronously in the same tick — the banked brief's approach — does NOT work with `usePreventRemove`, because its internal listener is memoized off the value passed on the last COMMITTED render; a same-tick ref mutation is invisible to it until a re-render happens. Confirmed by tracing usePreventRemove's useLatestCallback wiring in node_modules.)

Per-screen wiring: call `const { markSaved } = useDiscardGuard(isDirty)` near the other hooks; in each screen's intentional-save handler, replace the direct `router.back()` call with `markSaved()`:
- foodDBModal.tsx: isDirty = addedItems.length > 0; replace router.back() in handleAddAll (~line 167).
- addExerciseModal.tsx: isDirty = selectedItems.length > 0; replace router.back() in handleAddAll (~line 73). Quick-create sheet is a nested Modal with its own dismissal — out of scope, unaffected.
- editEntry.tsx: add a `dirty` boolean flipped true on first mutation (name onChangeText / setField / stepQty / addItem / removeItem); replace router.back() in handleSave (~line 121).
- addNutritionModal.tsx: reuse the existing hasContent (line 37) directly as isDirty; replace the single router.back() (~line 57).
- savedNutritionModal.tsx: isDirty = addedItems.length > 0 (same pattern as foodDBModal); replace its save-path router.back().
- updateBWModal.tsx: isDirty = weight.trim().length > 0; replace its save-path router.back() (~line 68).
- renameModal.tsx: isDirty = workoutName.trim() !== (workout?.name ?? '').trim(); replace its save-path router.back() (~line 43).

Deliberately NOT touched: app/_layout.tsx — gestureEnabled: true stays; usePreventRemove wires the native preventNativeDismiss flag automatically per-screen via context, no Stack.Screen option changes needed. subscription.tsx / analyzingModal.tsx keep their existing bespoke, unrelated guards.

DevHub plan (devHubApproval = pending, corrected): FoodDBTest.tsx does NOT exercise the real foodDBModal — it opens /devTest/foodDBVariant → FoodDBModalPreview, a separate teaser clone whose handleAddAll never navigates. Don't claim it verifies the foodDB guard. Verify the foodDB guard either on-device against the real shipped screen, or via a NEW thin devTest launcher built the same way as EditEntryTest (router.push the real nutritionScreens/foodDBModal route) rather than through FoodDBModalPreview. AddExerciseTest.tsx (new, per original plan) should likewise router.push() the real addExerciseModal route, not a cloned preview. The rationale 'the isolated DevHub host cannot drive the swipe event directly' does not hold for real-route launchers (EditEntryTest already proves a real swipe fires beforeRemove there); keep a 'simulate dismiss' DevControls button only as a simulator/CI convenience, not because it's structurally required.

Files to change:
  - lib/hooks/useDiscardGuard.ts
      NEW — usePreventRemove-based hook returning { markSaved }. Blocks a beforeRemove attempt while isDirty && !saved via Alert (Keep editing / destructive Discard that re-dispatches e.data.action, with a discardingRef re-entrancy guard); defers the intentional pop to a useEffect keyed on `saved` so Save/Add is never gated by a stale closure.
  - app/nutritionScreens/foodDBModal.tsx
      Call useDiscardGuard(addedItems.length > 0); replace router.back() in handleAddAll (~line 167) with markSaved().
  - app/workoutScreens/addExerciseModal.tsx
      Call useDiscardGuard(selectedItems.length > 0); replace router.back() in handleAddAll (~line 73) with markSaved().
  - app/nutritionScreens/editEntry.tsx
      Add a `dirty` state flipped true on first mutation; call useDiscardGuard(dirty); replace router.back() in handleSave (~line 121) with markSaved().
  - app/nutritionScreens/addNutritionModal.tsx
      NEW scope (was missing from banked brief). Call useDiscardGuard(hasContent) reusing the existing line-37 signal; replace the single router.back() (~line 57) with markSaved().
  - app/nutritionScreens/savedNutritionModal.tsx
      NEW scope (was missing from banked brief). Call useDiscardGuard(addedItems.length > 0); replace the save-path router.back() with markSaved().
  - app/nutritionScreens/updateBWModal.tsx
      NEW scope (was missing from banked brief). Call useDiscardGuard(weight.trim().length > 0); replace router.back() (~line 68) with markSaved().
  - app/workoutScreens/renameModal.tsx
      NEW scope (was missing from banked brief). Call useDiscardGuard(workoutName.trim() !== (workout?.name ?? '').trim()); replace router.back() (~line 43) with markSaved().
  - components/devTest/AddExerciseTest.tsx
      NEW DevHub page — router.push() the REAL addExerciseModal route (EditEntryTest pattern, not a FoodDBModalPreview-style clone) + simulate-dismiss control; register stub app/devTest/addExercise.tsx, Stack entry, GROUPS entry in DevHub.tsx.
  - lib/hooks/__tests__/useDiscardGuard.test.ts
      NEW — unit test with a mocked usePreventRemove/useNavigation/Alert: (a) isDirty=false → no preventDefault/Alert; (b) isDirty=true → Alert shown with destructive Discard; (c) Discard onPress → navigation.dispatch(e.data.action) called; (d) markSaved() defers router.back() to the following effect tick rather than firing synchronously; (e) a re-entrant beforeRemove during Discard short-circuits via discardingRef without re-alerting.

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    The hook is consumed only by the 7 modals listed above, each already inside the modal Stack in app/_layout.tsx (gesture-enabled entries at lines 177-190), so usePreventRemove resolves against the same navigator/route context these screens already run in. No context, shared component, or persist path (handleAddNutrition, handleAddExercises, handleEditNutrition, handleAddEntry, weight-save, rename-save) changes — each screen's single router.back() call site is swapped for markSaved(), preserving the exact same eventual navigation once unblocked. app/_layout.tsx itself is untouched; every other modal's swipe behavior is unaffected.
  Edge cases: Intentional Save/Add is not gated: markSaved() flips `saved` to true, which (a) changes the boolean passed to usePreventRemove on the next render so the guard is inert, and (b) triggers the useEffect that performs the actual router.back() only after that render commits — avoiding the stale-closure race that would occur if back() were called synchronously in the same tick as the state flip. Clean/untouched modal → isDirty false → usePreventRemove never preventDefaults, zero behavior change. Discard confirm re-dispatches e.data.action; a discardingRef short-circuits any re-entrant firing straight through without re-alerting (defensive; the base preventDefault+redispatch pattern is react-navigation's own documented 'Preventing going back' recipe and is not itself suspected broken). Nested quick-create sheet in addExerciseModal uses its own RN Modal, not the navigator, so it doesn't interact with the guard; a completed create still lands in selectedItems and is covered. Alert is OS-native, correct in both color schemes and at large Dynamic Type with no token work. renameModal/updateBWModal dirty signals are simple string comparisons against their known initial values, avoiding any deep-equality machinery.
  Preserved:  Existing behavior is preserved for every non-dirty case (the overwhelming majority of modal exits): the guard is a no-op whenever the relevant boolean is false, and every save handler explicitly clears the block via markSaved() before the deferred back(). The only new user-visible behavior is a confirm Alert when leaving one of the 7 screens with unsaved/staged content via swipe, header, or hardware back — matching the existing confirm-while-busy precedent in subscription.tsx in spirit, though that screen's own mechanism (gestureEnabled:false + bare beforeRemove) is not itself reused since it never had to face an interactive swipe.
  Tests:      New lib/hooks/__tests__/useDiscardGuard.test.ts (jest-expo) covering the five cases in the file-list entry above. Run the existing devTest hosts (FoodDBTest as corrected, EditEntryTest, plus new AddExerciseTest) in Dev Hub for light/dark + staged-vs-clean scenarios on all 7 screens; run npm run test:ci to confirm no regressions in the nutrition/workout suites. Recommend at least one on-device manual swipe-to-dismiss check on a physical/simulated iOS build per screen, since usePreventRemove's native preventNativeDismiss wiring is a native-layer effect that Jest cannot exercise.
  DevHub:     approval=pending; plan={"applicable":true,"components":"useDiscardGuard consumed by foodDBModal, addExerciseModal, editEntry, addNutritionModal, savedNutritionModal, updateBWModal, renameModal","testPage":"Existing FoodDBTest.tsx (corrected to launch the real foodDBModal route, not FoodDBModalPreview) and EditEntryTest.tsx; NEW AddExerciseTest.tsx (real-route pattern, mirroring EditEntryTest) + stub/registration","scenarios":["Stage/type content then trigger dismiss (swipe or simulate-dismiss control) -> confirm Alert appears with destructive 'Discard'","Tap 'Keep editing' -> staged/typed state preserved, verified in both dark and light","Tap 'Discard' -> screen closes, no repeated Alert","Clean/untouched modal -> dismiss with no Alert (guard inert)","Save/Add path -> closes with no Alert (markSaved bypass works, no stale-closure block)"],"expected":"Guard fires only when the screen's isDirty signal is true; Save/Add always completes; on-device swipe test confirms the native interactive gesture is actually cancelled (via preventNativeDismiss), not just the JS-side state.","naReason":""}

But it's a one-line, zero-risk defensive addition, so it is folded in as cheap insurance: the hook adds a discardingRef checked first in the callback so a re-entrant beforeRemove firing during the Discard dispatch short-circuits straight to navigation.dispatch instead of re-alerting. M26  (Medium)  [DONE]

[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: On the AI-analysis waiting screen (app/nutritionScreens/analyzingModal.tsx), the only way to abort an in-flight analysis that can take up to 30 seconds is an undiscoverable gesture. The cancel mechanism already exists and works correctly: the beforeRemove navigation listener (lines 31-36) flips canceledRef.current = true, which both the success path (line 86) and the failure/catch path (line 90) honor, and the analysis callback (line 84) uses to short-circuit. But that listener only fires on swipe-down dismiss or hardware back, neither of which is discoverable on an iOS-first app with only a passive, unlabeled drag handle (lines 123-126). The screen shows a 30s progress bar/spinner/tip with no tappable exit, so a user who wants to bail is stranded. This is a discoverability hole, not a missing behavior — the fix surfaces the existing cancel path.

Fix: Add a visible, tappable 'Cancel' control to app/nutritionScreens/analyzingModal.tsx that invokes the existing cancel path. Do NOT add a new cancel mechanism.

1. Render a TouchableOpacity text button after the tips block (~line 163), inside the existing `content` View, whose onPress calls router.back() (already in scope, line 15). This fires the existing beforeRemove listener -> canceledRef.current = true -> the in-flight analysis's keepGoing() returns false and both the success guard (line 86) and catch guard (line 90) suppress further navigation/Alert — identical outcome to today's swipe-dismiss, now discoverable.

2. GUARD AGAINST DOUBLE-TAP (amended per adjudication): analyzingModal is reached via router.push from cameraScreen.tsx:154-155, so cameraScreen remains mounted underneath during the ~300ms dismiss animation. A second onPress firing router.back() again during that window would pop cameraScreen too, landing the user on tabs instead of the camera preview. Wrap the onPress with the existing single-flight guard from lib/hooks/useSubmitOnce.ts (do not write a new debounce ref — this hook's own doc comment describes exactly this double-tap-during-close failure mode). Use the default (non-retryable) mode since the screen is navigating away.

3. STYLE (amended): match this file's own existing convention — hardcoded numeric radius/spacing values (see tipsContainer at lines 267-277: borderRadius:12, paddingVertical:12, paddingHorizontal:20 — all plain numbers, not tokens). Do NOT import radius/spacing from ThemeContext; the file does not use those tokens anywhere today and doing so would (a) contradict the 'only new import is TouchableOpacity' goal and (b) be inconsistent with the surrounding code. Use colors.labelMuted or colors.textSecondary for the label via the existing useColors() (line 17) and fonts.medium via the existing fonts import (line 3) — both already in scope, so the only new import needed is TouchableOpacity, added to the existing react-native import (line 9).

4. LAYOUT (amended): the brief's original 'place at the bottom of content, respecting paddingBottom:32 for the home indicator' claim is inaccurate — styles.content (lines 189-195) is flex:1/alignItems:'center' with no justifyContent, so children stack top-down and a button appended after tips renders directly below it, not pinned to the screen bottom. More importantly, content is a plain non-scrolling View inside a container with overflow:'hidden' (line 176); the existing stack (200px photo + icon + title + subtitle + progress bar + spinner + tips) is tall enough that on short devices (iPhone SE) or at largest Dynamic Type, adding another element risks silent clipping — a button that renders unreachable defeats the fix's own discoverability goal. Wrap the current content children in a ScrollView (contentContainerStyle taking over the existing `content` style) so the Cancel button is always reachable by scrolling even if it doesn't fit above the fold on smaller/larger-text configurations. The outer container's overflow:'hidden' is unaffected (it exists for the rounded top corners) and stays as-is.

5. Accessibility + touch target (reuse the pattern already used in cameraScreen.tsx:195-239): accessibilityRole="button", accessibilityLabel="Cancel analysis", minHeight >= 44 (never a fixed sub-44pt height) plus hitSlop.

Copy: "Cancel" (matches the existing failure-Alert cancel button at line 106).

DEV HUB PLAN (required, visual/interactive change) — see devHubPlan field.

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    analyzingModal is pushed from cameraScreen.tsx (scan flow, router.push at line 154 — confirmed the underlying screen is NOT replaced, so it stays mounted) and from the devTest ScanCancelTest page.   Edge cases: 1) Tap during in-flight request: beforeRemove sets canceledRef, keepGoing() aborts, success/catch guards (lines 86, 90) suppress dismissAll/Alert. 2) Tap after success but before dismissAll runs: canceledRef guard already covers this; router.back() is harmless once popping. 3) Rapid double-tap: NOW GUARDED by useSubmitOnce (amended) — without this guard, a second router.back() during the ~300ms dismiss animation would pop the still-mounted cameraScreen underneath, landing the user on tabs instead of the camera preview; this was the brief's original incorrect 'no-op' claim, now corrected. 4) Cancel vs. the failure Alert's own Cancel (line 106): independent; Alert only mounts on the catch path and the guard prevents it after cancel. 5) Small screens / largest Dynamic Type: NOW GUARDED by wrapping content in a ScrollView (amended) — without it, the button could be clipped and unreachable under the container's overflow:'hidden', silently defeating the fix's discoverability goal. 6) Safe-area: content's existing paddingBottom:32 still applies inside the ScrollView's contentContainerStyle.

M27  (Medium)  [DONE]

[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: Saving a logged meal to "My Meals" is entirely silent. The Entry-options Alert's "Save" item in app/nutritionScreens/nutritionScreen.tsx:44-48 calls handleSaveNutrition with no follow-up UI, and handleSaveNutrition (context/NutritionContext/index.tsx:101-118) returns Promise<void>, so the screen cannot confirm success. With zero feedback the user assumes the tap failed and taps again; each re-tap flows through savedMealName() (index.tsx:15-22), which auto-appends ' (2)'/' (3)', silently minting duplicate copies. The auto-numbering itself is intended (dev note: dedup logic is fine, must not be touched) - the defect is the missing confirmation. AMENDMENT: the originally banked fix resolved success as soon as the local optimistic save succeeded (saveNutrition returns true), before the async upsertSavedNutritionEntry settles. If that upsert later throws, reportPersistFailure (lib/powersync/persistErrors.ts:56-63) rolls the entry back via reloadFromDisk AND fires its own 'Couldn't save your change' alert, colliding with the already-shown 'Saved to Meals' alert and leaving a false success claim for an entry that no longer exists. Symmetrically, the banked null (validation-failure) branch would have stacked a new 'Couldn't Save' alert on top of validateNutritionEntry's own 'Invalid Input' alert (context/NutritionContext/functions/validator.tsx:27-33), which already fires on that same failure. Both gaps are closed by awaiting the full save path (including the async upsert) and treating every failure mode as 'already alerted elsewhere - resolve null, no additional alert.'

Fix: (1) context/NutritionContext/index.tsx: export the existing pure helper savedMealName unchanged (adds testability, zero behavior change - no logic touched). Change handleSaveNutrition to await the full path before resolving: if saveNutrition(savedEntry, setSavedNutritionEntries) returns false -> resolve null (validateNutritionEntry already alerted 'Invalid Input'; no extra alert needed). If !userID (offline/no-auth) -> resolve savedEntry.name immediately (no upsert attempted, matches existing offline gating, unchanged). Otherwise await upsertSavedNutritionEntry(savedEntry) in the existing try/catch: on success resolve savedEntry.name; on throw, call reportPersistFailure exactly as today (it rolls back via reloadFromDisk and shows its own alert) and resolve null - do NOT show a second alert for this case either. (2) context/NutritionContext/types.ts:52: signature becomes (nutritionEntry: NutritionEntry) => Promise<string | null>, where null now uniformly means 'already handled/alerted elsewhere, no caller action needed' (covers both validation failure and persist failure), and a string means the entry was actually saved (locally and, when online, persisted) under that final name. (3) app/nutritionScreens/nutritionScreen.tsx lines 44-48: make Save's onPress async, await handleSaveNutrition(nutritionEntry); if result is null, do nothing (the failure was already surfaced by validateNutritionEntry or reportPersistFailure - adding a third alert would be a stacked/contradictory alert, the exact bug reviewers found); if result differs from the trimmed input name, Alert.alert('Saved a Copy', ...) disclosing it was saved as '<name>' because a meal with that name already existed; otherwise Alert.alert('Saved to Meals'). Reuses the existing Alert.alert convention already used across nutritionScreens; no toast library or new component. (4) Add a test in a NEW file context/NutritionContext/__tests__/savedMealName.test.ts (NOT crudFunctions.test.ts, since crudFunctions.tsx's saveNutrition has no naming logic - the numbering lives only in index.tsx) importing the now-exported savedMealName directly: first save of 'Chicken' against [] returns 'Chicken'; second save against a list already containing 'Chicken' returns 'Chicken (2)'. Optionally add a second, provider-level test asserting handleSaveNutrition resolves null on a macro-invalid entry and a string on a valid one. Deliberately untouched: savedMealName's numbering logic itself, saveNutrition/crudFunctions, offline gating, reportPersistFailure, and savedNutritionModal (does not call this handler). DevHub: devHubApproval = 'na' - the only added surface is the OS-native Alert.alert, consistent with sibling native-Alert feedback in this folder; no themed component/color/radius/type is introduced.

Files to change:
  - context/NutritionContext/index.tsx
      Add `export` to savedMealName (15-22, no logic change). Rewrite handleSaveNutrition (101-118) to await the full path (including upsertSavedNutritionEntry) before resolving: null on saveNutrition()===false; savedEntry.name immediately when !userID; after awaiting the upsert, savedEntry.name on success or null on catch (reportPersistFailure still runs unchanged on catch).
  - context/NutritionContext/types.ts
      Line 52: handleSaveNutrition signature -> (nutritionEntry: NutritionEntry) => Promise<string | null>, where null means 'failure already alerted elsewhere, no caller alert needed' (validation OR persist failure).
  - app/nutritionScreens/nutritionScreen.tsx
      Lines 44-48: Save onPress becomes async; await handleSaveNutrition; null -> no-op (already alerted); name !== trimmed input -> 'Saved a Copy' disclosure; otherwise -> 'Saved to Meals'. No alert on the null branch (removes the previously planned redundant 'Couldn't Save, please try again' alert, which would have stacked on top of validateNutritionEntry's/reportPersistFailure's own alerts).
  - context/NutritionContext/__tests__/savedMealName.test.ts
      New test file (replaces the originally proposed crudFunctions.test.ts location) importing the now-exported savedMealName and asserting the base-name-then-(2) numbering contract directly; optionally a provider-level test for handleSaveNutrition's null/string resolution.

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    handleSaveNutrition has exactly one consumer: the Save item in app/nutritionScreens/nutritionScreen.tsx:47 (verified - only index.tsx definition and nutritionScreen.tsx usage exist repo-wide). savedNutritionModal.tsx does not call it. Changing void->Promise<string|null> and awaiting the upsert internally is additive for the sole caller (which previously ignored the return and fired-and-forgot); no other consumer is affected. types.ts:52 is the only type declaration to update.
  Edge cases: Offline / no userID: unchanged - local save still resolves synchronously with the name, no upsert attempted, success alert shows correctly. Validation failure: saveNutrition returns false -> resolves null -> validateNutritionEntry's own 'Invalid Input' alert is the only alert shown (no duplicate). Async persist failure (network/Supabase upsert throws after local optimistic save): reportPersistFailure rolls the entry back and shows its own 'Couldn't save your change' alert; handleSaveNutrition now resolves null so the caller shows nothing further - no false 'Saved to Meals' claim for data that was just reverted, and no contradictory double alert. This is pre-existing behavior unrelated to the duplicate-feedback root cause of M27 (logged entries reaching this handler already carry a name from their creation flow) and is out of scope for this fix - flagged here only to correct the banked brief's earlier (incorrect) claim that empty names are already rejected by this path. Rapid double-tap / stale-closure race: savedMealName's dedup check reads the savedNutritionEntries snapshot captured in handleSaveNutrition's useCallback deps; two saves fired within the same React render cycle (before state commits) could theoretically both compute against the same stale list and produce two identically-named entries with no disclosure. This is a pre-existing property of the dedup mechanism (not introduced by this fix) and the Save action can only be re-invoked by reopening the parent Options Alert.alert (which dismisses on every button press), making the window in practice far too narrow for a human double-tap to hit. Per the developer's explicit instruction not to modify savedMealName/the dedup mechanism, this is treated as a known, non-blocking residual risk rather than something this fix must close.
  Preserved:  savedMealName's numbering logic, saveNutrition/crudFunctions, upsertSavedNutritionEntry, offline gating, reportPersistFailure, and savedNutritionModal are all untouched. Existing behavior is unchanged except for the newly added await + Alert on the single caller. No theme tokens, layout, or navigation change.

Re-run npx jest context/NutritionContext to confirm no regression in existing crud/aiFunctions tests. Adjudication decisions:
  - Return savedEntry.name 'on successful local save' while treating the async persist-failure path as untouched, so a failed Supabase upsert produces a false 'Saved to Meals' confirmation for a meal that reportPersistFailure then rolls back (raised independently 5 times across the findings list, e.g. lines 36-40, 42-46, 84-88, 96-100, 102-106).
      -> MATERIAL and ACCEPTED. No new name-validation code was added since M27's root cause is duplicate-save feedback, not name validation, and entries reaching this handler already carry a name from their creation flow (out of scope; developer note also only blessed the numbering logic, not name validation).       -> MATERIAL and ACCEPTED. Verified validator.tsx:27-33 already alerts on validation failure. This also resolves the async persist-failure double-alert (see decision 1) with the same mechanism.  (Rule 1 (correctness) and Rule 4 (least code - one failure-alert convention instead of two overlapping ones).)
