M28  (Medium)  [DONE]
===============================================================================
[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: app/settingsScreens/notifications.tsx models OS permission as a single boolean `granted` (line 24), collapsing three distinct iOS states -- granted, undetermined (in-app prompt still works), and denied/blocked (must go to Settings). (1) `showDenied = prefs.enabled === false && !granted` (line 51) keys on stored intent rather than real OS state, so in the audit's actual revoke scenario (`prefs.enabled === true`, OS later revokes) the hint never appears. (2) `toggleMaster` calls `(await isPermissionGranted()) || (await requestPermission())` (line 42); once iOS has hard-denied, this returns false with no prompt shown, and `if (!ok) return` (line 44) dead-ends silently. Additionally (found in adjudication and confirmed material): the screen only ever fetches permission state once on mount (`useEffect(..., [])`, lines 27-30) with no foreground/focus refresh, so even after the fix adds a Settings deep-link, a user who grants permission in iOS Settings and returns to the (still-mounted) screen will see a stale OFF switch and a stale 'turned off in iOS Settings' hint, and a stale-state-driven toggleMaster would bounce them back to Settings again instead of enabling -- this needs a live tap-time recheck and a foreground refresh, not just a mount-time fetch.

Fix: Track `canAskAgain` alongside `granted`, refresh it live (not just at mount), and route correctly:

1) lib/notifications/permissions.ts -- add `getPermissionState(): Promise<{granted: boolean; canAskAgain: boolean}>` reading `Notifications.getPermissionsAsync()`. Leave `isPermissionGranted`/`requestPermission` untouched (used by scheduler.ts:36 and NotificationsTest.tsx).

2) app/settingsScreens/notifications.tsx:
   - Add `const [canAskAgain, setCanAskAgain] = useState(true)`. In the mount effect, replace `isPermissionGranted().then(setGranted)` with `getPermissionState().then(s => { setGranted(s.granted); setCanAskAgain(s.canAskAgain) })`.
   - ADDED (correctness amendment): register `AppState.addEventListener('change', ...)` in the same effect; on transition to 'active', re-run `getPermissionState()` and update both `granted`/`canAskAgain`, removing the subscription on unmount. This mirrors the existing foreground-refresh pattern already used in components/GuardComponents/SyncWatchdog.tsx, and is what makes the Settings deep-link actually self-heal the UI when the user returns from a real grant.
   - Fix the hint condition: `const showDenied = !granted && !canAskAgain`.
   - Rewrite toggleMaster: when enabling and not granted, first call `getPermissionState()` LIVE (not stale component state) to decide `nextPermissionAction({canAskAgain})`: if `'settings'` -> `openAppSettings()` and return; if `'request'` -> `requestPermission()`, then `setGranted(ok)`, and if declined `setCanAskAgain(false)`. Only call `update({...prefs, enabled: value})` once permission is actually granted (or when turning OFF). This preserves the original code's live-recheck behavior (which the banked-brief version had silently dropped) so a real Settings-app grant is honored on the very next tap instead of bouncing the user back to Settings.
   - Import `nextPermissionAction, openAppSettings` from @/lib/utils/permissions; use `openAppSettings` for the denied-text onPress (replacing the direct `Linking.openSettings()` call).
   - ADDED (Dev Hub fidelity amendment): extract the switch + conditional hint markup (currently inline, lines ~55-65) into a small local, prop-driven component, e.g. `NotificationMasterCard({enabled, granted, canAskAgain, onToggle, onSettingsPress})`, imported by both NotificationsScreen and the Dev Hub test page -- so the Dev Hub preview renders the actual shipped component rather than a hand-duplicated copy.

This mirrors the established camera pattern (cameraScreen.tsx:74-83: needsSettings ? 'Open Settings'/openAppSettings : 'Grant Permission'/requestPermission). Deliberately NOT touched: saveNotificationPrefs/runNotificationReschedule flow, per-meal and motivation toggles, scheduler.ts (its own isPermissionGranted gate at line 36 already blocks scheduling when revoked).

Files to change:
  - app/settingsScreens/notifications.tsx
      Add canAskAgain state; load {granted,canAskAgain} via getPermissionState in the mount effect AND on AppState foreground transitions (new, corrects a correctness-gate finding); fix showDenied to `!granted && !canAskAgain`; rewrite toggleMaster to do a live getPermissionState() check at tap time before branching on nextPermissionAction (openAppSettings when blocked, requestPermission()+setCanAskAgain(false) on decline, update() only when granted or turning off); import nextPermissionAction/openAppSettings from @/lib/utils/permissions; extract the switch+hint markup into a small prop-driven NotificationMasterCard component (new, corrects a Dev Hub fidelity finding) reused by the screen and the Dev Hub test page.
  - lib/notifications/permissions.ts
      Add getPermissionState(): Promise<{granted; canAskAgain}> reading Notifications.getPermissionsAsync(); leave existing isPermissionGranted/requestPermission unchanged.
  - components/devTest/NotificationsTest.tsx
      Add a DevControls permission-state segmented toggle {granted|undetermined|denied-blocked} that renders the extracted NotificationMasterCard (the real shipped component) driven by local simulated granted/canAskAgain props, with stubbed onToggle/onSettingsPress loggers -- this is genuinely new UI on this page (it currently has only imperative action buttons, no rendered card/DevControls), and light/dark testing uses the existing GLOBAL DevHub.tsx toggle, not a page-local one.
  - lib/notifications/__tests__/permissions.test.ts
      Add/extend unit test asserting getPermissionState maps status='granted'->granted:true and forwards canAskAgain for granted/undetermined/denied cases; nextPermissionAction is already covered in lib/utils/__tests__/permissions.test.ts.

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    Only NotificationsScreen renders this card. permissions.ts is imported by scheduler.ts:36 (isPermissionGranted, unchanged) and NotificationsTest.tsx (isPermissionGranted/requestPermission, unchanged); getPermissionState is a new additive export with no existing callers. lib/utils/permissions.ts (nextPermissionAction/openAppSettings) is already used by cameraScreen.tsx -- no signature change. The new AppState listener and NotificationMasterCard extraction are both scoped to this single screen/its Dev Hub twin; no context, persistence, or navigation touched.
  Edge cases: Fresh install (undetermined, canAskAgain true) -> showDenied false, tapping enable shows the real OS prompt. First-time in-app decline flips canAskAgain->false, hint appears immediately. OS-revoke-after-enable (audit case) -> switch OFF, hint visible, tap deep-links to Settings. Grant-via-Settings-and-return (the case surfaced in adjudication) -> AppState 'active' listener re-fetches permission so switch/hint self-correct without requiring a remount, and toggleMaster's live tap-time recheck prevents a stale-state Settings-bounce-loop. Turning master OFF never calls permission APIs. `prefs === null` early return still guards before any of this renders.
  Preserved:  Switch still shows `prefs.enabled && granted`; per-meal and motivation toggles, save/reschedule, and DateTimePicker rows untouched. scheduler.ts still self-gates on isPermissionGranted.   Tests:      Add getPermissionState mapping test in lib/notifications/__tests__/permissions.test.ts (mock Notifications.getPermissionsAsync for granted / undetermined / denied-blocked). Manual (required, RN AppState mocking is too brittle to pin reliably in jest): Dev Hub NotificationsTest scenarios in light and dark; on a real device, revoke notifications in iOS Settings, confirm the hint appears, tap through to Settings, re-grant, return to the app, and confirm the switch/hint self-correct via the AppState refresh and that toggling the switch now enables notifications instead of bouncing back to Settings.
  DevHub:     approval=pending; plan={"applicable":true,"components":"The extracted NotificationMasterCard (switch value logic + conditional Settings hint), previewed inside components/devTest/NotificationsTest.tsx","testPage":"components/devTest/NotificationsTest.tsx -- already registered via the existing route stub app/devTest/notifications.tsx and the existing 'Notifications' entry in DevHub.tsx's GROUPS array (lines 72-75); note: there is no app/devTest/_layout.tsx anywhere in the repo, correcting the banked brief's citation.","scenarios":["granted, light & dark -> switch ON, no hint","undetermined, light & dark -> switch OFF, no hint; tapping shows the OS-prompt path (stubbed), not the Settings text","denied-blocked (the revoke case), light & dark -> switch OFF, hint visible with adequate contrast in both schemes, tapping invokes the stubbed openAppSettings rather than doing nothing","simulated grant-after-blocked transition (toggle the DevControls segmented state from denied-blocked to granted) -> NotificationMasterCard re-renders correctly from the new props, verifying the props-driven design that the real screen's AppState listener will drive on-device","confirm switch + tappable hint meet the 44pt target"],"expected":"All five scenarios render correctly in both light and dark via the props-driven NotificationMasterCard, matching the description in each scenario above.","naReason":""}

M29  (Medium)  [DONE]
===============================================================================
[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: Font scaling (Dynamic Type) is enabled app-wide, but several text-bearing rows and CTAs pin an exact pixel `height` (some with `overflow:'hidden'`), so at large accessibility text sizes the text renders taller than the container and is clipped. Three sub-cases: (a) containers that SHOULD grow with the text — the Log workout/exercise row, the onboarding Back/Next CTAs in the shared OnboardingScaffold, AND (newly identified) paywall.tsx's own independent footer/cta buttons, which are NOT routed through OnboardingScaffold and share the identical fixed-height-plus-centered-Text anti-pattern; (b) deliberately compact square controls aligned to an input row (logsModal 50x50 add/date buttons) where growing would break row alignment, so the fix caps font scaling instead; (c) the progress `graphCard` height:400 is a chart container, not a text-clipping case, and — per adjudication — should NOT be changed, because doing so trades a non-existent clipping bug for an unverified risk of the nested flex:1 chart collapsing when sibling header/subtitle text grows under a now-indefinite (minHeight-only) parent height.

Fix: Per-case, not a blanket swap:

1) Log row (grow it): in `components/WorkoutComponents/Log.tsx`, change the fixed `height: 96` on `styles.wrapper` (line ~79) to `minHeight: 96`, and change the inline `{ height: wrapperHeight }` at line 27 to `{ minHeight: wrapperHeight }`. Keep `paddingVertical: 16`, `numberOfLines={textLines}`/subtitle `numberOfLines={1}`, `overflow:'hidden'`, and prop names/defaults unchanged.

2) Onboarding CTAs (grow them) — TWO locations, both required for correctness:
   2a) `components/NeutralComponents/OnboardingScaffold.tsx`: change `height: 58` to `minHeight: 58` on `backButton` (line 77) and `nextButton` (line 79); add `paddingVertical: 12` to each.
   2b) `app/onboardingScreens/paywall.tsx` (added on adjudication — paywall does not use OnboardingScaffold's footer): change `cta` (line 226, currently `height: 58, overflow: 'hidden'`), `backButton` (line 240, `height: 58`), and `laterButton` (line 242, `height: 58`) from `height: 58` to `minHeight: 58`, adding `paddingVertical: 12` to `backButton`/`laterButton` to match the OnboardingScaffold treatment. `cta`'s inner `ctaGradient` already uses `flex:1` with centered content so it grows correctly once the outer view is no longer height-locked.

3) logsModal compact square controls (cap them): in `app/workoutScreens/logsModal.tsx`, add `allowFontScaling={false}` to the three glyph `Text` nodes: `addButtonText` ('+', JSX ~195), `dateButtonMonth` (~166), `dateButtonDay` (~167). Matches the in-repo precedent in `components/NeutralComponents/CalendarMonthGrid.tsx:56,90`. Leave the fixed 50x50 heights as-is. (Note: `inputsRow` uses `alignItems:'flex-end'`, not center — corrected from the original brief; does not change the fix.)

4) progress `graphCard` — NO CHANGE. Reverted on adjudication. `app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:350`'s `height: 400` stays as-is. `chartContainer` (line 368) is `flex:1` and Graph1's own internal wrapper is also `flex:1` with no independent height source, so switching to `minHeight:400` would remove the definite main-size the flex:1 chart currently relies on to get any leftover space, risking a collapsing/vanishing chart when `cardHeader`/`cardSubtitle`/`rangeStrip` text grows under Dynamic Type — a worse and unproven regression versus the status quo (chart merely renders smaller, no clipped glyphs since there's no fixed-height overflow:hidden Text container in this card). Do not touch this file for M29.

Never hardcode new colors/radii; all edits are dimension-only and reuse existing tokens already in these files. Verify in both dark and light schemes.

- Components under test: WorkoutComponents/Log row, OnboardingScaffold footer CTAs, paywall.tsx footer/cta, logsModal add/date button row.

Adjudication decisions:
  - OnboardingScaffold blast-radius claim ('touches all of them uniformly, goals->paywall') is false — paywall.tsx renders its own footer (backButton/laterButton, both height:58) and its own cta (height:58, overflow:hidden) independent of OnboardingScaffold; these are unaffected by the OnboardingScaffold edit and will still clip at large Dynamic Type.
      -> MATERIAL — accepted and folds into the fix. Verified at app/onboardingScreens/paywall.tsx:226,240,242: cta/backButton/laterButton all pin height:58 with centered Text (cta additionally has overflow:'hidden'), the exact same clipping anti-pattern already being fixed elsewhere in this issue. Since paywall is a real onboarding screen (goals->...->paywall per CLAUDE.md) with the same root-cause pattern, leaving it out means the fix does not fully resolve the described bug class. Amended fix: apply the same 'grow' technique used on OnboardingScaffold — change cta/backButton/laterButton height:58 -> minHeight:58, add paddingVertical:12 to backButton/laterButton (matching OnboardingScaffold's treatment) and keep cta's ctaGradient flex:1 centering (already handles growth once outer height is not fixed). This is a same-pattern, same-file-class addition, not scope creep.  (Rule 1 (correctness, hard gate) — an incomplete fix that leaves a known instance of the same bug unfixed fails the gate regardless of how clean the rest of the diff is.)
  - Brief attributes Log-row drag re-measurement risk to 'DraggableFlatList,' but the codebase uses DraggableList.tsx wrapping react-native-reorderable-list (ReorderableList) — no react-native-draggable-flatlist import exists.
      -> MATERIAL for documentation accuracy, immaterial to the code diff. Verified at components/WorkoutComponents/DraggableList.tsx:3 (`import ReorderableList ... from 'react-native-reorderable-list'`). The actual Log.tsx style edit (height->minHeight) is unaffected — the risk being described (a reordering list needing to re-measure a grown row's height when dragging) is real regardless of which library is used, so the edge-case verification instruction stands, only the library name is wrong. Amended: correct blastRadius/edgeCases and devHubPlan wording to say 'DraggableList (react-native-reorderable-list)' instead of 'DraggableFlatList.'  (Rule 5 (testability) — accurate library naming is needed so the reviewer verifying drag behavior on the Dev Hub page tests the right component; does not change rule 1-4 outcome since no code edit changes.)
  - blastRadius claims logsModal inputsRow uses alignItems:'center' so a taller TextInput 'centers rather than clips' next to the fixed 50x50 chips; actual style is alignItems:'flex-end'.
      -> MATERIAL for documentation accuracy, immaterial to the code fix. Verified at app/workoutScreens/logsModal.tsx:334 (`alignItems: 'flex-end'`). The chosen fix (allowFontScaling={false} on the three glyph Text nodes, capping the compact chips) is unaffected by this correction — capping font scaling on the glyphs prevents overflow regardless of the row's cross-axis alignment. Amended: correct the prose to 'flex-end' and drop the now-inaccurate 'centers rather than clips' framing (chips are pinned to the bottom of the row via flex-end, not centered — this doesn't change whether the fix works, only the stated reasoning).  (Rule 3/5 (convention fit / testability — accurate rationale) — cosmetic correction to the brief's prose, no change to rule 1/2 outcome.)
  - blastRadius claims graphCard 'minHeight in a ScrollView resolves to exactly 400 (no competing flex)... chart dimensions are unchanged.' chartContainer is literally flex:1 (competing), and graphCard contains growing text siblings (cardHeader/cardTitle/cardSubtitle, rangeStrip) above/around it, so chart height is not invariant at large Dynamic Type.
      -> MATERIAL — this is not just a wrong justification, it exposes a real regression risk in item 4 of the fix that forces an amendment: DROP the progress.tsx graphCard change entirely (revert to leaving height:400 as-is, no edit). Verified: app/(tabs)/progress.tsx:368-370 chartContainer is `flex:1`, and Graph1.tsx's own internal wrapper (its immediate child) is ALSO `flex:1` (components/GraphComponents/Graph1.tsx:205-207) with no independent height source — the chart's rendered size is derived entirely from the flex chain rooted at graphCard. Under CSS/Yoga flexbox, a flexBasis:0/flexGrow:1 child only expands into 'leftover' space computed against a *definite* parent main-size; switching graphCard from a fixed `height:400` to `minHeight:400` removes that definite size. If cardHeader/cardSubtitle/rangeStrip text grows enough under Dynamic Type to reach or exceed 400px on their own, chartContainer (and Graph1's nested flex:1 wrapper) can end up with little-to-no leftover space to grow into and the chart can shrink toward collapse instead of gracefully scaling down as it does today with a fixed height. That is a genuine, unverified new failure mode (a vanishing/squashed chart) introduced by a change the author's own brief already flagged as 'least impactful ... include for consistency only,' with no proven upstream clipping bug in graphCard to justify the risk (there is no fixed-height, overflow:hidden Text container inside graphCard analogous to the other three cases — the chart is a canvas that resizes, it does not clip glyphs). Net: the safest, smallest, most correct choice is to leave app/(tabs)/progress.tsx untouched for this issue.  (Rule 1 correctness (the 'no competing flex / dimensions unchanged' premise is disproven, so the fix's own safety claim cannot be relied on) and Rule 2 safety/blast-radius (do not trade a cosmetic 'consistency' edit for an unverified risk of a collapsing chart) override Rule 4 least-code in the other direction here — the least-risk AND least-code option is to drop this edit, not keep it.)

Root cause: `handleDeleteAccount` (app/settingsScreens/profile.tsx:120-138) presents one flat `Alert.alert` with a single destructive 'Delete' button — the same one-tap weight used to delete a single set — for an irreversible, account-wiping action. There is no second confirmation stage, and no disclosure that deleting the account does NOT cancel the App Store subscription (RevenueCat/Apple bill independently), so a deleted-account user can keep being charged. The sibling file already contains a mechanically-reusable nested-Alert pattern (handleSignOut, lines 77-118 — note: that nesting is itself an error-recovery branch for a failed upload flush, not a true double-confirm precedent, but the Alert-inside-onPress mechanics are directly reusable).

Fix: Entirely inside `handleDeleteAccount` in app/settingsScreens/profile.tsx. Add `const { hasPremium } = useBilling()` (import `useBilling` from `@/context/BillingContext`, already exported at context/BillingContext/index.tsx:152/165). Replace the single alert with a two-stage flow:

Stage 1 (disclosure + intent, gated on hasPremium): `Alert.alert('Delete Account', message, buttons)` where `message` is always 'This permanently deletes your account and all your data and can't be undone.' PLUS, only when `hasPremium` is true, an appended sentence: 'Deleting your account does not cancel your subscription.' Buttons: `{ text: 'Cancel', style: 'cancel' }`; when `hasPremium` is true, also `{ text: 'Manage Subscription', onPress: () => Linking.openURL(Platform.select({ ios: 'https://apps.apple.com/account/subscriptions', android: 'https://play.google.com/store/account/subscriptions', default: 'https://apps.apple.com/account/subscriptions' })) }` — mirroring subscription.tsx:85-92's `handleManage` exactly (inline duplication or a one-line shared constant are both acceptable; do not block on which) — this button does not proceed to stage 2; and `{ text: 'Continue', style: 'destructive', onPress: <opens stage 2> }`. Stage 1 never calls `deleteAccount()`.

Stage 2 (final confirm, unconditional): a second `Alert.alert('Are you absolutely sure?', 'This can't be undone. Your account and all data will be permanently deleted.', [{ text: 'Cancel', style: 'cancel' }, { text: 'Delete Account', style: 'destructive', onPress: async () => { ...existing setDeleteLoading/try/await deleteAccount()/catch/finally body moved verbatim... } }])`.

Import additions: `useBilling` from `@/context/BillingContext`, `Platform` and `Linking` from `react-native` (Alert already imported). No other files change. Deliberately NOT touched: deleteAccount() in context/AuthContext/functions/accountFunctions.tsx, the disabled/loading guard on the button, the Sign Out flow, any theming/tokens. Do NOT introduce a type-DELETE text-entry gate.

DevHub plan: devHubApproval = 'na'. The entire change remains native `Alert.alert` copy plus a `Linking.openURL` call — system-rendered, no themed component, nothing to preview in light/dark. To exercise the free-vs-premium copy/button branch during manual QA, reuse the existing `lib/devtools/forceFreeMode.ts` toggle (already read by BillingContext/index.tsx:133 via `useForceFreeMode()`) rather than building a new Dev Hub harness — this is the established in-repo mechanism for flipping hasPremium, so no new components/devTest file is needed.

M31  (Medium)  [DONE]
===============================================================================
[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: Once a user completes Apple Sign-In, the route guard in app/_layout.tsx:162-173 traps them in the onboarding stack until settings.onboardingComplete flips (only the paywall sets it). None of the onboarding screens expose a sign-out affordance: OnboardingScaffold only renders an optional Back button + primary CTA, and the entry screen app/onboardingScreens/goals.tsx:28 has no Back at all. A user who signed in with the wrong Apple ID has no way out except fabricating a profile through to the paywall or deleting the app. signOut is already available app-wide via useAuth() and used correctly in profile.tsx:77-118 -- it just isn't reachable during onboarding. ADDITIONALLY (found on adjudication, confirmed against actual code): even if a sign-out control is added, it will not work as intended today, because the onboarding Stack.Protected guard at _layout.tsx:162 is `guard={!settings.onboardingComplete}` with no session term -- unlike the (tabs) guard at :175, which is `!!session && allContextsLoaded && settings.onboardingComplete`. On sign-out, session -> null but SettingsContext resets to DEFAULT_SETTINGS (onboardingComplete: false, defaults.ts:8), so both the login guard and the onboarding guard remain simultaneously true and the user stays stranded on goals with no session instead of being routed to login. This latent guard gap must be closed as part of the fix, or the escape hatch will not actually escape.

Fix: Two parts, both required (the second was added on adjudication to satisfy correctness):

1. Guard fix (REQUIRED, new): in app/_layout.tsx, change the onboarding group's guard from `guard={!settings.onboardingComplete}` to `guard={!!session && !settings.onboardingComplete}`, mirroring the existing (tabs) guard pattern at line 175 exactly. This is a no-op for every existing (working) flow -- by the time a user reaches onboarding today they already have a session -- and only removes the invalid state where onboarding stays reachable after sign-out. This preserves the 'let the guard handle routing, no manual router.replace' design: the existing useAsyncLoad-driven remount (userID change -> allContextsLoaded false -> AppLoadingScreen -> Stack remount) already happens on sign-out; once the guard correctly reflects `!!session`, that remount now resolves to login-only, with no additional navigation code needed.

2. Add a discoverable, accessible 'Sign out' escape to the onboarding flow via the shared OnboardingScaffold:
   a. components/NeutralComponents/OnboardingScaffold.tsx: add an optional `onSignOut?: () => void` prop. When provided, render a top-right text button ('Sign out') as a normal flex header row (NOT absolutely positioned) placed above/before the StepProgress dots and 'Step N of M' eyebrow, so it cannot overlap them. Style from tokens only (fonts.semibold, colors.textSecondary -- verify 4.5:1 contrast in both schemes, fall back to colors.text if not; radius/spacing for padding). Give it a 44pt touch target via hitSlop/padding, accessibilityRole="button", accessibilityLabel="Sign out". Keep the scaffold presentation-only -- do not import useAuth into it.
   b. app/onboardingScreens/goals.tsx: import useAuth, add a handleSignOut that confirms then signs out, and pass it as onSignOut to the scaffold. Reuse the exact confirm + offline/force fallback already written and tested in profile.tsx:77-118 (Alert 'Sign Out' / 'Are you sure you want to sign out?' -> destructive 'Sign Out' -> await signOut(); on isUploadFlushError show the offline ('Sign out anyway? Unsynced data will be lost.') vs still-syncing ('Force sign out') branch with forceSignOut; else a generic error Alert). Since goals is the onboarding entry screen and every other onboarding step can navigate Back to it, wiring only goals is sufficient to unblock the user.

To avoid copy-pasting the ~40-line flush/offline/force block into a second call site, extract it once into a shared helper (e.g. confirmSignOut({ signOut, forceSignOut, setLoading }) in lib/utils/) and call it from both profile.tsx and goals.tsx, keeping the two entry points behaviorally identical.

Files to change:
  - app/_layout.tsx
      REQUIRED (added on adjudication): change the onboarding Stack.Protected guard at line 162 from `guard={!settings.onboardingComplete}` to `guard={!!session && !settings.onboardingComplete}`, matching the (tabs) guard's session term at line 175. Without this, sign-out from onboarding leaves the user stranded on goals with no session instead of routing to login.
  - components/NeutralComponents/OnboardingScaffold.tsx
      Add optional onSignOut?: () => void prop; render a token-styled, right-aligned 'Sign out' text button as a normal flex header row placed above StepProgress/eyebrow (not absolutely positioned, so it cannot overlap them) when provided. 44pt hitSlop, accessibilityRole/Label. Presentation-only -- no useAuth import.
  - app/onboardingScreens/goals.tsx
      Import useAuth; add handleSignOut (confirm + offline/force fallback, reusing profile's flow via the shared helper) and pass it as onSignOut to OnboardingScaffold at line 28.
  - lib/utils/confirmSignOut.ts
      NEW (optional but recommended): extract the confirm + isUploadFlushError/forceSignOut/error-Alert logic currently inline in profile.tsx:77-118 into a shared helper so both profile and onboarding share one tested flow.
  - app/settingsScreens/profile.tsx
      If the helper is extracted, replace the inline handleSignOut body with a call to the shared confirmSignOut helper (behavior unchanged).

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    OnboardingScaffold's new onSignOut prop is optional/undefined by default, so every existing call site (obstacles, aboutYou, activity, goal, pace, timeline, plan, projection) renders unchanged; only goals.tsx opts in. paywall.tsx doesn't use the scaffold and is unaffected. The _layout.tsx guard change adds a clause that is already true on every currently-working path (a user only ever reaches onboarding today with a live session), so no existing caller/consumer of the onboarding routes changes behavior except the newly-broken sign-out-during-onboarding path, which this fix repairs. If confirmSignOut is extracted, profile.tsx becomes a second caller -- verify its loading state and offline/force/error branches are unchanged.
  Edge cases: (1) Offline mid-onboarding: signOut() throws UploadFlushNotConnectedError -- must land in the offline 'Sign out anyway?' branch with forceSignOut. (2) Still-syncing: must show the 'Still syncing / Force sign out' branch. (3) Double-tap while signing out: guard with a loading flag (mirroring profile's signOutLoading) so confirm/sign-out isn't fired twice. (4) After sign-out, with the corrected guard, only the login group is guard-eligible (`!session` true, onboarding guard now false because `!!session` is false) so the existing AppLoadingScreen remount naturally lands on login -- no manual router.replace needed, avoiding a race with the guard. (5) VoiceOver: the new button must not steal focus order from the title; it renders before StepProgress/eyebrow/title in DOM order but should carry a clear, distinct label so it isn't confused with the screen title.
  Preserved:  Existing Back/Next footer behavior, StepProgress/eyebrow layout (no overlap, since the new control is a separate row above them, not an overlay), keyboard handling, and safe-area padding all unchanged. signOut/forceSignOut semantics (Gate C flush, PowerSync teardown, scoped storage clear) are reused verbatim -- no new sign-out path invented. The only routing-relevant change is the one-line guard clause, which is a no-op for the existing working flow and only forecloses the previously-latent broken state.
  Tests:      Add a unit test for the extracted confirmSignOut helper (if created) covering: happy-path signOut called, isUploadFlushError -> forceSignOut branch, generic-error Alert branch -- mirroring context/AuthContext/functions/__tests__/accountFunctions.test.ts. Run existing accountFunctions.test.ts and profile-related tests to confirm no regression. Add or update a routing-level check (manual or test) confirming that signing out from goals.tsx actually lands on authScreens/login (not stuck on goals) given the corrected guard -- this is the behavior the original brief assumed but that was previously false; it must be explicitly verified given how easy it is to regress. Manually verify in both dark/light schemes and at large Dynamic Type that the sign-out affordance renders, is reachable, and doesn't collide with StepProgress/eyebrow.
  DevHub:     approval=na; plan={"applicable":false,"naReason":"This change lives entirely in production onboarding screens (goals.tsx) and the shared OnboardingScaffold consumed directly by them -- it is exercised by the real onboarding flow, not an isolated dev-harness component. The existing components/devTest/onboarding/* area previews historical V3/V4 design variants, not the live production scaffold, so no new Dev Hub page is warranted; manual verification of the live onboarding flow (per blastRadius.tests) is the appropriate check."}

Adjudication decisions:
  - The onboarding Stack.Protected guard at app/_layout.tsx:162 is `guard={!settings.onboardingComplete}` with no `!!session` term (unlike the (tabs) guard at :175 which is `!!session && allContextsLoaded && settings.onboardingComplete`). Verified against actual code: on sign-out, session -> null but SettingsContext's useAsyncLoad resets to DEFAULT_SETTINGS (onboardingComplete: false, confirmed in context/SettingsContext/defaults.ts:8) once userID becomes falsy, so BOTH the login guard (!session) and the onboarding guard stay true simultaneously. Verified goals.tsx has zero session check of its own. The brief's claim that 'the route guard swaps back to authScreens/login automatically' is therefore false for the onboarding case -- the user is left on goals with no session, unable to sign in with a different Apple ID, which is the entire point of the fix.
      -> MATERIAL -- correctness gate failure. The brief as banked does not resolve the root cause: it adds an escape button that doesn't reliably escape. Amended the fix to add a required (not optional) one-line change to app/_layout.tsx: change the onboarding guard from `guard={!settings.onboardingComplete}` to `guard={!!session && !settings.onboardingComplete}`, mirroring the existing (tabs) guard pattern exactly. Verified this is safe for the normal (non-broken) path: by the time a user reaches onboarding today, session is already non-null (login guard already excluded them), so the added clause is a no-op for every existing flow and only removes the invalid dual-eligible state. This keeps the brief's 'let the guard handle routing, no manual router.replace' philosophy -- it just fixes the guard's condition instead of bypassing it.  (Rule 1 (Correctness, HARD GATE))
  - Reviewer restated the same guard omission (session not gating the onboarding group) via the (tabs) guard contrast at :175 as a second, separately-worded finding.
      -> Same defect as above -- folded into the single guard-fix amendment; not counted as a second independent change.  (Rule 1 (duplicate of prior finding))
  - Reviewer traced the current (pre-fix) auto-return-to-login behavior to the useAsyncLoad loading-flash / Stack remount (userID change -> allContextsLoaded false -> AppLoadingScreen -> remount), arguing the brief mis-credits a direct guard swap and that this remount path is fragile.
      -> MATERIAL insofar as it explains *why* the guard fix is necessary and sufficient: the remount already happens today (confirmed in useAsyncLoad.ts:22-24 and _layout.tsx:133-139) and will continue to happen after sign-out; the missing piece was that the guard evaluated on that remount didn't reflect the intended state. With the `!!session` clause added, the remount now correctly resolves to login-only. No separate fix to useAsyncLoad or the remount mechanism is needed once the guard is corrected.  (Rule 1 (Correctness) -- resolved by the guard fix, no additional change required; Rule 4 (Least code) -- avoids touching useAsyncLoad.)
  - Reviewer claims the brief describes its Alert copy as 'byte-identical' to profile.tsx and instead specifies different copy ('Sign out?' / "You'll return to the sign-in screen... wrong Apple ID"), contradicting itself.
      -> IMMATERIAL -- verified against the actual banked brief text: neither the phrase 'byte-identical' nor the quoted alternate Alert copy ('Sign out?' / "You'll return to the sign-in screen...") appears anywhere in brief.fix or brief.blastRadius. The banked brief's fix text explicitly instructs reusing profile.tsx's exact title ('Sign Out'), message ('Are you sure you want to sign out?'), and offline/still-syncing branch copy verbatim via the shared confirmSignOut helper. This finding does not match the brief under adjudication (appears to reference a different/earlier draft) and is dismissed.  (Materiality check failed -- claim not supported by actual brief content)
  - Reviewer claims 'brief option 1' places the sign-out link 'absolutely positioned top-right within topPad,' which would overlap StepProgress dots and the 'Step N of M' eyebrow that goals.tsx renders (step is passed at goals.tsx:24/28).

M32  (Medium)  [DONE]

[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: Frequently-tapped remove/add/stepper controls in the nutrition and add-exercise flows render at 18-32pt visible size with no (or insufficient) hitSlop, so their effective touch target falls below Apple's 44pt HIG floor. Verified in code:
- app/nutritionScreens/foodDBModal.tsx:285 — staged-item remove button, 28x28 (styles 501-510), NO hitSlop.
- app/nutritionScreens/savedNutritionModal.tsx:157 — staged remove button, 28x28 (styles 375-384), NO hitSlop.
- components/NutritionComponents/FoodRow.tsx:45 — search-result add button, 32x32 (styles 105-114), NO hitSlop.
- app/nutritionScreens/editEntry.tsx:181,193 — servings stepper buttons stepBtn, 28x28 (style 277), NO hitSlop. (trash button at line 163 already has hitSlop={8} -> compliant, out of scope.)
- app/workoutScreens/addExerciseModal.tsx:139 — selected-chip remove chipRemove, 18x18 (styles 441-448) with hitSlop={6} -> ~30pt effective, still under floor.
This is an interaction/accessibility defect, not a visual one. NOTE (added on adjudication): for the two tightly-packed controls — the editEntry steppers (flanking a TextInput 8pt away) and the addExerciseModal chip-remove (in an 8pt-gapped flexWrap row) — a fully symmetric or naively-generous hitSlop is not safe; the fix must be asymmetric and, for the chip control, cannot reach 44pt on both axes without a layout change that is out of scope for this fix.

Fix: Add/enlarge hitSlop on each control, reusing in-repo precedents (editEntry.tsx:163 trash hitSlop={8}; progress.tsx:232-238 week stepper hitSlop={12} + accessibilityRole/accessibilityLabel). No StyleSheet/visible-layout changes.
- foodDBModal.tsx:285 removeButton (28pt) -> hitSlop={8} (28+16=44, exact). Add accessibilityRole="button" + accessibilityLabel="Remove item".
- savedNutritionModal.tsx:157 removeButton (28pt) -> hitSlop={8} + same a11y label. (44pt exact.)
- FoodRow.tsx:45 addButton (32pt) -> hitSlop={6} (32+12=44, exact) + accessibilityLabel="Add food". Keep disabled logic intact.
- editEntry.tsx:181 & :193 stepBtn (28pt), AMENDED per adjudication: the input-facing side stays minimal (2pt, preserving 6pt buffer to the TextInput and protecting it from tap theft) but the outer side — verified to face only non-interactive content (a Text label with no onPress on the minus side, open card padding on the plus side) — is raised from 8 to 14 so the horizontal axis actually reaches the 44pt floor (the original 8/2 split only reached 38pt, which the review correctly flagged): minus `hitSlop={{ top: 8, bottom: 8, left: 14, right: 2 }}`, plus `hitSlop={{ top: 8, bottom: 8, left: 2, right: 14 }}` (horizontal 14+28+2=44, vertical 8+28+8=44 — both axes now genuinely compliant). Labels "Decrease servings"/"Increase servings".
- addExerciseModal.tsx:139 chipRemove (18pt), AMENDED per adjudication: the review correctly found the proposed vertical hitSlop of 13 exceeds chipsRow's 8pt row/column gap (styles 413-417) and would let the X's touch area bleed into an adjacent wrapped-row chip — a new functional regression (wrong item removed) that is not present today (current hitSlop=6 is safely under the 8pt gap). Cap vertical hitSlop at 6 (no regression vs. shipped behavior) and put the entire improvement budget on the horizontal axis, which has 16pt of dead space to its right (8pt chip paddingRight + 8pt chipsRow gap) and a non-interactive chipText (no onPress) to its left: `hitSlop={{ top: 6, bottom: 6, left: 12, right: 14 }}` -> horizontal = 12+18+14 = 44pt (floor met); vertical = 6+18+6 = 30pt (unchanged from today, NOT 44pt — see documented limitation below). Keep existing accessibility label if present, else add "Remove exercise".

Documented limitation (do not silently drop): full 44x44 compliance for the chip-remove control cannot be reached via hitSlop alone without increasing chipsRow's shared `gap` (currently 8) — a small, low-risk layout tweak that is explicitly OUT OF SCOPE for this fix (visible-layout changes were excluded to avoid Dynamic-Type/clipping risk). This is an acceptable, non-blocking residual gap: severity is Medium and non-launch-blocker, and the fix strictly improves (never worsens) the current interaction without introducing new bugs.

Files to change:
  - app/nutritionScreens/foodDBModal.tsx
      Line 285: add hitSlop={8} + accessibilityRole/accessibilityLabel to the staged-item removeButton TouchableOpacity. StyleSheet untouched. (44pt exact — unchanged from banked brief.)
  - app/nutritionScreens/savedNutritionModal.tsx
      Line 157: add hitSlop={8} + accessibilityLabel to the staged removeButton TouchableOpacity. (44pt exact — unchanged from banked brief.)

  - app/workoutScreens/addExerciseModal.tsx
      Line 139: AMENDED — replace hitSlop={6} with `{ top: 6, bottom: 6, left: 12, right: 14 }` (vertical capped at 6 to avoid the wrap-row overlap regression the review caught; horizontal raised to reach 44pt on that axis). Vertical remains at ~30pt, a documented non-blocking residual gap, not the full 44pt the banked brief claimed.

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    All five are leaf UI controls; hitSlop/accessibility props are additive and consumed only by the TouchableOpacity itself — no callers, props, or shared helpers change.   Edge cases: 1) editEntry steppers flank a TextInput (stepRow gap:8): the amended split keeps the input-facing side at 2pt (6pt buffer preserved) while the outer side (verified to face only non-interactive label text / open padding) absorbs 14pt to reach 44pt horizontally — no new tap-theft risk. 2) addExerciseModal chip: vertical hitSlop is capped at 6 (unchanged from shipped behavior) specifically to prevent the wrap-row overlap regression the review identified (chipsRow gap:8 for a flexWrap list means a naive vertical hitSlop of 13 would bleed into the adjacent row's chip and could remove the wrong exercise) — this is a genuine fix of a bug the banked brief would have introduced. Existing activeOpacity, onPress handlers, disabled/loading branches are all unchanged. The amendment specifically removes a functional regression (chip vertical overlap) that the banked brief's numbers would have introduced, so post-adjudication the fix is strictly non-regressive versus the current shipped app.

Adjudication decisions:
  - editEntry.tsx stepBtn (28pt) asymmetric hitSlop {outer:8, inner:2} yields only 38pt horizontal, not the 44pt the brief claims ('28+16=44' math applies only to the symmetric case explicitly rejected for these two buttons). Raised twice (near-duplicate) by reviewers.
      -> Material — accepted and amended. Verified in code: stepBtn is 28x28 (editEntry.tsx:277), stepRow gap:8 (editEntry.tsx:280). Reviewer math is correct: 8+28+2=38pt < 44pt floor, so the brief overstates what its own prescribed fix achieves. This is a rule-1 (correctness) defect: the fix's own stated goal (reach the 44pt HIG floor) is not met on the horizontal axis for the two controls where horizontal miss-taps are the primary failure mode. Resolution: rebalance the asymmetric split rather than abandon it — checked that the OUTER side of each stepBtn faces only inert content (servRow gap:12 to a non-interactive servLabel Text on the minus button's left; open card padding on the plus button's right), so it can safely absorb more hitSlop than the input-facing side. Amended values: minus `hitSlop={{top:8,bottom:8,left:14,right:2}}`, plus `hitSlop={{top:8,bottom:8,left:2,right:14}}` -> horizontal = 14+28+2 = 44pt exactly, vertical unchanged at 8+28+8=44pt (this axis was already correct per the reviewer's own finding). Input-facing side stays minimal (2pt into the existing 8pt gap, 6pt buffer preserved before the TextInput) so the original anti-tap-theft rationale is preserved.  (Correctness (rule 1, hard gate) — fix must actually reach the stated target, not just claim to)
  - addExerciseModal.tsx chipRemove (18pt) asymmetric hitSlop {left:8, right:13, vertical:13} yields only 39pt horizontal ('reach 44pt' claim is false on this axis). Raised twice (near-duplicate) by reviewers.
      -> Material — accepted and amended (see combined resolution below with the vertical-overlap finding). Verified: chipRemove is 18x18 (addExerciseModal.tsx:441-448); math 8+18+13=39pt confirmed correct, under the 44pt floor.  (Correctness (rule 1, hard gate))
  - chipsRow uses flexWrap with gap:8 (addExerciseModal.tsx:413-417, gap applies to both row and column spacing in RN). The brief's proposed vertical hitSlop of 13 (top/bottom) exceeds this 8pt gap, so the X's expanded touch area bleeds into the neighboring wrapped row's chip when the selection list wraps to 2+ lines — a functional regression (wrong exercise could get removed) introduced BY this fix, not present before it (the pre-existing symmetric hitSlop=6 was safely under the 8pt gap). The brief's left-side reduction (for the label/next-chip-in-row concern) does nothing to address this vertical/cross-row overlap.
      -> Material and the most serious finding in this set — accepted. This is a new bug the fix would introduce, which fails rule 2 (safety/no broken behavior for adjacent controls) outright, independent of the HIG-floor question. Resolution: cap vertical hitSlop for chipRemove at 6 (unchanged from the current shipped value, i.e. 6 < 8pt gap, preserving the existing no-overlap safety margin) rather than raising it to 13. Shift the achievable-improvement budget entirely to the horizontal axis, which has ample dead space (8pt chip paddingRight + 8pt chipsRow gap before the next chip on the right; chipText itself has no onPress, so the left side tolerates more overlap with no functional consequence). Amended value: `hitSlop={{ top: 6, bottom: 6, left: 12, right: 14 }}` -> horizontal = 12+18+14 = 44pt (floor met), vertical = 6+18+6 = 30pt (no regression vs. current shipped state, though it does not reach 44pt). The brief must stop asserting full 44x44 compliance for this control and instead document the residual vertical gap as a known, accepted limitation: reaching 44pt vertically would require increasing chipsRow's `gap` (currently 8, shared row/column spacing) — a small, low-risk layout tweak, but out of this fix's stated 'no visible layout change' scope, and not required since severity is Medium/non-launch-blocker and the fix still measurably improves (and never worsens) the current behavior.  (Safety / blast radius (rule 2) — outranks the correctness shortfall on this single axis because rule 2 gates a newly-introduced functional bug, which must be eliminated before any further reach for HIG-perfect compliance is worth pursuing)

L2  (Low)  [DONE]
===============================================================================
[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: The maintain branch of the adjust-nutrition wizard hardcodes goalPace to '0' at app/settingsScreens/adjustNutrition/adjustNutrition1.tsx:38 ({ ...commonParams, goalPace: '0' }). adjustNutrition3.tsx:85 and adjustNutrition4.tsx:53 only forward that param, so '0' is written to settings.goalPace on save. goalPace is stored in lb/week. The onboarding maintain path (app/onboardingScreens/goal.tsx:37-44) does NOT touch goalPace at all (setSettings only sets goalType and goalWeight), so the two maintain-producing flows disagree: onboarding preserves the user's existing pace, the settings wizard destroys it. The audit's stated symptoms are masked downstream — macroCalculation.tsx:45-50 ignores goalPace for maintain, goalMath.ts:5 short-circuits maintain to 12 weeks before the pace<=0 divide, and profile.tsx:255 hides the Goal Pace row for maintain — so no '0.0 lbs/week' string ever renders and the goalMath fallback is unreachable for maintain. The genuine defect is silent data loss: because adjustNutrition2.tsx:35-36 seeds its pace slider from settings.goalPace (clamping out-of-range values to r.def), a user who briefly selects maintain via the wizard and later switches back to lose/gain has their previously-chosen pace silently reset to the default, while a user who reaches maintain only via onboarding keeps their pace intact. The primary real-world trigger for entering the wizard's maintain branch is the unconditional 'Goal' row on profile.tsx:247 (router.push to adjustNutrition1), not just the conditionally-hidden Target Weight/Goal Pace rows.

Fix: Stop clobbering the stored pace on the maintain branch. In app/settingsScreens/adjustNutrition/adjustNutrition1.tsx:36-39, change the maintain push param from goalPace: '0' to goalPace: settings.goalPace.toString(). settings.goalPace is already in the lb/week storage unit — identical to what the lose/gain path emits at adjustNutrition2.tsx:48 (metric ? kgToLbs(goalPace) : goalPace) — so no unit conversion is introduced and the value round-trips cleanly. This makes the wizard maintain path match the onboarding maintain path (both now preserve the existing pace) and prevents the reset-to-default when a user toggles maintain then returns to lose/gain. No change to adjustNutrition3.tsx or adjustNutrition4.tsx: adjustNutrition4.tsx:53 already does params.goalPace ? Number(params.goalPace) : 0 and will now persist the preserved value. No change to lib/utils/goalMath.ts: its pace<=0 -> 1 fallback is documented, unit-consistent, tested (goalMath.test.ts:12-14), and unreachable for maintain — do not touch it. If the coordinator prefers the opposite convention (canonically zero the pace for every maintain user), that must instead be applied in BOTH goal.tsx and adjustNutrition1.tsx together to avoid re-introducing the drift; the preserve approach here is the smaller correct change and aligns with existing onboarding behavior.

Files to change:
  - c:\Users\aadik\Downloads\App\app\settingsScreens\adjustNutrition\adjustNutrition1.tsx
      Line 38: replace goalPace: '0' with goalPace: settings.goalPace.toString() so the maintain branch preserves the user's stored pace (lb/week) instead of overwriting it with 0, matching the onboarding maintain path.

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    Only the maintain branch of adjustNutrition1.handleNext is affected. Entry points into adjustNutrition1: profile.tsx:247 (unconditional 'Goal' row — always visible, the primary real-world way a user switches into maintain and triggers this branch), profile.tsx:259 (Target Weight row, hidden when goalType==='maintain') and profile.tsx:271 (Goal Pace row, also hidden when goalType==='maintain'). The value flows to adjustNutrition3 (preview only — macroCalculation ignores goalPace for maintain, so shown macros are unchanged) then to adjustNutrition4.handleSave which persists settings.goalPace. Consumers of a maintain user's goalPace: macroCalculation.tsx:45 (skips it for maintain), goalMath.ts:5 (returns 12 for maintain before using pace), profile.tsx:275 (row hidden for maintain via :255), and adjustNutrition2.tsx:35 (slider seed on a later switch to lose/gain — now correctly seeded from the preserved pace).   Tests:      Add a screen test app/settingsScreens/adjustNutrition/__tests__/adjustNutrition1.test.tsx (mirror the existing pattern in app/nutritionScreens/__tests__/editEntry.test.tsx and app/settingsScreens/__tests__/devStatsModal.test.tsx): mock useSettings to return goalPace 1.5 (imperial) AND a valid height/bodyWeight (e.g. height=70, bodyWeight=180 — both must clear validateHeightWeight's imperial minimums of 24in/50lb at validator.tsx:4-24, or handleNext short-circuits before any router.push, per adjustNutrition1.tsx:26); spy on expo-router's router.push; render AdjustNutrition1Screen, select the Maintain OptionCard, tap Next, and assert router.push was called with pathname adjustNutrition3 and params.goalPace === '1.5'.   - The proposed regression test (mock useSettings with goalPace 1.5, select Maintain, tap Next, assert router.push goalPace==='1.5') will never reach the assertion because handleNext (adjustNutrition1.tsx:26) calls validateHeightWeight(Number(settings.height), Number(settings.bodyWeight), unitSystem) first, which returns false (and shows an Alert, no push) when height/bodyWeight are undefined/0/below the imperial minimums (24in / 50lb, validator.tsx:4-24). The brief's test guidance only mentions mocking goalPace, so as written the test would fail for the wrong reason (or silently no-op).

  - The blast-radius caller list cites profile.tsx:259 and :271 (Target Weight / Goal Pace rows) as the entry points into adjustNutrition1 but omits profile.tsx:247, the primary 'Goal' row, which is the exact tap target a user uses to switch their goal type to maintain — i.e., the real-world trigger for this bug.

It is in fact the only one of the three rows guaranteed to be reachable when a user is choosing to switch into maintain. Omitting it makes the caller enumeration incomplete for a fix whose whole premise is about the maintain-selection path. Amended blastRadius.callers to add profile.tsx:247 explicitly as the primary/unconditional entry point that triggers the maintain branch.  (Safety / blast radius (rule 2) — an accurate, complete caller inventory is required to certify the fix's blast radius; this doesn't change the fix's correctness but the brief's safety analysis was incomplete without it.)

===============================================================================
L3  (Low)  [AUTHORED]
===============================================================================
[Author brief]

Root cause: In `Connector.updateRecord`'s settings branch (`lib/powersync/Connector.ts:191-241`), a PATCH op that can't resolve its Supabase row via `id` (line 198-202) falls back to looking up the row by the session's `user_id` (line 209-219). If that lookup ALSO finds nothing — i.e., no settings row exists upstream for this user at all — the function hits `return;` at line 223 with no error, no Sentry capture, nothing. Back in `uploadData` (Connector.ts:80-122), a normal `return` looks identical to success: the loop moves on and `transaction.complete()` (line 121) marks the op delivered. The user's settings change is now gone — never reached Supabase, and marked done so PowerSync will never retry it.

This state is reachable and self-perpetuating: the very first settings write for a user is a local INSERT, which PowerSync queues as a PUT and `uploadData` routes to `createRecord` (Connector.ts:129-153, which does have correct select-then-insert-or-update handling). If that initial create is ever dead-lettered — e.g. it throws a genuine Postgres 22/23-class permanent error via `isNonRetryableUploadError` (line 15-24) — the settings row never gets created upstream, but the local SQLite row (`upsertSettings`, `context/SettingsContext/database/powersyncStore.ts:64-115`) does exist, so every later settings edit queues as a PATCH, not a PUT. Every one of those PATCHes then hits the exact `return` at line 223, forever, with zero telemetry. This is worse than an ordinary dead-letter: it's invisible (no Sentry event ever fires) and it silently discards every future settings change for that user, not just the one that failed. It is simultaneously an infra-reliability defect (a silent, self-perpetuating hole in the sync/dead-letter machinery with no observability) and a logic-correctness defect (the function's early-return is the wrong terminal action — it should attempt a real repair, not discard the write).

The reason `return` was chosen instead of an insert is that `updateData` for a PATCH is a partial diff (only the changed columns), and Postgres `settings` columns are populated in full only on insert (`settingsToRow`, `context/SettingsContext/database/powersyncStore.ts:33-35`) — a naive insert from partial PATCH data could violate constraints on the missing columns, so simply upserting `updateData` isn't safe. That's a real constraint, but it doesn't justify silent discard: the local SQLite row for this same `id` always holds the full, valid row (settings is one-row-per-user with all columns populated by `upsertSettings`), so the correct row to insert from is the local one, not the partial patch.

Fix: Code fix, contained to `lib/powersync/Connector.ts` (no schema change, no new dependency, no new call sites).

1. Thread the `database: AbstractPowerSyncDatabase` handle (already in scope in `uploadData`, Connector.ts:80) into `updateRecord` as a parameter, alongside the existing `table`/`record` args. `updateRecord` is a private method with exactly one call site (Connector.ts:101), so this is a same-file, no-external-impact signature change.

2. Replace the terminal `return;` at Connector.ts:223 (the branch reached when neither the `id` lookup at 198-202 nor the session/`user_id` lookup at 214-219 finds an existing Supabase settings row) with:
   - Read the full local row for this `id` from PowerSync's local SQLite (`database.getAll('SELECT * FROM settings WHERE id = ?', [id])`) — this is the authoritative, fully-populated row (every column set by `upsertSettings`), unlike the partial PATCH `updateData`.
   - If found, call the existing `createRecord('settings', fullLocalRow)` helper (Connector.ts:129-153) instead of returning. `createRecord` already implements the correct select-then-insert-or-update dance with the `user_id` unique constraint — reuse it rather than duplicating upsert logic. This repairs the missing upstream row using real data instead of discarding the change.
   - If the local row is *also* gone (op stale, e.g. row deleted locally between being queued and drained) — this is a genuine no-op, but replace the silent `return` with a `Sentry.captureMessage` (tag `area: 'powersync-dead-letter'`, matching the existing tag scheme at Connector.ts:110-113) before returning, so this edge case is visible instead of untraceable.

3. No change needed to error propagation: if the fallback `createRecord` call itself throws (e.g. the local row also carries data that trips a genuine permanent Postgres constraint), it propagates normally out of `updateRecord` into `uploadData`'s existing try/catch (Connector.ts:93-117), which already dead-letters + Sentry-captures 22/23-class errors (with the settings-specific 23505 self-heal carve-out at line 20-21 preserved). No duplicate handling is written.

This closes the silent-drop path while reusing 100% of the existing upsert and dead-letter machinery — the only new code is the local-row read and the fallback call/capture in the one branch that previously discarded data.

Files to change:
  - lib/powersync/Connector.ts
      updateRecord (lines 191-241): add a database param; replace the line-223 `return;` (reached when the settings row can't be found by id or by session user_id) with: read the full local row for `id` from SQLite, call the existing createRecord('settings', fullLocalRow) helper to repair the missing upstream row from authoritative local data; if the local row is also absent, Sentry.captureMessage (tag area: 'powersync-dead-letter') before returning instead of returning silently.
  - lib/powersync/__tests__/connector.test.ts
      Fix the pre-existing broken 'should handle PATCH operation (update record)' test (verified failing at HEAD: `supabase.from(...).select is not a function` because the mock never stubs `.select().eq().maybeSingle()`, so it doesn't exercise the real id/user_id lookup chain at all). Add a test for the new fallback-create path (no row found by id or user_id -> local row read -> createRecord invoked with full local data -> transaction still completes) and a test for the true-no-op edge case (local row also missing -> Sentry.captureMessage fires once, no upstream write attempted).

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    updateRecord is a private method of Connector with exactly one call site, Connector.ts:101 inside uploadData's own switch statement. No other file calls it, so the added `database` parameter is a purely internal signature change with zero external blast radius.
  Edge cases: (1) The genuine no-op case where the local SQLite row is also gone by the time uploadData drains this op (deleted locally between queuing and processing) — now gets a Sentry.captureMessage instead of vanishing untraced, but still correctly performs no write. (2) The fallback createRecord call can itself hit a genuine permanent Postgres error (e.g. corrupted local data) — this is not new: it flows into uploadData's existing catch block unchanged, dead-lettering exactly as any other PUT/PATCH failure does today. (3) The SELF_HEALING_CONFLICT_TABLES 23505 retry carve-out for settings/weight_progress and the id-then-user_id lookup order are both left completely intact — the change touches only the single terminal `return` branch that previously had no recovery path.
  Preserved:  All other Connector behavior is unchanged: PUT handling, DELETE handling, non-settings PATCH handling (updates by id), the 22/23-class dead-letter classification, the 23505 self-healing carve-out for settings/weight_progress, and transaction.complete() semantics are untouched.
  Tests:      lib/powersync/__tests__/connector.test.ts — fix the currently-broken 'should handle PATCH operation (update record)' test (confirmed failing at HEAD via `npx jest lib/powersync/__tests__/connector.test.ts -t "PATCH operation"`: `TypeError: supabase.from(...).select is not a function`) so it actually exercises the id/user_id lookup chain; add a test asserting the fallback-to-createRecord repair path fires when neither lookup finds a row, and a test asserting Sentry.captureMessage fires (with no upstream write) when the local row is also absent.
  DevHub:     approval=na; plan={"applicable":false,"naReason":"This is a Connector/sync-queue backend fix with no UI surface — there is nothing to preview in Dev Hub. Verification is via the Jest tests on lib/powersync/__tests__/connector.test.ts, not a devTest screen."}

L12  (Low)  [DONE]
===============================================================================
[Adjudicated final brief]

Root cause: Both legacy windowing functions build their inclusion cutoff from `new Date()` (current wall-clock instant, carrying time-of-day) and then subtract days, but compare that cutoff against `log.date` values that are local-midnight-normalized (`parseDateKey` in context/WorkoutContext/database/powersyncStore.ts:44 returns `new Date(year, month-1, day)`).

- context/WorkoutContext/functions/fatigueFunctions.tsx:212-219 (calculateFatiguePercentage) — `cutoffDate = new Date(); cutoffDate.setDate(cutoffDate.getDate() - numDays)`; then `if (log.date < cutoffDate) continue`.
- context/WorkoutContext/functions/oneRepMaxFunctions.tsx:23-34 (oneRMMap) — `refCutoff = new Date(); refCutoff.setDate(refCutoff.getDate() - refDays)`; then `if (log.date < refCutoff) continue`.

Verified by tracing the comparison algebraically: cutoffDate keeps 'now's time-of-day T. For the boundary day exactly N days ago (log.date = midnight(today-N)), `log.date < cutoffDate` reduces to `0 < T`, which is true for every wall-clock call except the literal millisecond of local midnight (T=0) — an edge case that essentially never fires in real usage. So in practice the current code already yields a deterministic N-day window (day 0 .. day N-1 ago), matching the `getDailyBudget * numDays` divisor at fatigueFunctions.tsx:234; only a call at exactly 00:00:00.000 would flip the boundary day to included. The bug is real but its practical blast radius is narrower than the original description implied.

CORRECTNESS ISSUE FOUND IN THE PROPOSED FIX (raised by adversarial review, verified against the code): simply normalizing the cutoff to start-of-day using the SAME `-numDays`/`-refDays` offset (as originally proposed, and as cited from buildRefByName's `-refDays` + `getStartOfDay` pattern) makes the boundary day ALWAYS included instead of almost-always excluded — widening the window from N days to N+1 days, deterministically, at every call. For calculateFatiguePercentage this inflates the numerator while the denominator (`getDailyBudget * numDays`) stays N days, skewing the reported percentage upward (materially so for small numDays, e.g. numDays=1 becomes a 2-day window). buildRefByName (fatigueFunctions.tsx:74-81) is not a valid precedent to copy here: it already carries this identical +1-day widening itself (refCutoff = addDays(now,-refDays), compared via getStartOfDay on both sides), which is harmless there only because it feeds a 30-day rolling MAX with no per-day divisor — sum/divide functions like calculateFatiguePercentage cannot absorb the same widening without skewing. calculateFatigueSummary, in the SAME file, already demonstrates the correct convention: cutoff3 = addDays(todayStart, -2) for a 3-day window, i.e. -(N-1), not -N.

Fix: Normalize each cutoff to local start-of-day using a -(N-1) day offset — matching the already-established convention in calculateFatigueSummary (cutoff3/6/9 = addDays(todayStart, -(N-1))) — NOT a -(N) offset. A -(N) offset, once normalized to midnight, flips the previously-excluded boundary day to always-included while leaving the N-day divisor unchanged, inflating the result. This is the corrected version of the originally banked fix.

1. context/WorkoutContext/functions/fatigueFunctions.tsx (calculateFatiguePercentage, ~line 212-213): replace
   `const cutoffDate = new Date(); cutoffDate.setDate(cutoffDate.getDate() - numDays)`
   with
   `const cutoffDate = addDays(getStartOfDay(new Date()), -(numDays - 1))`
   reusing the file's existing module-private `addDays`/`getStartOfDay` helpers (already used at lines 75/81/118/125) — no new helper or import. The comparison at line 219 (`if (log.date < cutoffDate) continue`) stays as-is; `log.date` is already local-midnight-normalized. This preserves the numDays-day window that the `getDailyBudget * numDays` divisor at line 234 assumes, while making the boundary deterministic regardless of wall-clock call time.

2. context/WorkoutContext/functions/oneRepMaxFunctions.tsx (oneRMMap, ~line 23-24): replace
   `refCutoff.setDate(refCutoff.getDate() - refDays)`
   with
   `refCutoff.setDate(refCutoff.getDate() - (refDays - 1)); refCutoff.setHours(0, 0, 0, 0)`
   preserving the refDays-day window (day 0 through day refDays-1 ago) deterministically. Still no new imports — plain Date primitives, smallest correct change for this file. (Even though oneRMMap has zero production callers today, the function's own contract and its new regression test must be correct — an unreachable function that's subtly wrong, pinned by a test, is still a defect this patch would be introducing.)

Both copies must land together with the identical -(N-1) correction; there is no shared cross-file helper, so the coupling stays manual. Do NOT additionally 'fix' buildRefByName (fatigueFunctions.tsx:74-81) as part of this issue — its own +1-day width is pre-existing, harmless for a rolling-max reference with no divisor, and out of scope for L12; touching it would widen calculateFatigueSummary's already-correct 30-day reference pool for no benefit and is a larger blast radius than this issue requires. Do NOT cite buildRefByName's pattern as validation for a -(N) (non -(N-1)) offset in any sum/divide function.

Files to change:
  - context/WorkoutContext/functions/fatigueFunctions.tsx
      In calculateFatiguePercentage (~line 212-213), replace the raw `new Date()` + `setDate(-numDays)` cutoff with `const cutoffDate = addDays(getStartOfDay(new Date()), -(numDays - 1))`, reusing the file's existing addDays/getStartOfDay helpers. Do NOT use -(numDays) — that widens the window by one day relative to the getDailyBudget*numDays divisor at line 234. Comparison at line 219 stays as-is. Do not modify buildRefByName.
  - context/WorkoutContext/functions/oneRepMaxFunctions.tsx
      In oneRMMap (~line 23-24), change `refCutoff.setDate(refCutoff.getDate() - refDays)` to `refCutoff.setDate(refCutoff.getDate() - (refDays - 1))` and then add `refCutoff.setHours(0, 0, 0, 0)`. Do NOT subtract refDays directly then normalize — that widens the window by one day. No new imports.
  - context/WorkoutContext/functions/__tests__/oneRepMaxFunctions.test.ts
      Add a boundary/wall-clock determinism test using jest.useFakeTimers()/setSystemTime at two times (e.g. 00:05 and 23:55) on the same date. Assert: (a) a log dated exactly (refDays-1) days ago at local midnight is included and yields the same oneRMMap value at both mocked times; (b) a log dated exactly refDays days ago at local midnight is EXCLUDED at both mocked times (guards against reintroducing the N+1 widening the original literal fix would have caused).
  - context/WorkoutContext/functions/__tests__/fatigueFunctions.test.ts
      Add the mirror determinism test for calculateFatiguePercentage: with numDays fixed (e.g. 1), a log dated exactly today is included and yields an identical, non-zero, non-inflated percentage at both a 00:05 and 23:55 mocked time; a log dated exactly numDays days ago is excluded at both mocked times (pins the numerator/denominator match, i.e. no window widening).

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    oneRMMap: zero production callers — confirmed via grep, imported only by oneRepMaxFunctions.test.ts; the visible 1RM chart path is getOneRepMaxData, untouched. calculateFatiguePercentage: reached only via handleCalculateFatiguePercentage (context/WorkoutContext/index.tsx), confirmed via grep to have no callers under app/ — one of the de-emphasized fatigue handlers with no production callers. Runtime blast radius is effectively nil today; the change future-proofs both if re-wired, and correctness now matters more because a regression test will assert it.
  Edge cases: The day exactly (numDays-1)/(refDays-1) days ago at local midnight must be deterministically INCLUDED at every wall-clock hour after the fix (it already was, in practice, before the fix, at any non-exact-midnight call time — so no behavior change there for real users). The day exactly numDays/refDays days ago must be deterministically EXCLUDED at every wall-clock hour after the fix (previously excluded at all times except the literal millisecond of local midnight, essentially never triggered in production) — the fix removes that last sliver of nondeterminism WITHOUT widening the window, unlike the originally-banked -(N) fix which would have widened it at every call. DST: getStartOfDay/setHours(0,0,0,0) resolve to local wall-clock midnight, consistent with parseDateKey, so day-count is stable across DST transitions. Empty logs and reps<=0/weight<=0 guards are untouched. buildRefByName's separate, pre-existing +1-day width is unaffected and intentionally left alone.
  Preserved:  Window semantics genuinely unchanged this time (last N days through today = N calendar days, matching the N-day divisor); Epley e1RM math, per-name max bucketing, fatigue scoring, daily-budget scaling, and bodyweight effectiveLoad logic all unchanged. Only the determinism of the single boundary comparison changes — and unlike the originally-banked fix, the window LENGTH does not silently change.
  Tests:      context/WorkoutContext/functions/__tests__/oneRepMaxFunctions.test.ts and .../fatigueFunctions.test.ts: add the two-sided boundary tests described in the files list (N-1-ago included, N-ago excluded, both at two mocked times) so the tests fail against the ORIGINAL -(N) literal fix (would wrongly include the N-ago log) as well as against the unpatched wall-clock-dependent code, and pass only against the corrected -(N-1) fix. Run: npx jest context/WorkoutContext/functions/__tests__/oneRepMaxFunctions.test.ts context/WorkoutContext/functions/__tests__/fatigueFunctions.test.ts

Adjudication decisions:
  - Proposed cutoff getStartOfDay(addDays(now,-numDays)) widens calculateFatiguePercentage's window from numDays to numDays+1 calendar days while the divisor stays getDailyBudget*numDays, inflating the percentage (file fatigueFunctions.tsx:212).
      -> ACCEPT — verified algebraically against the actual code (line 212-219, 234): original code excludes the boundary day at every call time except literal T=0 (midnight), so it already yields an effective N-day window; the literal getStartOfDay(-numDays) fix instead ALWAYS includes that boundary day, a strictly worse, 100%-reproducible skew versus the near-zero-probability original flakiness. Amended the fix to use `addDays(getStartOfDay(new Date()), -(numDays - 1))` instead of `-(numDays)`, which fixes the determinism without changing window length.  (Rule 1 (correctness, hard gate) — a fix that introduces a new, more consistently-reproducible bug while 'fixing' a rare one fails the gate.)
  - Brief's own sibling calculateFatigueSummary already uses -(N-1) (cutoff3 = addDays(todayStart,-2) for a 3-day window), which is the correct pattern to preserve window length; the banked brief incorrectly forbids the N-1 adjustment.

Adopted this exact convention for calculateFatiguePercentage's cutoff, reusing the file's existing addDays/getStartOfDay helpers (no new code, matches sibling exactly).  (Rule 3 (convention fit) reinforcing Rule 1 — the fix must match the codebase's own already-correct established pattern rather than inventing a new, wrong one.)
  - buildRefByName is mischaracterized in the banked brief as the 'proven correct' sibling to copy, but it itself uses a -refDays (not -(refDays-1)) cutoff and is therefore already off-by-one-wide; that's harmless there (30-day rolling MAX, no divisor) but not a valid template for a divide/sum function.
      -> ACCEPT the narrative correction — the final brief's rootCause/fix no longer cites buildRefByName as validated proof for a -(N) offset in calculateFatiguePercentage. REJECT the implicit suggestion to modify buildRefByName itself — its own +1-day width is pre-existing, immaterial for a rolling-max reference, and out of scope for L12; changing it would widen calculateFatigueSummary's reference pool for no correctness benefit.  (Rule 1 for the narrative fix (don't cite false precedent); Rule 2/4 (safety, least code) for declining to touch unrelated, currently-correct-enough code outside this issue's scope.)
  - oneRMMap's proposed fix (refCutoff.setHours(0,0,0,0) after -refDays) applies the identical off-by-one widening (refDays -> refDays+1); brief's own blast-radius note confirms zero production callers so it's inert today but still bakes in the bug.
      -> ACCEPT — confirmed zero callers via grep (only the test file imports oneRMMap). Amended the fix to subtract (refDays - 1) before zeroing the time, preserving the refDays-day window deterministically, consistent with the corrected fatigueFunctions.tsx approach. Kept the no-new-import constraint (plain Date primitives) since the file has no local start-of-day helper.  (Rule 1 (correctness) — even for an unreachable function, the patch being applied and the regression test pinning it must both be correct; landing a known-wrong implementation with a passing-but-wrong test is not acceptable under a hard correctness gate.)
  - Numerator/denominator mismatch: cutoff = getStartOfDay(addDays(now,-numDays)) includes D-numDays..D (numDays+1 days) while the divisor at line 234 spans only numDays days, inflating the percentage whenever a log exists on the boundary day.
