L13  (Low)  [DRAFT]
===============================================================================
[Draft — not yet reviewed]

Root cause: `scheduleBatch` (lib/notifications/scheduler.ts:22-30) performs a non-atomic sequence: `cancelAllScheduledNotificationsAsync()` followed by a loop of awaited `scheduleNotificationAsync()` calls. Nothing serializes concurrent invocations. Three independent, unsynchronized entry points reach `runNotificationReschedule`: the debounced data-change effect (useNotificationScheduler.ts:19-26), the un-debounced AppState-foreground effect (useNotificationScheduler.ts:29-34), and the pref-save handler (settingsScreens/notifications.tsx:35). The 1500 ms debounce only guards the data-change effect, so a foreground event or a pref save can fire a reschedule while a debounced one is already in flight. Triggering interleaving: (A) run-A cancelAll resolves; (B) run-B cancelAll resolves (nothing left to cancel); (A) schedules spec1..N; (B) schedules its spec1..N. Because both cancelAlls completed before either scheduled, both batches end up booked and every reminder is double-scheduled. cancelAll only de-duplicates if it runs after the previous batch finishes scheduling.

Fix: Serialize the cancel+schedule critical section so both steps of any one batch complete before the next batch's cancelAll runs. Smallest correct change: add a module-level promise chain in scheduler.ts that `scheduleBatch` links onto. Keep `let scheduleQueue: Promise<void> = Promise.resolve()`; in `scheduleBatch`, capture `const run = scheduleQueue.then(() => <existing cancelAll + schedule loop>)`, set `scheduleQueue = run.catch(() => {})` (so a rejected batch does not poison the chain), and `return run` so direct callers and tests still observe errors. Each cancel+schedule pair becomes atomic relative to every other one; concurrent reschedules queue and last-writer-wins, so the final scheduled set is exactly one batch, never a union. Serialize at `scheduleBatch` (the true critical section) rather than `runNotificationReschedule`, since the prefs/permission reads earlier are safe to run concurrently and a slightly stale read is harmless (foreground always reschedules). Do NOT touch the hook's debounce or the three call sites — fixing the shared primitive covers all three callers at once. Preserve the existing try/catch-and-Sentry behavior in `runNotificationReschedule` unchanged.

Files to change:
  - lib/notifications/scheduler.ts:22-30
      Add module-level `let scheduleQueue: Promise<void> = Promise.resolve()` above `scheduleBatch`; wrap the existing cancelAll + schedule-loop body so it chains onto `scheduleQueue`, reassign `scheduleQueue = run.catch(() => {})`, and return the un-swallowed `run`. Add the required one-line comment noting the serialization guarantee.
  - lib/notifications/__tests__/scheduler.test.ts
      Add a concurrency test in the `scheduleBatch` describe block proving batches do not interleave (see tests).

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    `scheduleBatch` is called by `runNotificationReschedule` (same file) and directly by the test suite. `runNotificationReschedule` is called from three production sites: useNotificationScheduler.ts data-change effect (debounced) and foreground effect (un-debounced), and settingsScreens/notifications.tsx pref-save handler — plus components/devTest/NotificationsTest.tsx (dev harness). All benefit from the shared serialization with no caller-side changes. Return type stays `Promise<void>`, so `void`-invoked and `await`-ing callers are unaffected.
  Edge cases: Concurrent reschedule (the target bug): overlapping calls now queue, last wins. Empty batch: cancelAll still runs, still serialized. Batch error (e.g. cancelAll rejects): `run` rejects for the direct caller (runNotificationReschedule catches -> Sentry), while `scheduleQueue = run.catch(()=>{})` keeps the chain alive so a later batch is not blocked. Rapid foreground toggling: extra batches queue harmlessly. No offline/DST/date-key concerns — purely local-notification scheduling.
  Preserved:  Cancel-before-schedule ordering and empty-batch-still-cancels are unchanged (existing tests at scheduler.test.ts:31-46 still pass — a lone call sees `scheduleQueue` already resolved). Error-capture path (test at :56-60) preserved because `run` still rejects to the catch in `runNotificationReschedule`. Only new observable behavior: overlapping calls run sequentially instead of interleaving — strictly a correctness improvement.
  Tests:      Add to lib/notifications/__tests__/scheduler.test.ts a test 'serializes overlapping batches so cancel/schedule never interleave': make `mockCancel` return a manually-deferred promise for the first call; invoke `scheduleBatch([specA])` and `scheduleBatch([specB])` WITHOUT awaiting; resolve the deferred cancels; await both. Assert via jest `mock.invocationCallOrder` that batch A's `scheduleNotificationAsync` calls all precede batch B's second `cancelAllScheduledNotificationsAsync` (no second cancel while A's schedules are pending). Fails before the fix (interleaved order), passes after. Also run the existing suite: `npx jest lib/notifications/__tests__/scheduler.test.ts`.

L16  (Low)  [DRAFT]
===============================================================================
[Draft — not yet reviewed]

Root cause: ProgressWheel animates its center number by attaching an Animated.Value listener (numberValue.addListener at ProgressWheel.tsx:74) that calls setDisplayPercent(Math.round(value)) on every frame for the full 1000ms ANIMATION_DURATION — roughly 60 setState calls per calorie change. Because displayPercent lives on the top-level component, each setState re-renders the entire SVG subtree (Defs, LinearGradient, both Circles, marker), not just the text. Compounding it: both real callers (DailyIntakeCard.tsx:52 production, DualModeScene.tsx:93 devTest) pass children, which override the displayPercent text at line 134 — so in production the animated number is never even rendered, making the ~60/s re-render storm pure waste on every calorie/macro change. The cost is day-one (fires per interaction and per mount) but low-severity and does not grow with account age.

Fix: Two-part minimal change, no new dependencies. (1) Confine the per-frame setState to a leaf: extract the count-up label into a small React.memo child component (e.g. AnimatedPercentLabel) that owns its own displayPercent useState, its own numberValue Animated.Value, and the addListener/timing/cleanup currently in the parent effect. It renders only the percent Text node. Now the ~60 setStates/sec re-render just that Text instead of the whole SVG/gradient tree — the standard state-colocation fix, mirroring the memoization discipline used in the correctly-memoized contexts. (2) Only mount that label when children is absent: at line 134 render children when present, otherwise the AnimatedPercentLabel receiving target=actualPercent, fontSize, and color=colors.text. When a caller passes children (every current production usage), the number animation/listener never runs at all — eliminating the wasted setState storm with zero visible change, since children already override that text. Remove numberValue, displayPercent, and the number half of the useEffect from the parent; keep the arc animatedValue timing untouched (it is native-driven and never setStates). Do NOT touch the arc/marker/gradient logic or ANIMATION_DURATION — visible motion is preserved.

[Draft — not yet reviewed]

Root cause: In lib/foodDB/foodDB.ts the two module-level caches (searchCache keyed by normalized query, detailsCache keyed by foodId) are plain objects that only ever grow. Writes at :54 and :71 add keys unconditionally; the 7-day TTL in isFresh (:18-20) is a read-time freshness check that never evicts, so expired entries linger. The only reclamation is clearFoodDBCaches() at sign-out. Result: unbounded in-memory growth proportional to distinct searches/details opened per session. The companion note — food results rendered via .map() in a ScrollView — is bounded to ~50 by the API page size and is not a real cost.

Fix: Add a bounded-cache cap and eviction to lib/foodDB/foodDB.ts, and a guard test. Leave the modal render path as-is.

Files to change:
  - lib/foodDB/foodDB.ts
      Add MAX_CACHE_ENTRIES const near CACHE_TTL_MS (:5-9); add a setCapped<T>(cache, key, entry) helper that writes then evicts stale-first, oldest-FIFO when over cap; replace the direct write at :54 (searchCache) and :71 (detailsCache) with setCapped(...). No signature changes to any exported function.
  - lib/foodDB/__tests__/foodDB.test.ts
      Add cases: (a) after MAX_CACHE_ENTRIES+N distinct searches, Object.keys(searchCache) length stays <= MAX_CACHE_ENTRIES (assert via observable refetch of the earliest-evicted query, mirroring the existing clearFoodDBCaches refetch test pattern at :1214-1246 rather than reaching into module internals); (b) the entry written on the current call is never the one evicted; (c) existing clearFoodDBCaches test still passes.

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    Only two call sites feed the caches: getFoodSearchResults (invoked by the debounced search effect in foodDBModal.tsx:80) and getFoodDetails via getFoodItem (invoked by handleAddResult in foodDBModal.tsx:109). No other importers write to these caches. clearFoodDBCaches (AuthContext accountFunctions.tsx:42,93 on sign-out) is unaffected — it still wipes everything. Exported function signatures and return types are unchanged, so all consumers see identical behavior.
  Edge cases: Empty/whitespace query returns early before any cache write (:47) — unaffected. Expired-but-resident entries are already excluded by isFresh on read, so evicting them is purely memory reclamation with zero behavior change. The just-written key is guaranteed to survive eviction (evict-oldest never targets the freshly inserted last key; stale-first pass skips it because it is fresh). Single JS thread means no concurrent-write race. Offline/first-run: eviction only ever forces a later refetch, which already has a network path and error handling (:56-58, :74-76).
  Preserved:  The fix changes only *retention*, never returned values: a cache miss caused by eviction re-invokes the same edge function and returns identical data, exactly as a natural TTL miss does today. No rendered value in foodDBModal changes — FoodRow inputs (description, brandName, parsed preview) are derived from the same fetched objects. Because the cap (200) far exceeds a realistic single-session working set, steady-state hit rate is effectively unchanged; only pathological long sessions trigger eviction.
  Tests:      Run npx jest lib/foodDB/__tests__/foodDB.test.ts. Existing 'clearFoodDBCaches forces a refetch' test (:1214) must still pass. Add the bounded-size guard test (earliest query refetches after cap exceeded) and the never-evict-current-write test described above.

Root cause: WorkoutContext holds the static 1,317-entry exercise library in React state (`useState(exerciseLib)`, index.tsx:41) and re-derives it via an effect that spreads the whole library into a fresh object on every `userExercises` change (`setFullExerciseLib({ ...exerciseLib, ...userExercises })`, index.tsx:409-411). Using state+effect instead of a derived `useMemo` forces a second render pass on each custom-exercise create/delete, and the new `fullExerciseLib` reference invalidates the `fullExerciseLibAsList` memo (index.tsx:414-416), which maps over all 1,317 entries to rebuild the list — even when the user has zero custom exercises, so the merge and rebuild are pure churn. Feeding off that same state are two context handlers, `handleCalculateFatiguePercentage` and `handleGetFatigueSummary` (index.tsx:370-380), which have zero production callers (fatigue is de-emphasized in the UI); they sit in the provider value and recreate on every `logs`/`exercises` change, enlarging the value surface for no benefit. Degradation profile: fixed day-one cost proportional to library size (does NOT grow with account age or history), triggered only on the rare custom-exercise mutation — hence Low/fast-follow, not a launch gate.

Fix: Two independent small edits in context/WorkoutContext/index.tsx (see rootCause/fix detail).

Lines 409-411: replace the setFullExerciseLib effect with a useMemo deriving fullExerciseLib (return shared exerciseLib when userExercises empty). Lines 414-416: fullExerciseLibAsList memo returns precomputed exerciseLibAsList when userExercises empty. Lines 505-507: remove the three fatigue handlers from the provider value object.

      Lines 87-89: remove handleCalculateFatiguePercentage, handleGetFatigueSummary, and getFatigueFeedback from WorkoutContextInterface.

Blast radius & safety:

All are pure reads, so both switching to useMemo and returning the shared reference are transparent to them. The three removed handlers have NO production callers — grep across app/ and components/ finds only their definitions, the value object, and the type interface (fatigue is de-emphasized per project memory). No screen imports them.
  Edge cases: Empty userExercises (the overwhelmingly common account state): the guarded branch returns the shared exerciseLib / exerciseLibAsList references — stable across renders, correct content. L30  (Low)  [DRAFT]
===============================================================================
[Draft — not yet reviewed]

Root cause: The Settings header derives its sync line purely from the live pending-upload count (`app/(tabs)/settings.tsx:40-66`): `null → "Checking sync…"`, `<=0 → "Everything is up to date!"`, `>0 → "Syncing N changes..."`. There is no notion of *time* or *progress*, so a wedged upload queue (e.g. `Connector.uploadData()` repeatedly throwing while the queue stays non-zero) renders "Syncing N changes..." forever as passive muted text (rendered at `settings.tsx:148`), with no escalation and no user action. SyncWatchdog does track health (`components/GuardComponents/SyncWatchdog.tsx`) and publishes it via `subscribeWatchdogStatus` (`lib/powersync/watchdogStatus.ts:37`), but the Settings screen never consumes it. Worse, the watchdog can miss a pure upload-only wedge entirely: if the socket is `connected` and `lastSyncedAt` is recent, `check()` reports `currently_healthy` (`SyncWatchdog.tsx:109-122`) even though uploads are not draining — so watchdog reason alone is not a sufficient signal.

Fix: Smallest correct change: give the existing poll a sense of *progress over time* and escalate the line when the queue stops draining.

1) Add a pure helper `deriveSyncStatus()` in a new `lib/powersync/syncStatus.ts` (keep the derivation testable and out of the component). Inputs: `pendingEstimate: number | null`, `stalledForMs: number`, `watchdogReason: WatchdogReason`. Output: `{ tone: 'muted' | 'warning'; message: string; canRetry: boolean }`. Rules: null → muted "Checking sync…"; `<=0` → muted "Everything is up to date!"; `>0` and NOT stalled/healthy → muted "Syncing N changes…" (unchanged); `>0` AND (`stalledForMs > STALL_MS` e.g. 60s, OR watchdogReason is `'error' | 'disconnected'`) → warning "Sync is stuck — tap to retry", `canRetry: true`.

2) In `settings.tsx`, track stall inside the *existing* 1s poll effect (`:42-60`): keep a `stalledSinceRef`/`lastCountRef`; when the estimate decreases or hits 0, reset it; when it stays `>0` and unchanged, `stalledForMs = now - stalledSince`. Subscribe to `subscribeWatchdogStatus` (reuse the existing store; unsubscribe on cleanup) to capture `watchdogReason` for the connection-level case. Feed both into `deriveSyncStatus`.

3) Extract the line into a small presentational component `components/NeutralComponents/SyncStatusLine.tsx` taking `{ tone, message, canRetry, onRetry }`. When `canRetry`, render it as a `TouchableOpacity` with `accessibilityRole="button"`, `accessibilityLabel="Retry sync"`, min 44pt touch target, warning styling from `colors.warning`; otherwise plain `Text` in `colors.labelMuted` (current look preserved exactly). Extraction is required because the wedge state cannot be reproduced in the real screen for Dev Hub, so it must be drivable by props.

4) `onRetry` calls `kickPowerSync('watchdog_stale_last_sync')` (reuse orchestrator; do NOT add a new reason). Handle every `PowerSyncKickOutcome`: `'throttled'` → briefly swap the message to "Retry on cooldown ({getKickThrottleRemainingMs()/1000}s)"; `'ok'` → let the next poll clear it; `'error'`/`'background'` → keep the warning line. Do NOT bypass the 10-min `MIN_KICK_GAP_MS` throttle (`orchestrator.ts:18`).

Deliberately NOT touched: watchdog cadence/thresholds, orchestrator throttle, the healthy/checking/up-to-date copy and muted styling.

Tokens: `colors.warning` (defined in BOTH schemes — `colors.ts:41` light `#9C5D00`, `:86` dark `#FFB020`) and `fonts`; no hardcoded values. Verified warning color exists and reads in both schemes.

devHubPlan:
- Component(s) under test: `components/NeutralComponents/SyncStatusLine.tsx` (the new presentational line + retry affordance).
- Test page: `NEW:` — add `components/devTest/SyncStatusTest.tsx`, route stub `app/devTest/syncStatus.tsx` (guarded `require`), a Stack entry in `app/devTest/_layout.tsx`, and a `GROUPS` entry under "Components" in `components/devTest/DevHub.tsx` (`{ label: 'Sync Status Line (L30)', route: '/devTest/syncStatus' }`).
- Scenarios (DevControls toggles + the light/dark switch, both required): (a) light + "Checking"; (b) dark + "Checking"; (c) light + "Syncing 3 changes" (not stalled); (d) light + STALLED/warning (canRetry) at default Dynamic Type; (e) dark + STALLED/warning; (f) STALLED/warning at the largest Dynamic Type; (g) STALLED/warning with VoiceOver focus to confirm label/role order; (h) tap retry with a simulated `'throttled'` outcome.
- Expected result: a-c match today's muted line exactly in both schemes; d-e show `colors.warning` text as a tappable ≥44pt target reading "Sync is stuck — tap to retry"; f wraps without clipping (no fixed height); g announces "Retry sync, button"; h swaps to the cooldown copy without firing a second kick.
- devHubApproval: pending.

Files to change:
  - app/(tabs)/settings.tsx
      In the existing poll effect (:42-60) track stall (lastCount/stalledSince refs) and subscribe to subscribeWatchdogStatus for watchdogReason; replace the syncStatusLine useMemo (:62-66) with a deriveSyncStatus() call; render the new SyncStatusLine component at :148 wiring onRetry→kickPowerSync; drop the now-unused inline lastUpdated Text if fully replaced (keep the muted style for the non-warning path).
  - lib/powersync/syncStatus.ts
      NEW pure helper deriveSyncStatus(pendingEstimate, stalledForMs, watchdogReason) returning { tone, message, canRetry } + a STALL_MS constant. No React, no side effects.
  - components/NeutralComponents/SyncStatusLine.tsx
      NEW presentational component: plain muted Text for tone 'muted'; TouchableOpacity (44pt target, accessibilityRole=button, accessibilityLabel, colors.warning) for tone 'warning' with canRetry. All tokens from ThemeContext.
  - components/devTest/SyncStatusTest.tsx

Blast radius & safety:
  Callers:    settings.tsx is the sole consumer of getPendingUploadEstimate/uploadQueueStats in the UI and the only renderer of the sync line — the change is contained to that screen plus two new isolated files. subscribeWatchdogStatus is a read-only observer (returns an unsubscribe fn, watchdogStatus.ts:37-41); adding a subscriber does not affect SyncWatchdog or other consumers. kickPowerSync is already the sanctioned recovery entry point used by the watchdog; a manual caller reuses the same serialized, throttled path (orchestrator.ts:113-141) with no new reason added.
  Edge cases: Pure upload-wedge where watchdog still reports 'currently_healthy' — covered by the time-based stall detector, not just watchdogReason. Offline/background: kickPowerSync returns 'background' when AppState!=='active' (orchestrator.ts:114), leaving the warning line intact (no false 'fixed'). Throttle: within the 10-min MIN_KICK_GAP_MS, retry returns 'throttled' → cooldown copy, never a double kick. Rapid drain: stalledSince resets whenever the count drops or reaches 0, so brief large syncs never falsely escalate. Poll error path (getUploadQueueStats throws) still sets estimate=null → 'Checking sync…', unchanged. Largest Dynamic Type: no fixed height on the line, so it wraps.
  Preserved:  The three existing states (checking / up-to-date / syncing N) keep identical copy and muted styling; the warning branch is purely additive and only triggers on a genuine stall (>STALL_MS) or a watchdog error/disconnected reason. No change to watchdog cadence, orchestrator throttle, or sign-out flush gating. Extraction into SyncStatusLine is a lift-and-shift of the same JSX for the muted case.

L31  (Low)  [DRAFT]
===============================================================================
[Draft — not yet reviewed]

Root cause: In `app/onboardingScreens/paywall.tsx`, `finishOnboarding` (lines 59-66) awaits `completeOnboarding()` and, on a `false` (write failure), shows a dead-end Alert ("Couldn't finish setup") with a single OK button and returns — leaving the user parked on the paywall with no explicit retry. This edge is reached AFTER a successful purchase: `handleSubscribe` calls `purchasePackage` then `finishOnboarding` (lines 73-76). Once the purchase lands, RevenueCat flips `customerInfo` so `hasPremium` becomes true (`BillingContext/index.tsx:133`), which disables the primary CTA and relabels it "Subscription Active" (paywall.tsx:157,161). So the CTA can no longer act as the retry. The only enabled controls that still re-invoke `finishOnboarding` are "Maybe later" (line 192) and "Restore Purchases" (lines 88-93). A user who has already PAID is thus told their retry action is "Maybe later" — copy that reads as "decline / continue free," the opposite of what they need. The retry path exists but is mislabeled and undiscoverable.

Fix: Make the retry explicit and correctly labeled at the point of failure instead of relying on a mislabeled footer button. In `finishOnboarding`, replace the single-button Alert with the repo's standard buttons-array Alert (same shape used in `app/settingsScreens/profile.tsx:79-98`): a cancel button ("Not now") plus a primary "Try Again" button whose `onPress` re-invokes `finishOnboarding` (retries `completeOnboarding()` → `router.replace('/(tabs)')` on success). Keep the copy honest for the paid case — e.g. title "Couldn't finish setup", message noting the purchase went through and only the profile save failed, so the user knows their payment is safe and they are just retrying the save. This is the smallest correct change: it does NOT touch `handleSubscribe`/`handleRestore`/purchase logic, and it does NOT relabel the "Maybe later" button (that label is still correct for the genuine free-decline path where no purchase occurred). Do not add new theme surfaces — this is a native OS Alert; no colors/fonts/radii involved.
